S0005 To: This consultation is aimed at current and former members of the judiciary, salaried and fee-paid who are entitled to be a member of a judicial pension scheme, legal professionals, pension industry professionals, as well as anyone with an interest in public service pensions in England and Wales, Scotland and Northern Ireland.

S0013 Foreword 3 Introduction 4 Scope of Consultation 7
S0014 The proposals 9 Annexes 33 Questionnaire 117 About you 118 Contact details/How to respond 119 Consultation principles 121
S0015 Implementation of the new Fee-Paid Judicial Pension Scheme (FPJPS) is the way in which the government intends to meet the requirement to provide eligible fee-paid judicial office holders with pension benefits.
S0016 The FPJPS will provide a remedy to those eligible office holders who, as a consequence of court and tribunal rulings on this issue, are entitled to the equivalent of the pension benefits provided to their salaried counterparts.
S0017 This consultation seeks views on our proposals for the FPJPS, including detailed draft regulations, as well as related changes to the Judicial Pensions Scheme Regulations 2015.
S0018 The aim of the proposals is to provide both the appropriate retrospective pension benefits for eligible fee-paid judicial office holders – mirroring, as far as possible the Judicial Pensions and Retirement Act 1993 scheme (JUPRA) – and the right future pension benefits for serving fee-paid judges.
S0019 Due to the nature of fee-paid service, the regulations have proven to be complex to develop and some provisions necessarily differ in practice from the scheme that applies for salaried judges.
S0020 An explanation is provided for any differences.
S0021 The intention is that the FPJPS terms will be no less favourable than for salaried judges and will comply with the decisions of the UK courts and tribunals.
S0022 At the same time we are also consulting on consequential amendments to the Judicial Pensions Regulations 2015 which take account of the creation of the FPJPS and ensure parity of treatment under those regulations between those with entitlement in existing pension schemes such as JUPRA and those with entitlements under the FPJPS.
S0023 Following consideration of responses, the Ministry of Justice intends to issue a formal consultation response in the New Year and seek Parliament’s approval of the final regulations.
S0024 Our aim is for the FPJPS to come into force by 1st April 2017.
S0025 The Right Honourable Elizabeth Truss MP Lord Chancellor and Secretary of State for Justice
S0026 Background 1.
S0027 The UK courts and tribunals considered the terms and conditions of current and former fee-paid judicial office holders in a number of cases, including the case of O’Brien and Miller.
S0028 They found that a historic lack of pension and other specified benefits amounted to less favourable treatment of some fee-paid judicial office holders in comparison to salaried judges doing the same or broadly similar work.
S0029 This less favourable treatment was on grounds of their part-time status.
S0030 In particular, they found that eligible fee-paid judicial office holders were entitled to benefits that were no less favourable than those provided at the time to salaried judges by the scheme established under the Judicial Pensions and Retirement Act 1993 (JUPRA).
S0031 2.
S0032 This led to a commitment by the Ministry of Justice to implement a retrospective remedy – a Fee-Paid Judicial Pension Scheme – in respect of reckonable fee-paid service from 7 April 2000.
S0033 This is the date when the Part-Time Workers Directive, under which less favourable treatment should have been eliminated, came into force.
S0034 3. Over the same period, in line with wider public service pension reform, a new judicial pension scheme was established on 1 April 2015.
S0035 The Judicial Pension Scheme 2015 (JPS 2015) is for serving salaried and fee-paid office holders who were first appointed to judicial office on or after 1 April 2015 and for judicial office holders who were appointed prior to 1 April 2015 and do not have transitional protection allowing them to remain in their existing scheme.
S0036 4.
S0037 The FPJPS therefore needs to provide pension benefits for eligible current and former judicial office holders in respect of their service from 7 April 2000 (the majority of those judicial office holders who had not yet retired became members of the JPS 2015).
S0038 It also needs to provide pension benefits for eligible current and former fee- paid judicial office holders in respect of their service from 1 April 2015 if they are entitled to transitional protection.
S0039 5.
S0040 Litigation is ongoing about pension entitlement in respect of fee-paid service prior to 7 April 2000.
S0041 The MoJ does not want, however, to delay commencement of the FPJPS until that is resolved.
S0042 If the legal position changes, the MoJ will make provision to comply with its obligations.
S0043 6.
S0044 Litigation is also ongoing in respect of fee-paid judicial office holders who the MoJ believes are not entitled to pension benefits equivalent to the JUPRA scheme.
S0045 The MoJ’s view is that they are entitled to another form of pension benefit.
S0046 For example, the MoJ considers that some office holders are entitled to pension benefits which are no less favourable than those provided to salaried office holders which were “by analogy” to membership of the Principal Civil Service Pension Scheme.
S0047 The MoJ does not want to delay commencement of the FPJPS until this litigation is resolved.
S0048 Individuals eligible for a remedy other than the FPJPS will be provided for separately; their position is not within scope of this consultation.
S0049 7.
S0050 It is the MoJ’s position that fee-paid judges (and where applicable, non-legally qualified chairs) who sat in the following tribunals: Her Majesty’s Land Registry (HMLR), Asylum Support Tribunal (AST), Residential Property Tribunal Service (RPTS) and Criminal Injuries Compensation (CIC) should receive pensions under FPJPS which mirror the arrangements in place for salaried office holders in their respective jurisdictions at the relevant time.
S0051 This includes periods where salaried office holders received pension arrangements other than through JUPRA.
S0052 8.
S0053 The MoJ has set out detailed proposals to the Employment Tribunal in respect of each of the above Tribunals setting out what retrospective arrangements should apply.
S0054 The Tribunal has circulated these proposals to all affected claimants.
S0055 The matter was stayed pending the outcome of a related appeal at the Employment Appeal Tribunal which has recently been determined in the MoJ’s favour.
S0056 The Employment Tribunal is likely to reinstate directions which require affected claimants to state whether they agree with the MoJ’s proposed methodology.
S0057 If not, the Tribunal will determine this issue.
S0058 9.
S0059 The MoJ is currently considering how it will give effect to these proposals (e.g. whether it will be within FPJPS, for example by providing an appropriate amount of service credit for periods when salaried office holders would have received none JUPRA pension arrangements, or otherwise) and will provide a further update in due course.
S0060 10.
S0061 For the purposes of the draft regulations, Schedule 1 sets out where an affected office will only receive reckonable service under FPJPS after a set date.
S0062 This is because prior to this date, salaried office holders in the relevant jurisdictions did not have access to a JUPRA pension.
S0063 These dates are as follows:
S0064 • Eligible roles within the First-tier Tribunal Property Chamber (Residential Property) – service after 1 July 2013 will be reckonable under FPJPS.
S0065 • Deputy Adjudicator (fee-paid) to HMLR – service after 1 January 2009 will be reckonable under FPJPS.
S0066 • Eligible roles within the Asylum Support Tribunal – service after 1 April 2007 will be reckonable under FPJPS.
S0067 • Eligible roles within the Criminal Injuries Compensation Appeal Panel –service after 3 November 2008 will be reckonable under FPJPS.
S0068 Previous consultation 11.
S0069 The draft regulations are informed by the MoJ’s previous consultation on the proposed design of the Fee-Paid Judicial Pension Scheme which ran in autumn 2014.
S0070 There were 51 responses to this consultation from 39 members of the judiciary, eight judicial and legal associations and four other respondents.
S0071 12.
S0072 The majority of responses (51%) were from active or retired fee-paid judicial office holders and covered themes such as: eligibility; legislative approach; transitional protection; calculating pension; time limit for reckonable service; historical contributions; medical retirement; judicial added years scheme; and the equalities impact.
S0073 The subsequent response issued by MoJ on 23 March 2015, set out the Government’s position in relation to the comments received.
S0074 Remedy payments 13.
S0075 Whilst the scheme is being developed, provision has been made for interim payments to eligible retired fee-paid judicial office holders in respect of pension benefits that they would have received, or will receive, from their retirement up to any point before 31 March 2017.
S0076 This is to mitigate any financial detriment in relation to pension and lump sum payments pending the commencement of the scheme.
S0077 Any additional sums due in respect of this period will be paid under the scheme once it has commenced.
S0078 Purpose of the FPJPS 14.
S0079 The FPJPS will provide pension benefits to current and former fee-paid judicial office holders who have succeeded – or who have succeeded with – with pension benefit claims under the Part-time Workers (Prevention of Less Favourable Treatment) Regulations 2000.
S0080 The FPJPS seeks to provide benefits that are no less favourable than those provided to salaried judges under JUPRA.
S0081 As far as possible, the regulations therefore mirror JUPRA.
S0082 Where is this not possible, due to the particular characteristics of fee-paid working, our intention is to provide benefits which are no less favourable than the JUPRA 15.
S0083 In order to mirror, as far as possible, the JUPRA scheme on a pro-rata basis, the MoJ has used the Pension Schemes Act 2015 to insert a new Part 1A in JUPRA which confers a power on the Lord Chancellor to create a scheme for eligible members of the fee-paid judiciary.
S0084 16.
S0085 The draft regulations, provided for consultation with this paper, will establish and set out the FPJPS which must be approved by Parliament in order to come into force.
S0086 17.
S0087 Subject to such approval, the Ministry of Justice aims to have the FPJPS in force from 1 April 2017.
S0088 18.
S0089 The consultation is focused on the draft regulations to create the FPJPS, which have been designed to represent the policy that was set out in detail in the previous consultation and the government’s response, mirroring as far as possible the 1993 JUPRA scheme reflecting the outcome of the O’Brien and Miller litigation.
S0090 19.
S0091 The draft regulations required to create the FPJPS will give effect to commitments made by the MoJ in response to the O’Brien and Miller judgements.
S0092 The consultation is aimed at both former and serving members of the judiciary in the United Kingdom.
S0093 About this document 20.
S0094 This consultation also sets out eligibility for the FPJPS, the features of the scheme, options for members and information about leaving the scheme, and how and where the draft regulations achieve this.
S0095 21.
S0096 Annexed to this consultation are both the draft FPJPS regulations, and also the draft consequential amendment regulations.
S0097 Next Steps 22.
S0098 Following consultation, the MoJ will issue a formal response and finalise the draft regulations before they are laid in Parliament for approval.
S0099 23.

S0112 Eligibility for membership 25.
S0113 Membership of the FPJPS will be open to current and former fee-paid judicial office holders in England, Wales, Scotland and Northern Ireland who have been determined as eligible for pension rights that are no less favourable than those provided to salaried judges under JUPRA.
S0114 This is either as a result of decisions of the Employment Tribunal (and subsequent appeals) or by consent.
S0115 26.
S0116 The FPJPS is for eligible fee-paid judicial office holders with reckonable service from 7 April 2000 (this date is currently subject to ongoing litigation) up to 31 March 2015.
S0117 Transitional or tapering arrangements may apply in respect of service after 31 March 2015, where the fee-paid judicial office holder meets the relevant criteria for transitional protection in respect of their transfer to the JPS 2015.
S0118 27.
S0119 Like JUPRA, the FPJPS is closed to new members.
S0120 The MoJ has agreed (in a document described as the pensions’ moratorium) that fee-paid judges who held an eligible office on or after 2 December 2012 (and prior to 1 April 2015) will be eligible to be a member of FPJPS in respect of service in that particular office.
S0121 Fee-paid judges who held an eligible office which terminated (or they died) prior to 2 December 2012 will only be eligible to be a FPJPS member in respect of their service in that fee- paid office if they issued a part-time worker pension claim (or one was issued on their behalf) and:
S0122 (i) it was presented in time;
S0123 (ii) the MoJ agrees that it is just and equitable to extend time; or (iii) the Employment Tribunal orders that it is just and equitable to extend time Summary of eligibility for FPJPS 28.
S0124 The Supreme Court decision in O’Brien v MoJ established that recorders were entitled to pension benefits that were no less favourable than those provided to their full-time comparable worker (full-time Circuit Judges) under the JUPRA scheme.
S0125 Following the Supreme Court’s decision in O’Brien v MoJ (February 2013)
S0126 the entitlement of other types of fee-paid judicial office holder to pro-rata pension rights has been established (with a small number of issues yet to be determined) through a combination of:
S0127 • various decisions by the Employment Tribunal (and subsequent appeals) where the existence of a comparable full-time worker for the purpose of the regulations has been a matter of dispute;
S0128 • publication of lists of fee-paid offices which the MoJ accepts have a comparator for the purposes of establishing eligibility for membership of FPJPS or other pension benefits;
S0129 • various decisions by the Employment Tribunal about the particular pension remedy to which eligible fee-paid judicial office holders in particular jurisdictions are entitled – either pension benefits that are no less favourable than those provided under the JUPRA scheme (which the MoJ will provide through the FPJPS) or other pension benefits (which the MoJ will provide separately, outside the scope of this consultation).
S0130 Summary of eligibility for membership for the FPJPS Transitional provisions 29.
S0131 From 1 April 2015, reckonable fee-paid service will be pensioned under the JPS 2015, except where transitional provisions apply.
S0132 As part of the reforms of public service pensions, scheme members who were holding office on 1 April 2012 and who were within 10 years or less of scheme retirement age (65) are fully protected and allowed to remain in their current pension arrangements.
S0133 More limited tapering protection is available to those members who were within 13.5 years of scheme retirement age at 1 April 2012.
S0134 These arrangements will apply to the fee-paid scheme.
S0135 30.
S0136 For service beyond 1 April 2015, the pension scheme under which a fee-paid judicial office holder will accrue benefits will depend upon their eligibility for transitional protection.
S0137 The table below shows how the transitional arrangements apply.
S0138 Scenario: Eligibility.
S0139 In eligible fee-paid office as at 1 April 2015: Eligible in respect of service in that fee-paid office.
S0140 Their appointment to an eligible fee-paid office terminated on or after 2 December 2012 (by reason of retirement or otherwise): Eligible as covered by the moratorium1.
S0141 Their appointment to an eligible fee-paid office terminated before 2 December 2012 (by reason of retirement or otherwise) and made a part time worker claim to the Employment Tribunal for a pension in respect of service in that particular office within 3 months from the date of termination of the appointment to that particular office (or they were provided with an extension to this deadline by the Employment Tribunal or with the MoJ’s consent).:
S0142 Eligible.
S0143 Time period: Type of Judicial office holder; Pension Scheme.
S0144 7 April 2000 – 31 March 2015: All judicial office holders; FPJPS.
S0145 1 April 2015 – onwards: judicial office holders with transitional protection; FPJPS.
S0146 1 April 2015 – onwards: judicial office holders with tapering protection; FPJPS (for a period) or JPS 2015.
S0147 1 April 2015 – onwards: judicial office holders without transitional protection; JPS 2015.
S0148 Question 1: Do you agree that the FPJPS regulations as drafted provide a pension scheme for eligible fee-paid judges that is no less favourable than the pension provision for salaried judges under the JUPRA scheme?
S0149 Question 2: If you do not agree please explain why and specifically in relation to which part of the regulations?
S0150 Key Features of the Fee-Paid Judicial Pension Scheme 31.
S0151 The key feature of the FPJPS is that it is designed to mirror as far as possible the judicial pension scheme for salaried judicial office holders established by JUPRA.
S0152 To help with cross referencing between the two schemes, we have provided a reference to the relevant provision in JUPRA and the draft FPJPS regulations.
S0153 Main Scheme Features – Table 1 32.
S0154 Table 1 below reflects the main scheme features, highlighting any difference with JUPRA:
S0155 Scheme feature [FPJPS Regulations]: FPJPS; JUPRA
S0156 [JUPRA Reference].
S0157 a. Tax status of the scheme: Not a Registered Scheme for tax purposes; Same as FPJPS.
S0158 b. Pension benefits: annual pension and pension lump sum
S0159 [regulations 5, 13, 25]: Accrual rate of 1/40th for every year and part years’ worth of reckonable service that a judicial office holder has completed prior to retirement Benefits are calculated using an appropriate annual salary which is defined as the highest daily fee in the final 3 years before retirement for the relevant office, multiplied by the divisor / multiplicand for that office.
S0160 The daily fee is the higher of • the rate of the daily fee received in that office or • the daily fee that has otherwise been determined by an Employment Tribunal Pension lump sum is calculated by multiplying the annual pension by 2.25.;
S0161 Same as FPJPS – accrual rate of 1/40th for every full time year and part years’ worth of service that a judicial office holder has completed prior to retirement Pensionable pay is the highest 12 consecutive months of salary received before retirement
S0162 [sections 3, 4].
S0163 c. Maximum accruable service
S0164 [regulation 5]: The maximum amount of reckonable service that can be used in calculating benefits is 20 years’ worth of service (which can be accrued by a fee-paid judicial office holder over a period of more than 20 calendar years of appointment);
S0165 Same as FPJPS – the maximum amount of service that can be used in calculating benefits is 20 full time years of service [section 3].

S0167 [JUPRA Reference].

S0170 Under age 60 benefits are preserved.;
S0171 Same as FPJPS [section 2].

S0173 [regulations 36, 37]: Pension payable to a surviving spouse or civil partner upon death during service.
S0174 For death in service the rate of pension at one-half of the pension to which the judicial office holder would have been entitled if they retired on grounds of ill health at the time of death.
S0175 For death in retirement, a pension paid at a rate of one-half of the judicial pension actually in payment at the time of death.;

S0190 [section 4].

S0195 [Part 4]: Does not offer partial retirement for an individual office.
S0196 Where more than one office is held consecutively it is possible to take partial retirement.; No partial retirement.
S0197 l. Medical retirement

S0253 Information on Scheme Features – Table 1 33.
S0254 A summary of these scheme features of the FPJPS scheme is provided below.
S0255 The numbering reference in each section below cross refers to the tables 1, 2, or 3 above.
S0256 a. Tax status 34.
S0257 The FPJPS will be a non-registered scheme for tax purposes.
S0258 This means that benefits accrued in the scheme do not count towards either the Annual Allowance or the Lifetime Allowance under the Finance Act 2004.
S0259 Tax relief is not available on contributions.
S0260 35.
S0261 However, the Judicial Additional Voluntary Contributions Scheme (JAVCS), Judicial Added Years (JAYS) and Judicial Added Surviving Adults Pension Scheme (JASAPS) are all registered schemes for tax purposes and therefore contributions do attract tax relief.
S0262 The Annual and Lifetime Allowances does apply for these schemes.
S0263 36.
S0264 As the FPJPS will be non-registered the pension lump sum will be taxed at the highest rate of personal taxation as it is treated as earned income and taxed accordingly.
S0265 37.
S0266 As with members of JUPRA, members of the FPJPS are entitled to an automatic pension lump sum payable on retirement, which will be taxed at 45%.
S0267 They will, however, receive a lump sum equivalent to the tax amount.
S0268 This means in practice that FPJPS members will receive the same amount as if the lump sum had been tax free.
S0269 38.
S0270 See FPJPS Part 3, paragraph 25 and JUPRA section 3.
S0271 b. Pension benefits 39.
S0272 Pension benefits in JUPRA and in the FPJPS consist of two components – an annual pension and an automatic pension lump sum.
S0273 In addition a service award is payable which compensates for the tax deducted from the pension lump sum.
S0274 40.
S0275 Regulations 1 to 7 define the main concepts used in these Regulations.
S0276 In particular, “reckonable service”, which will determine the level of pension to which a fee-paid judge is entitled, is defined as the total number of qualifying fee-paid days (part or full) in which a fee has been paid divided by the appropriate annual divisor.
S0277 This is subject to a maximum amount (provided for in regulation 5(3) to (9)) of 20 years, minus any other pensionable service to which the judge is entitled.
S0278 41.
S0279 In addition, regulation 13 sets out how the annual rate of pension is calculated.
S0280 Regulation 25 sets out the calculation of the pension lump sum entitlement.
S0281 42.
S0282 See also JUPRA sections 3 and 4.
S0283 Example: Total Pension Calculation Judicial office holder A was appointed as a Deputy District Judge on 5 April 2007 and retires on 31 March 2019.
S0284 This office holder has full transitional protection and therefore is eligible to remain in the FPJPS until retirement.
S0285 During that time Judge A sat 435 pensionable days (M).
S0286 The divisor for a DDJ is 215 days (N).
S0287 Reckonable Service [Regulation 5] Reckonable Service is used in relation to a fee-paid judicial office held by a member, expressed in terms of years and a fraction of a year in accordance with the following formula:

S0289 M = qualifying N = R fee-paid service in the relevant office = the applicable annual divisor for that office M N = qualifying fee-paid service in the relevant = the applicable annual divisor for that office M N

S0296 As with JUPRA, reckonable service for the FPJPS (regulation 5 (3)) is capped at 20 years full time equivalent worth of service.
S0297 A fee-paid judicial office holder is permitted to accrue the maximum of 20 years’ worth of service over a period of more than 20 calendar years.
S0298 The total accrued service to which the cap applies is expressed in years = total number of pensionable days served (over the entire period of appointment, which may be more than 20 calendar years) /
S0299 the applicable annual divisor for the office (as per the example above).
S0300 44.
S0301 If a salaried judicial office holder with a preserved pension under the FPJPS subsequently sat in retirement as a fee-paid judicial office holder, the preservation of their FPJPS pension would be suspended.
S0302 This judicial office holder would continue to accrue pension benefits under the FPJPS, in the same arrangement as their previous fee-paid service, subject to the operation of the 20-years’ worth of service cap on accruable service.
S0303 When the judicial office holder finally retired the benefits would be recalculated and paid.
S0304 45.
S0305 See the FPJPS regulation 5 and JUPRA section 3.
S0306 d. Pension Age: age at which pension is payable 46.
S0307 The pension age provisions in the FPJPS mirror JUPRA:
S0308 • An unreduced pension becomes payable at the age of 65 years providing the member has 5 years of qualifying judicial service at the date and is not linked to a member’s state pension age.
S0309 • A pension cannot be drawn before leaving all judicial offices and cannot be drawn before the age of 60 even if a member of the FPJPS has retired.
S0310 If a member retires before 60, their pension is preserved until the earlier of their retirement age (aged 65) and when they would have completed 5 years’ service.
S0311 •
S0312 It is possible to take early retirement and receive an actuarially reduced pension between the age of 60 and 65.
S0313 47.
S0314 See FPJPS regulations 12, 14 and 17 and JUPRA section 2.
S0315 e. Spouses’ and civil partners’ pensions 48.
S0316 A pension will be payable to a surviving spouse or civil partner upon the death of a judicial office holder either during service or in retirement.
S0317 49.
S0318 If there is a death in service, this pension will be paid at a rate of one-half of the pension to which the judicial office holder would have been entitled if they retired on grounds of ill health at the time of death.
S0319 50.
S0320 If a judicial office holder dies in retirement or holding preserved benefits, this pension will be paid at a rate of one-half of the judicial pension actually in payment at the time of death.
S0321 51.
S0322 See FPJPS regulations 36 and 37 and JUPRA section 5.
S0323 f. Children’s pensions 52.
S0324 A pension will also be payable in respect of a child or children under the age of 16, or over that age and in full time education, at the time of the judicial office holder’s death.
S0325 Children’s pension calculations 53.
S0326 If a member of the FPJPS has a surviving spouse or surviving civil partner, the pension is calculated as:
S0327 • ¼ of the judicial pension if there is one eligible child; or • ½ of the judicial pension if there is more than one eligible child.
S0328 54.
S0329 If there is no surviving spouse or surviving civil partner, the pension is calculated as:
S0330 • ⅓ of the judicial pension if there is one eligible child; or • ⅔ of the pension of if there is more than one eligible child.
S0331 • See FPJPS regulations 40, 41, 42, 43 and JUPRA sections 6, 7 and 8.
S0332 g. Member Contributions 55.
S0333 The FPJPS mirrors JUPRA.
S0334 For the period 7 April 2000 until 31 March 2015 contributions are required towards the cost of spouses’, civil partners’ and children’s pensions (Dependents’ Pension Contributions).
S0335 These contributions are compulsory for all members payable at a rate of 1.8% of the judicial office holder’s fee.
S0336 They are to be paid until retirement or 20 years’ reckonable service has been completed.
S0337 Although during the period up to 5April 2006 the actual rate of contributions in JUPRA was 3%, as tax relief was given the net contribution payable was 1.8%.
S0338 56.
S0339 Personal Contributions are payable by all JUPRA and FPJPS scheme members from 1 April 2012.
S0340 Schedule for recovery of historical contributions 1 April 2014 – 31 March 2015 3.2% 1.8% 5.00% 57.
S0341 Contributions from 1 April 2015 will mirror that of the JPS 2015 although the rates will reflect that no tax relief is available.
S0342 Historical member contributions 58.
S0343 Arrears of contributions due before the commencement of the scheme will be recovered.
S0344 This will be done by deducting the amount as a one off payment from the member’s automatic pension lump sum upon retirement.
S0345 The member can otherwise choose to pay the amount owing on commencement of the scheme or make additional contributions from each fee payable until the amount owing is repaid.
S0346 7 April 2000 – 31 March 2012: Personal Contributions: 0.00.%; Dependent Contributions: 1.8%; Total member contribution rate: 1.8%.
S0347 1 April 2012 – 31 March 2013: Personal Contributions: 1.28%; Dependent Contributions: 1.8%; Total member contribution rate: 3.08%.
S0348 1 April 2013 – 31 March 2014: Personal Contributions: 2.56%; Dependent Contributions: 1.8%; Total member contribution rate: 4.36%.
S0349 1 April 2014 – 31 March 2015: Personal Contributions: 3.2%; Dependent Contributions: 1.8%; Total member contribution rate: 5.00%.
S0350 59.
S0351 The amount of contribution due is the amount that would have been payable had the member been able to pay at the relevant time.
S0352 60.
S0353 See FPJPS parts 8 and 9, JUPRA section 9 and Judicial Pensions (Contributions) Regulations 1995.
S0354 h. Refunding of member contributions 61.
S0355 As with JUPRA, under the FPJPS dependent contributions will be refunded with compound interest as follows:
S0356 • A full refund will be made were a scheme member is unmarried and does not have a registered civil partner or children eligible to receive benefits at the time the member retires or dies or at any previous time whilst holding office.
S0357 • A partial refund will be made where, although the member was not married, in a civil partnership, or had an eligible child on their date of retirement, they did hold one of these conditions at a previous time whilst in office.
S0358 62.
S0359 See FPJPS Part 10, JUPRA section 9 and Judicial Pensions (Contributions) Regulations 1995.
S0360 i. Death in service 63.
S0361 Under the FPJPS, if an active member of the scheme dies whilst holding judicial office, a lump sum death benefit will be paid out.
S0362 It is equal to twice the amount of the pay of the office holder’s salaried comparator, multiplied by their qualifying fee-paid service (expressed in terms of years) and divided by the period during which they held fee-paid office.
S0363 It is payable either to the personal representative(s), or a nominated person.
S0364 It is necessary to calculate the lump sum by reference to the salary of a comparator office (“the appropriate annual salary”) rather than referring to pensionable pay as the working patterns of fee-paid judicial office holders can vary.
S0365 64.
S0366 For comparison, pensionable pay in JUPRA is the highest amount of salary paid to a scheme member in any period of 12 consecutive months in the three years immediately preceding the day on which that person ceases to hold office.
S0367 65.
S0368 Death in service benefits are payable in respect of each eligible office held at time of death.
S0369 66.
S0370 Following the death of a member, the lump sum will count as employment income for tax purposes.
S0371 The tax due must be deducted by the relevant Department, but a supplementary payment equal to the amount of tax deducted on it will be made to ensure that the beneficiary receives the full amount.
S0372 67.
S0373 The FPJPS will allow members to make a nomination for the death in service lump sum.
S0374 68.
S0375 See FPJPS regulation 45 and JUPRA section 4.
S0376 j. Death in retirement 69.
S0377 If a member of the FPJPS dies soon after retirement, when the judicial pension has only been in payment for a short time, the scheme member’s personal representatives may be entitled to an additional lump sum payment.
S0378 70.
S0379 The lump sum will be payable if the aggregate of the sums paid or payable fall short of an amount equal to five times the annual rate of pension a member is entitled to before their death.
S0380 This is calculated on the basis of the pension together with the lump of the pension to which the member is entitled immediately before the date of death.
S0381 71.
S0382 As any lump sum is payable to the personal representatives, any tax liability is chargeable to the relevant Department.
S0383 The liability will not be recovered any such liability paid from the personal representatives.
S0384 72.
S0385 See FPJPS regulation 46 & 47, JUPRA section 4 and Judicial Pensions (Preservation of Benefits) Order 1995.
S0386 k. Partial retirement 73.
S0387 Pensions are only payable on retirement from all judicial offices.
S0388 However under FPJPS where a fee-paid judicial office holder holds different offices consecutively it is possible to elect to take benefits when leaving the original office.
S0389 This does not apply where offices are held concurrently.
S0390 74.
S0391 See FPJPS Part 4.
S0392 l. Medical retirement 75.
S0393 A member of the FPJPS who retires at any time after appointment for reasons of ill health is entitled to the immediate payment of a pension and automatic lump sum based on his or her actual service.
S0394 It is determined by the calculation below.
S0395 76.
S0396 The FPJPS includes provision for a single level of ill health retirement benefits on the following basis:
S0397 • Ill health pensions are payable to an office holder who had “suffered a permanent breakdown in health” involving “incapacity for employment”.
S0398 •
S0399 If a member has not reached their 65th birthday before the date of retirement, the length of service upon which the pension is calculated will be enhanced.
S0400 Example Judicial office holder B was appointed as a Deputy District Judge on 5 April 2007 and retires on 31 March 2019, this office holder has full transitional protection and therefore is eligible to remain in the FPJPS until retirement.
S0401 During that time Judge A sat 435 pensionable days (M).
S0402 The divisor for a DDJ is 215 days (N)
S0403 If Judge B was aged 63 at the time of their ill health retirement the enhancement would cover the period from aged 63 to aged 65 divided by 2.

Multiple fee-paid offices 77.
S0407 The FPJPS makes provision for pension entitlement in relation to multiple fee-paid offices, to both consecutive and concurrent appointment to multiple fee-paid offices 78.
S0408 Where an office holder holds multiple fee-paid offices at the time of retirement, pension benefits for each office will be calculated separately.
S0409 They have to satisfy the qualifying service condition in respect of each office.
S0410 79.
S0411 If a judicial office holder has consecutive fee-paid and salaried service, and the final post held before retirement is a salaried post, there will be a link to that salary at retirement.
S0412 This means that all service will be based on the highest salary in the last three years before retirement from salaried office.
S0413 The final salary link will also apply to a fee-paid office holder who is subsequently appointed to a fee-paid office at a higher or lower rate of remuneration.
S0414 The appropriate annual salary used to determine the pension rate will be the highest salary paid to the holder of a comparable office in the last three years before retirement.
S0415 80.
S0416 In order to apply a final salary link where the consecutive offices are fee-paid, office holders will be given an option in respect of each office – to bank reckonable service so that it will qualify for the final salary link on retirement or receive the pension, depending on their age or preserve it.
S0417 Example: Judicial office holder (P) sits for 8 years as a Deputy District Judge and then for 7 years as a Recorder.
S0418 There is no overlap of service.
S0419 At 58, P resigns from being a Deputy District Judge and then he would choose whether to bank that reckonable service or have the pension for that office deferred.
S0420 P decides to retire at 65 after sitting for 7 years as a Recorder.
S0421 If he chose option a), his full 15 years reckonable service would be subject to the final salary link and so pension benefits would be calculated using the Recorded salary comparator.
S0422 If P chose option b), the two offices would be treated for pension purposes as separate entitles.
S0423 The 8 years’ service as a Deputy District Judge would have no link to the 7 years’ service as a Recorded, but would separately have increased in value by operation of the Pension Increase Act 1971.
S0424 n. Pension sharing on divorce or dissolution 81.
S0425 As is the same as under JUPRA, when a Pension Sharing Order is made, the former spouse or civil partner of the office holder becomes a member of the FPJPS in his or her own right.
S0426 The value of the member’s accrued judicial pension rights to be shared with them will be determined at the date of the Decree Absolute or Final Order.
S0427 A pension debit will be applied to the office holder’s pension benefits.
S0428 82.
S0429 See FPJPS Part 5 and JUPRA section 23A and Schedule 2A.
S0430 o. Revaluation of preserved and retirement benefits 83.
S0431 Preserved benefits and benefits in payment are revalued in line with the Pension (Increase) Act 1971.
S0432 An annual Pension Increase Order is issued which determines the rate of amendment for pension benefits.
S0433 p.
S0434 Contracting out of the State retirement benefits 84.
S0435 Like JUPRA, we intend that the FPJPS will be a contracted out pension scheme for the purposes of the Pension Schemes Act 1993 until 5 April 2016.
S0436 At that stage, contracting out ceased for all pension schemes.
S0437 An application for contracting out can be made once the Regulations have been made by Parliament.
S0438 Scheme members will therefore pay reduced National Insurance contributions and will not accrue additional state benefits beyond basic state pension entitlements.
S0439 The MoJ is in discussion with HMRC on the process to enable refunds of overpaid National Insurance contributions once the scheme commences.
S0440 q.
S0441 Opting in/out 85.
S0442 See table 2 above.
S0443 Also see FPJPS regulations 10 and 11 and JUPRA section 13.
S0444 r. Appeals 86.
S0445 See table 2 above.
S0446 Also see FPJPS Part 15 and JUPRA section 20.
S0447 s. Times when pension are payable 87.
S0448 See table 2 above.
S0449 Also see FPJPS regulation 24 and JUPRA section 2.
S0450 t. Salaried judicial office holder sitting in retirement 88.
S0451 A salaried judicial office holder may in some circumstances sit in retirement as a fee- paid holder.
S0452 In these circumstances, the individual may accrue further pension benefit in respect of their fee-paid service under the FPJPS, subject to the operation of applicable transitional provisions.
S0453 89.
S0454 An office holder will be able to accrue such benefit whilst in receipt of their JUPRA salaried pension.
S0455 Any pension accrued under the FPJPS will be payable on the point of leaving fee-paid office.
S0456 90.
S0457 If an office holder sitting in retirement has accrued the maximum of 20 years of salaried service under JUPRA, s/he will not be able to accrue further credit under the FPJPS.
S0458 The total service available under the FPJPS and JUPRA is 20 years.
S0459 91.
S0460 See FPJPS regulations 6 and 8.
S0461 Ancillary benefits that can be secured under FPJPS – Table 3 92.
S0462 Members of the FPJPS are able to supplement their pension as described below 93.
S0463 To aid understanding, the Government Actuary’s Department has provided examples at Annex II showing the process, costs and links to the relevant Regulations.
S0464 It should be noted the cost of ancillary benefits are calculated as cost neutral to the scheme at the time the calculation would have been completed.
S0465 u. Fee-Paid Judicial Added Years Scheme (if eligible)
S0466 94.
S0467 The Judicial Added Years Scheme (JAYS) allows judicial office holders to purchase added years (or part years) of reckonable service in judicial office.
S0468 The benefits from JAYS are paid as part of the judicial pension.
S0469 JAYS provides an additional lump sum and a pension for a surviving spouse/ civil partner and children.
S0470 95.
S0471 The scheme closed to new applicants on 6 April 2006 when the JUPRA became a non-registered scheme.
S0472 96.
S0473 For equality of treatment, the FPJPS will offer eligible fee-paid judicial office holders who were in service on or before 5 April 2006 the same opportunity.
S0474 They may only do this if they are still an active scheme member upon purchase or in the first year of the scheme’s operation for those that have already retired.
S0475 The following conditions also apply:
S0476 •
S0477 The maximum number of added years that a scheme member may purchase is determined by their assumed retirement age.
S0478 The maximum cannot exceed 20 years reckonable service at that assumed retirement age, which includes the value of retained benefits.
S0479 •
S0480 If a member has been contributing to any other pension scheme before or during holding eligible office they may be precluded from purchasing JAYS.
S0481 97.
S0482 See Annex II for examples for retired and active scheme member.
S0483 98.
S0484 Different provision is made for contributions to purchase JAYS between retired members and active members.
S0485 The maximum contribution a retired member may contribute is determined by the amount they could have contributed had the added years scheme been established before they were first in office.
S0486 This cannot be more than 15% of their annual fee income in each year up to their retirement day.
S0487 These contributions equal lump sum payments made in the first twelve months after the date on which the retired office holder has given notice that they intend to purchase added years.
S0488 99.
S0489 Active members may also make such lump sum payments i.e. amounts they could have contributed pre-commencement.
S0490 This is subject to the same maximum figure as applies to retired members.
S0491 These lump sum payments must be made in the three years after the regulations come into force, and no more than one lump sum payment may be made in a single year.
S0492 100.
S0493 In addition, active members may make periodic payments out of the fee income they earn after the regulations come into force.
S0494 As the sitting pattern of fee-paid judicial office holders may vary and they do not therefore receive a set income, the amount they contribute may also vary.
S0495 Accordingly the scheme actuary will be required, on their retirement, to calculate the number of added years to which they are entitled.
S0496 This is done by dividing the total contributions they have made by the cost of purchasing an additional year.
S0497 Regular contributions through payroll will attract tax relief automatically; for any member paying the lump sum by cheque they must notify HMRC to obtain appropriate tax relief.
S0498 101.
S0499 The MoJ provided an undertaking to the Employment Tribunal which confirmed the MoJ’s approach to claims that a fee-paid judge has been subjected to less favourable treatment as a result of the operation of JAYS.
S0500 This undertaking was recorded in the Employment Tribunals judgment dated 13 December 2015 and is available at Annex III to this consultation 102.
S0501 See FPJPS Part 13, JUPRA section 10 and Judicial Pensions (Additional Voluntary Contributions) Regulations 1995.
S0502 v. Judicial Added Surviving Adults Pension Scheme (if eligible)
S0503 103.
S0504 A member of the FPJPS is also able to purchase additional pension for their spouse or civil partner through the Judicial Added Surviving Adults Pension Scheme (JASAPS).
S0505 This can be done in units of £1,000.
S0506 The maximum amount of added units which may be purchased is the amount necessary to give the surviving partner a pension equal to 20/45ths of the appropriate annual salary applying to the judicial office held by the member on the date he or she retires.
S0507 104.
S0508 Purchasing JASAPS is subject to the same eligibility criteria as JAYS.
S0509 It has been closed to salaried judicial office holders since 6 April 2006 (when the Finance Act 2006 came into force).
S0510 105.
S0511 As with JAYS, retired members may make contributions to the scheme by lump sum payments made within 12 months of the date on which they have given notice of their intention to purchase added units of surviving partner pension (“added units”).
S0512 This is up to a maximum amount determined by what they could have contributed to the scheme if it had been established before 6 April 2006.
S0513 (Again, it cannot be more than 15% of their fee income in any single tax year).
S0514 106.
S0515 Active members may make the same lump sum payments.
S0516 In addition, they may make periodic payments out of their fees after the commencement of the regulations.
S0517 As with the added years scheme, the scheme actuary will determine how many added units of surviving partner pension the active member has purchased by dividing their total contributions by the cost of a single unit.
S0518 If the amount which has been contributed exceeds the cost of the units the member wishes to buy (or the maximum they were permitted to buy), the member is entitled to a refund.
S0519 107.
S0520 See Annex II for examples for a retired and active scheme member.
S0521 108.
S0522 The MoJ provided an undertaking to the Employment Tribunal which confirmed the MoJ’s approach to claims that a fee-paid judge has been subjected to less favourable treatment as a result of the operation of JASAPS.
S0523 This undertaking was recorded in the Employment Tribunals judgment dated 13 December 2015 and is available at Annex III to this consultation.
S0524 109.
S0525 See FPJPS Part 14, JUPRA section 10 and Judicial Pensions (Additional Voluntary Contributions) Regulations 1995.
S0526 w. Fee-Paid Judicial Added Voluntary Contributions Scheme 110.
S0527 The FPJPS includes arrangements for Judicial Added Voluntary Contributions Scheme (JAVCS).
S0528 This reflects the fact that members of JUPRA are able to top-up their benefits through JAVCS.
S0529 This is a registered scheme for tax purposes and contributions to this scheme will attract tax relief.
S0530 Only active members (that is members who still hold a fee paid judicial office) may be members of JAVCS 111.
S0531 Contributions made to the scheme will be invested with an authorised independent pension provider.
S0532 They will invest the member’s contributions as requested by the member according to a range of investment options.
S0533 The fund which is generated may be used to purchase pension benefits for the scheme member or their dependents.
S0534 112.
S0535 Members of JAVCS may make contributions by way of lump sum to cover contributions they could have made in the period up to the commencement of the regulations had the scheme been established on 7th April 2000.
S0536 Any contributions made after the commencement of the regulations must be taken from their fees.
S0537 113.
S0538 The MoJ provided an undertaking to the Employment Tribunal which confirmed the MoJ’s approach to claims that a fee-paid judge has been subjected to less favourable treatment as a result of the operation of JAVCS.
S0539 This undertaking was recorded in the Employment Tribunals judgment dated 13th December 2015 and is available at Annex III to this consultation 114.
S0540 See FPJPS Part 12, JUPRA section 10 and Judicial Pensions (Additional Voluntary Contributions) Regulations 1995.
S0541 x. Transfer of accrued pension rights 115.
S0542 Under the FPJPS regulations, members may elect to transfer non-judicial pension rights into the scheme or they may choose to transfer their judicial pension rights out of the scheme, subject to certain conditions.
S0543 Where a transfer out is paid, all rights to benefits under the FPJPS will be extinguished.
S0544 116.
S0545 Members of the scheme can only exercise rights to transfer accrued pension rights in or out of the scheme from the date which the FPJPS commences.
S0546 117.
S0547 It should also be noted that under JUPRA, members over the age of 64 years are ineligible to transfer accrued pension rights into or out of that scheme.
S0548 By the same principle only members of the FPJPS who were under the age of 64 at 6 February 2013 (the date of the UK Supreme Court judgment in O’Brien) can transfer accrued pension rights into or out of the FPJPS.
S0549 118.
S0550 The MoJ provided an undertaking to the Employment Tribunal which confirmed the MoJ’s approach to claims that a fee-paid judge has been subjected to less favourable treatment.
S0551 This undertaking was recorded in the Employment Tribunals judgment dated 13th December 2015 and is available at Annex III to this consultation.
S0552 119.
S0553 See FPJPS Part 11 and JUPRA Schedule 2.
S0554 Fee-paid members who have left service before scheme commencement Interim payments 120.
S0555 The MoJ and other departments have paid interim payments to office holders who have or will have retired before the commencement of the scheme.
S0556 In a few cases this includes to the estates of office holders who have died and / or to their dependant(s).
S0557 The level of these compensation payments is based upon the provisions of JUPRA, but no equivalent of a service award has been paid.
S0558 The amount of contributions owed, where payable, has been based upon the level of the fee that would have been payable at time they would have been due.
S0559 In addition, where an office holder left office after 1 April 2012
S0560 no deductions were made for the Personal Pension Contributions which would have been payable from that date.
S0561 121.
S0562 The regulations make provision for where a judicial office holder has received an interim payment before these regulations came into force, or where a judicial office holder would have received benefits under the pension scheme constituted by these regulations if the regulations were in force before that office holder retired or died.
S0563 The regulations require the Minister to calculate the difference between the amount a person actually received by way of interim payment before the scheme commenced and the amount that person would have received if the regulations had been in force (i.e. calculated net of any contributions which would have been paid).
S0564 122.
S0565 If additional payments are due to any person, they must be made.
S0566 If a person has been paid more by way of interim payments than they would have received under the regulations if they had been in force that person is liable to repay the difference to the Minister or other Government Department.
S0567 That repayment may be made by deduction from monthly pension payments.
S0568 123.
S0569 The Regulations do not provide for any payment of interest or compensation of an interest like nature for the late payment of pension benefits to judicial office holders who have already retired.
S0570 The Employment Tribunal has confirmed that compensation of an interest like nature is due on these sums.
S0571 Such payments will be calculated by the MoJ once the recalculation is complete and paid as a separate payment.
S0572 In its judgment of 13 December 2015 the Employment Tribunal confirmed that the Preston formula is the appropriate methodology when calculating interest on pension payments under the FPJPS.
S0573 For the avoidance of doubts, the interim payments also include an element of interest which was calculated in accordance with the Preston formula.
S0574 124.
S0575 See FPJPS Regulations 48 – 51.
S0576 Trivial Pensions 125.
S0577 There may be some pensions that derive from membership of the FPJPS that provide relatively low monthly pension payments.
S0578 Some pension schemes allow these trivial pensions to be converted into a single one off payment to save on administration time and processing and the member to receive a capital sum rather than a very small monthly payment.
S0579 The factors for calculating the value of a trivial pension would be provided by the scheme actuary.
S0580 126.
S0581 This facility is not provided for within JUPRA but it may be that fee-paid office holders may find this option beneficial.
S0582 127.
S0583 Therefore as part of this consultation, we are asking if such a facility would be welcome.
S0584 If so what value of pension (e.g. less than £1000 pa) would be thought to be trivial.
S0585 In addition we welcome views on whether it should be an automatic process or if it should always be a choice of the member.
S0586 Question 3: In relation to trivial pensions, should the regulations allow the facility to take a single one off payment rather than receive a monthly pension?
S0587 Question 4: In relation to trivial pensions, if you agree with having the facility for a one off payment, what should be the amount?
S0588 Question 5: In relation trivial pensions, should this be an automatic process or always be the choice of the individual member?
S0589 Consequential Amendments 128.
S0590 The regulations amending the Judicial Pension Regulations 2015 are at Annex IV.
S0591 129.
S0592 The purpose of the regulations is to ensure that, where relevant, pensions calculated under the 2015 regulations take account of a person’s service in, and entitlements under, the JUPRA scheme.
S0593 In order to ensure that the pensions payable in respect of scheme members who have already died or retired are calculated by reference to the entitlements which they would have had if the FPJPS had been in force regulation 14 of the amending regulations provides for such people’s pension entitlements to be calculated as though the FPJPS and the consequential amendments to the 2015 scheme were already in force.
S0594 130.
S0595 The amending regulations make the following changes to the 2015 scheme:
S0596 Expanding references to existing schemes 131.
S0597 The regulations expand references to “existing schemes” (listed in Schedule 5 to the Public Service Pensions Act 2013 and including the JUPRA scheme) to include the FPJPS.
S0598 This has been done by creating a new concept of “relevant scheme” which means (see new regulation 3) an existing scheme or the FPJPS.
S0599 Specifically the amendment ensures that:
S0600 • a person has opted not to remain in the FPJPS is treated in the same way as a person who has opted not to continue in an existing scheme in the same circumstances (regulation 4);
S0601 • the calculation of “qualifying service” (used to determine eligibility for benefits under the 2015 scheme) includes service under the FPJPS (regulation 5(a));
S0602 • the calculation of surviving adult pensions on death in service and death out of service takes into account service in, and entitlements under, the FPJPS (regulation 6(a), 7(a), 10(a) and 11);
S0603 • the definitions in Schedule 2 which are used in relevant parts of the 2015 scheme have been amended to reflect membership of the FPJPS (regulations 12(2)(a) to (c), (3)(b) and (4(a))).
S0604 In addition the definition of “tapered protection member” is amended to reflect the different eligibility and opt-in provisions which apply to those in fee-paid office on 31 March 2012 (regulation 12(2)(d)) and (4)(b));
S0605 • the calculation of ill health and death benefits for those with continuous service in the FPJPS and the 2015 scheme is calculated in the same way as for those with continuous service between an existing scheme and the 2015 Scheme (regulation 12(5), (6), (7)(a), (8) (a), (9) and (10));
S0606 • the repayment of contributions made under the FPJPS is to take place in the same circumstances as repayment of contributions under an existing scheme (regulation 12(13)).
S0607 Ensuring that pensionable service is properly taken into account 132.
S0608 Where the 2015 regulations will refer to a specific period of pensionable service in the FPJPS the amending regulations add a gloss to the definition of pensionable service to ensure that it is the period during which the person held office, rather than the amount of qualifying judicial service, which is taken into account (regulations 5(b), 6(b), 7(b), 10(b), 12(3)(c), 12(7)(b) and 12(8)(b).
S0609 Pensionable earnings for the purposes of determining lump sum death benefits 133.
S0610 Lump sum death benefits under the 2015 scheme are determined by reference to the highest pensionable pay received in the period 12 months before death and in each of the 10 scheme years immediately before the last active scheme year (“final pay”).
S0611 Because the FPJPS does not use the concept of pensionable pay, in order to determine this it is proposed to amend the 2015 scheme regulations to provide the closest equivalent measure by reference to the member’s relevant earnings.
S0612 This is calculated by multiplying by the member’s reckonable service for the period in question the sum which is calculated by multiplying the relevant daily fee by the annual divisor for that office.
S0613 The relevant daily fee is either the highest daily fee paid to a holder of that office in that period or the daily fee which has been determined by an Employment Tribunal to be payable to a holder of that office in that period, whichever is the higher.
S0614 Nominations 134.
S0615 The 2015 scheme recognises death benefit nominations which were made in respect of an existing scheme.
S0616 The same approach will be taken to nominations made in respect of the FPJPS.
S0617 In addition the scheme will recognise any nomination made in relation to potential benefits under the scheme which was made before it came into effect (regulation 12(15)).
S0618 Modification of other provisions 135.
S0619 The Regulations ensure that the modifications made to the Occupational Pension Schemes (Contracting-Out)Regulations 1996 and the Pension Schemes Act 1993 by Schedule 3 to the 2015 regulations include reference to the FPJPS as they do to other existing schemes (regulation 13).
S0620 Question 6: Do you agree that the draft amendment regulations provide parity of treatment for pensions calculated under the Judicial Pension Regulations 2015, to take account of a person’s service in the FPJPS, as they would take account of a person’s entitlement under JUPRA?
S0621 Annex I – the FPJPS draft regulations including Main scheme benefits, Transfers in / out, and the Added Benefits (JAYS, JASAPS, AVC).
S0622 Annex II – Examples by Government Actuary’s Department for retired and active members for ancillary benefits (Added Years, and Added Surviving Adults Benefits).
S0623 Annex III – Undertaking made by the Ministry of Justice Annex IV – Consequential Amendment Regulations Annex I – Fee-Paid Judicial Pension Scheme Draft Regulations Draft Regulations laid before Parliament under section 29(2A) of the Judicial Pensions and Retirement Act 1993, for approval by resolution of each House of Parliament.
