S0752 Appeals SCHEDULE — FEE-PAID OFFICES, ANNUAL DIVISORS The Lord Chancellor in relation to judicial offices for which she is the appropriate Minister within the meaning of section 30 of the Judicial Pensions and Retirement Act 1993(a) and the Secretary of State in relation to judicial offices for which he is the appropriate Minister within the meaning of section 30 of that Act, make the following Regulations in exercise of the powers conferred by section 18A of that Act(a):
S0753 PRELIMINARY Citation and commencement 1.
S0754 These Regulations may be cited as the Judicial Pensions (Fee-Paid Judges) Regulations 2017 and come into force on 1st April 2017.
S0755 Interpretation 2.—(1)
S0756 In these Regulations— “the 1971 Act” means the Pensions (Increase) Act 1971(b);
S0757 “the 1995 Regulations” means the Judicial Pensions (Additional Voluntary Contributions) Regulations 1995(c);
S0758 “the 2017 schemes” means the principal scheme, the JAVC scheme, the FPJAYS and the JASAPS;
S0759 “the JAVC scheme” means the Fee-Paid Judicial Additional Voluntary Contributions Scheme constituted by Part 12;
S0760 “the FPJAYS” means the Fee-Paid Judicial Added Years Scheme constituted by Part 13;
S0761 “the JASAPS” means the Fee-Paid Judicial Added Surviving Adult’s Pension Scheme constituted by Part 14;
S0762 “active member” has the meaning given by regulation 9;
S0763 “the administrators” means the persons entrusted with the administration of the 2017 schemes;
S0764 “appropriate annual salary” has the meaning given by regulation 7;
S0765 “benefits” means any pension or lump sum payable under the principal scheme except where the context otherwise requires;
S0766 “benefit crystallisation event” has the meaning given in section 216 of the Finance Act 2004;
S0767 “the commencement day” means the date on which these Regulations come into force;
S0768 “eligible child” has the meaning given by regulation 40;
S0769 “fee-paid judicial office” has the meaning given by regulation 3(1);
S0770 “the ill-health certification condition” has the meaning given by regulation 23;
S0771 “the interim payments amount” has the meaning given by regulation 49;
S0772 “judicial office” has the meaning given by regulation 3(1);
S0773 “member” means a person who is a member of the principal scheme under regulation 8;
S0774 “normal pension age” means (except in Part 11) age 65, or (if later) the date on which P has completed in aggregate at least 5 years’ qualifying judicial service;
S0775 “opted-out member” has the meaning given by regulation 9;
S0776 “partial retirement” has the meaning given by regulation 3(3);
S0777 “permitted maximum” has the meaning given in section 3(3A) of the Judicial Pensions and Retirement Act 1993;
S0778 “the principal scheme” means the scheme constituted by Parts 2 to 10 of these Regulations;
S0779 “qualifying child” in relation to a member means a child who would be an eligible child if the member was dead;
S0780 “qualifying fee-paid day” has the meaning given by regulation 4;
S0781 “qualifying fee-paid service” has the meaning given by regulation 4;
S0782 “qualifying judicial service” has the meaning given by regulation 6;
S0783 “reckonable service” has the meaning given by regulation 5;
S0784 “registered pension scheme” means a pension scheme that is a registered pension scheme under Chapter 2 of Part 4 of the Finance Act 2004(a);
S0785 “retires” has the meaning given by regulation 3(2) and cognate expressions are to be construed accordingly;
S0786 “scheme actuary” means the actuary appointed by the appropriate Minister for the purposes of these Regulations;
S0787 “surviving adult” has the meaning given by regulation 35(1);
S0788 “surviving adult’s pension” has the meaning given by regulation 35(4);
S0789 “surviving civil partner” has the meaning given by regulation 35(3);
S0790 “surviving spouse” has the meaning given by regulation 35(2);
S0791 “tax year” has the meaning given in section 4(2) of the Income Tax Act 2007(b).
S0792 (2) Where a calculation performed under these Regulations— (a) results in an amount containing a fraction of £1, the amount is to be rounded up to the next whole £1, (b) results in a period containing a fraction of a year, the period is to be given to four decimal places.
S0793 Judicial office, retirement and partial retirement 3.—(1)
S0794 In these Regulations— (a) “judicial office” means an office specified in column 1 of the Schedule (“a specified office”), or an office which has been replaced by a specified office;
S0795 (b) “fee-paid judicial office” means a judicial office held by a person whose service in that office is remunerated by the payment of fees (as opposed to the payment of salary).
S0796 (2) For the purposes of these Regulations, a person (“P”) “retires” at the time when P, having held one or more judicial offices— (a) ceases to hold all such offices, other than by reason of P’s death; and (b) is not immediately afterwards appointed to another judicial office.
S0797 (3) In these Regulations, a reference to a member taking “partial retirement” in respect of a fee- paid judicial office is to the member giving notice in relation to that office under regulation 27(2).
S0798 Qualifying fee-paid days and qualifying fee-paid service 4.—(1)
S0799 For the purposes of these Regulations a day (“the relevant day”) is a “qualifying fee-paid day” worked by a member (“P”) in a fee-paid judicial office, if— (a) P received a fee, in P’s capacity as the holder of that fee-paid judicial office, in respect of the relevant day; and (b) the following conditions are met.
S0800 (2) The conditions are— (a) that P is not an opted-out member of the principal scheme on the relevant day;
S0801 (b) that the relevant day is after 6th April 2000;
S0802 (c) that where, on 1st April 2012, P had not reached the age of 51 years and 6 months, the relevant day is before 1st April 2015; and (d) that where, on 1st April 2012, P had reached the age of 51 years and 6 months but had not reached the age of 55 years, the relevant day is before the specified date.
S0803 (3) The specified date is such date, within the period beginning with 31st May 2015 and ending with 31st January 2022, as is determined by the appropriate Minister by reference to a table published for the purpose of paragraph 3 of Schedule 2 to the Judicial Pensions Regulations 2015(a).
S0804 (4) In these Regulations “qualifying fee-paid service”, in relation to a fee-paid judicial office held by P means (subject to paragraph (5))
S0805 the total number of qualifying fee-paid days worked by P in that office.
S0806 (5) Where the fee paid to P in respect of a qualifying fee-paid day was not paid at the full daily rate for the fee-paid judicial office in question but at a proportion of that rate, only that proportion of the qualifying fee-paid day is to be taken into account under paragraph (4) in determining P’s qualifying fee-paid service.
S0807 Reckonable service 5.—(1)
S0808 In these Regulations, “reckonable service”, in relation to a fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant office”) held by a member (“P”), means the period, expressed in years and any fraction of a year, determined in accordance with the following formula— M N where— (a) M is P’s qualifying fee-paid service in the relevant office;
S0809 (b) N is the annual divisor for that office specified in column 2 of the Schedule.
S0810 (2)
S0811 But where P’s reckonable service in relation to the relevant office, as determined under paragraph (1), is greater than the maximum amount in relation to that office, P’s reckonable service is instead that maximum amount.
S0812 (3) The maximum amount in relation to the relevant office is the amount determined in accordance with the following formula— 20 A B C where A, B and C have the meanings given in paragraphs (4), (5) and (6) respectively.
S0813 (4) A is— (a) where P is not entitled to a pension under Part 1 of the Judicial Pensions and Retirement Act 1993 on the reckonable service date and will not become entitled to such a pension at any time after that date, nil;
S0814 (b) otherwise, the smaller of— (i) the aggregate length of P’s service in qualifying judicial office (within the meaning of that Part of that Act), and (ii) 20.
S0815 (5) B is— (a) where— (i) A is 20, or (ii) P has not, before the reckonable service date, ceased to hold a fee-paid judicial office in respect of which P has taken partial retirement, nil;
S0816 (b) otherwise, the smaller of— (i) the aggregate length of P’s reckonable service in fee-paid judicial offices which P ceased to hold before the reckonable service date and in respect of which P has taken partial retirement, and (ii) 20 A.
S0817 (6) C is— (a) where A B t 20, nil;
S0818 (b) otherwise, the smaller of— (i) where P retired from one or more fee-paid judicial offices on the reckonable service date, the aggregate length of P’s reckonable service in those judicial offices, and (ii) 20 A B.
S0819 (7) For the purposes of paragraphs (5)(b)(i) and (6)(b)(i), the aggregate length of P’s reckonable service in the fee-paid judicial offices referred to in those paragraphs is to be determined in accordance with the formula in paragraph (1) as though paragraph (2) does not apply.
S0820 (8) Where paragraph (5) or (6) do not permit all P’s reckonable service to be taken into account, P’s reckonable service in judicial offices with a higher appropriate annual salary shall be taken into account before P’s reckonable service in judicial offices with a lower appropriate annual salary.
S0821 (9) In this regulation “the reckonable service date” means the day on which P ceases to hold the relevant office.
S0822 Qualifying judicial service 6.—(1)
S0823 In these Regulations, “qualifying judicial service”, in relation to a member (“P”), means the period during which P holds a judicial office.
S0824 (2) For the purposes of determining P’s qualifying judicial service— (a) it does not matter whether P holds a judicial office before or after the commencement day;
S0825 (b) where there is more than one period during which P holds a judicial office, all such periods are to be aggregated;
S0826 (c) where P holds more than one judicial office during any period, the period is to be counted only once; and (d) any period during which P is an opted-out member of the principal scheme is to be disregarded.
S0827 Appropriate annual salary 7.—(1) References in these Regulations to “the appropriate annual salary” of a judicial office (“the relevant judicial office”) held by a member (“P”), are to be construed in accordance with paragraphs (2) and (3).
S0828 (2) Where the relevant judicial office is a fee-paid judicial office, the references are to the amount determined in accordance with the following formula— F u N where— (a) F is the higher of— (i) the highest daily fee payable to a holder of that office within the period of 3 years ending with— (aa) where P takes partial retirement in relation to the relevant judicial office, the day on which P takes partial retirement;
S0829 (bb) otherwise, the day on which P retires, or (ii) the daily fee which has within the period referred to in paragraph (i) been determined by an Employment Tribunal to be payable to a holder of that office, and (b) N is the annual divisor for that office specified in column 2 of the Schedule.
S0830 (3) Where the relevant judicial office is a salaried judicial office, the references are to the highest salary payable to a holder of that office in respect of any continuous period of 12 months falling within the period of 3 years ending with the day on which P retires.
S0831 (4) In this regulation, “salaried judicial office” means a judicial office held by a person which is remunerated by payment of a salary.
S0832 PRINCIPAL SCHEME MEMBERSHIP Members 8.—(1)
S0833 A person (“P”) is a member of the principal scheme if the person held a fee-paid judicial office at any time in the period— (a) beginning with 7th April 2000; and (b) ending with 31st March 2015.
S0834 (2) But— (a) if P retired before the relevant date, P is a member of the principal scheme only if condition A is met; and (b) if P died before the relevant date without having retired, P is a member of the principal scheme only if condition B is met.
S0835 (3) Condition A is that— (a) P notified the appropriate Minister on or before the day 3 months after the day on which P retired, and in a form the appropriate Minister considers to be satisfactory, that P is entitled to a pension by virtue of holding fee-paid judicial office, or (b) an employment tribunal or an industrial tribunal has determined, or the appropriate Minister has accepted, that P is entitled to bring a claim that P has a right to a pension by virtue of holding such office.
S0836 (4) Condition B is that— (a) P’s personal representatives notified the appropriate Minister on or before the day 3 months after the day on which P died, and in a form the appropriate Minister considers to be satisfactory, that benefits are payable in respect of P’s death by virtue of P having held fee-paid judicial office, or (b) an employment tribunal or an industrial tribunal has determined, or the appropriate Minister has accepted, that P’s personal representatives are entitled to bring a claim that P has a right to a pension by virtue of holding such office.
S0837 (5) Where P retired before the relevant date but died before the relevant day, notice under paragraph (3)(a) may be given by P’s personal representatives.
S0838 (6) A person (“P”) is also a member of the principal scheme if— (a) P did not hold a fee-paid judicial office before 1st April 2015;
S0839 (b) P is a person in whose case section 18(1) of the 2013 Act or section 18(1) of the 2014 Act (restriction of existing pension schemes) does not apply in relation to an existing scheme by virtue of provision made under section 18(5) or (5A) of the 2013 Act or section 18(5) or (6) of the 2014 Act (transitional protection under existing schemes); and (c) within the period of 5 years beginning with the final day of service in respect of which P is entitled to benefits under the existing scheme mentioned in sub-paragraph (b), P is appointed to a fee-paid judicial office.
S0840 (7) In paragraph (6)— “the 2013 Act” is the Public Service Pensions Act 2013;
S0841 “the 2014 Act” is the Public Service Pensions Act (Northern Ireland) 2014;
S0842 “existing scheme” has the meaning given by section 18(2) of the Public Service Pensions Act 2013 and section 18(2) of the Public Service Pensions Act (Northern Ireland) 2014.
S0843 (8) For the purposes of this regulation, “the relevant date” is— (a) 2nd February 2013 where P only held a fee-paid judicial office whose jurisdiction is exercised exclusively in relation to Northern Ireland;
S0844 (b) 2nd December 2012 in all other cases.
S0845 Opted-out members and active members 9.—(1)
S0846 A member (“P”) is an “opted-out member” of the principal scheme on any day if P— (a) has, on or before that day, opted out of the principal scheme under regulation 10; and (b) has not opted back in under regulation 11.
S0847 (2) P is an “active member” of the principal scheme on any day if— (a) P is not an opted-out member;
S0848 (b) P holds a fee-paid judicial office on that day; and (c) if P had received a fee in P’s capacity as a holder of that office in respect of that day, the day would have been a qualifying fee-paid day.
S0849 Opting out 10.—(1)
S0850 A member who has not retired may opt out by sending a notice to the appropriate Minister.
S0851 (2) The notice— (a) must be in the form determined by the appropriate Minister, and (b) must specify the date on which the member wishes to opt out.
S0852 (3) The member opts out on the date specified under paragraph (2) or, if earlier, 3 months after the date on which the appropriate Minister receives the notice.
S0853 Opting back in 11.—(1)
S0854 A member (“P”) who has opted out and who has not retired may apply to the appropriate Minister, in the form determined by the appropriate Minister, to opt back in.
S0855 (2) The application must be accompanied by such evidence relating to P’s health as the appropriate Minister may require.
S0856 (3) P must submit to any medical examination as the appropriate Minister may require in connection with the application.
S0857 (4) If the appropriate Minister is satisfied that P is in good health, the appropriate Minister must allow the application.
S0858 (5) The appropriate Minister must, within the period of 3 months beginning with the date on which the application is received, send a notice to P specifying— (a) whether the appropriate Minister has allowed the application; and (b) the date on which the decision was made.
S0859 (6) The member opts back in on the date specified under paragraph (5)(b).
S0860 RETIREMENT BENEFITS Retirement aged 65 or over: entitlement to ordinary pension 12.—(1)
S0861 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day); and (b) on the day on which P retires, P— (i) has reached the age of 65, and (ii) has at least 5 years’ qualifying judicial service.
S0862 (2) On the pension start day, P becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S0863 (3) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day after that on which P retires; or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S0864 Annual rate of ordinary pension 13.—(1)
S0865 The annual rate of a pension to which a member (“P”) becomes entitled under regulation 12 is determined under paragraph (3), (5) or (7) (whichever applies).
S0866 (2) Paragraph (3) applies where, on retirement, P has reckonable service in only one fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant office”).
S0867 (3) The annual rate is determined in accordance with the following formula— R u S 40 where— (a) R is P’s reckonable service in the relevant office;
S0868 (b) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before retirement.
S0869 (4) Paragraph (5) applies where— (a) on retirement, P has reckonable service in more than one fee-paid judicial office; and (b) P did not, at any time before retirement, hold more than one fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S0870 (5) The annual rate is determined in accordance with the following formula— AR u S 40 where— (a) AR is the aggregate length of P’s reckonable service in fee-paid judicial offices;
S0871 (b) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before retirement.
S0872 (6) Paragraph (7) applies where— (a) on retirement, P has reckonable service in more than one fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant offices”); and (b) at any time before retirement, P held more than one fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S0873 (7) The annual rate is determined by taking the following steps— Step 1 Determine the annual rate under paragraph (3), in relation to each relevant office, as if— (i) that office were the only office in which P had reckonable service on retirement (except in determining the maximum amount in relation to that office under regulation 5(3)); and (ii) P held that office (and held no other judicial office) immediately before retirement.
S0874 Step 2 Add together the rates found under Step 1.
S0875 Retirement between the ages of 60 and 65: entitlement to reduced pension 14.—(1)
S0876 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day); and (b) on the day on which P retires, P— (i) has reached the age of 60 but not the age of 65, and (ii) has at least 5 years’ qualifying judicial service.
S0877 (2) On the pension start day, P becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S0878 (3) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day after that on which P retires; or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S0879 Retirement under age of 60 on removal from judicial office: entitlement to reduced pension 15.—(1)
S0880 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day) by virtue of having been removed from all judicial offices held by P;
S0881 (b) on the day on which P retires, P has not reached the age of 60; and (c) the appropriate Minister recommends that P’s accrued rights under the principal scheme be given effect.
S0882 (2) The administrators must notify P of the recommendation under paragraph (1)(c).
S0883 (3) If P elects in writing to the administrators within 3 months of receiving the notice that P wishes to receive benefits under this regulation— (a) P becomes entitled, on the day on which the administrators receive the election, to the payment for life of a pension; and (b) P also becomes entitled, on that day, to the amount P would have been entitled to if P had been entitled under this regulation to the payment of a pension in respect of the period beginning with the pension start day and ending with the day before the day mentioned in sub-paragraph (a).
S0884 (4) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day after that on which P retires; or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S0885 Annual rate of reduced pension 16.—(1)
S0886 The annual rate of a pension to which a member (“P”) becomes entitled under regulation 14 or 15 is determined under paragraph (3), (5) or (7) (whichever applies).
S0887 (2) Paragraph (3) applies where, on retirement, P has reckonable service in only one fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant office”).
S0888 (3) The annual rate is determined in accordance with the following formula— · · C A ¸ ¸¸ ¸ ¨¨ ¨ 40 ** (Reference: 1)

S0894 (e) C is the actuarial reduction factor set out in the Table appropriate to P’s age on P’s birthday following the retirement day.
S0895 (4) Paragraph (5) applies where— (a) on retirement, P has reckonable service in more than one fee-paid judicial office; and (b) P did not, at any time before retirement, hold more than one fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S0896 (5) The annual rate is determined in accordance with the following formula— · · C A ¸ ¸¸ ¸ ¨¨ ¨ 40 ** (Reference: 1)

S0902 (e) C is the actuarial reduction factor set out in the Table appropriate to P’s age on P’s birthday following the retirement day.
S0903 (6) Paragraph (7) applies where— (a) on retirement P has reckonable service in more than one fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant offices”); and (b) at any time before retirement, P held more than one fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S0904 (7) The annual rate is determined by taking the following steps:
S0905 Step 1 Determine the annual rate under paragraph (3), in relation to each relevant office, as if— (i) that office were the only office in which P had reckonable service on retirement (except in determining the maximum amount in relation to that office under regulation 5(3)), and (ii) P held that office (and held no other judicial office) immediately before retirement;
S0906 Step 2 Add together the rates found under Step 1.
S0907 (8) In this regulation— (a) “the retirement day” means the day on which P retires;
S0908 (b) “the Table” means the Table in the Schedule to the Judicial Pensions (Miscellaneous)
S0909 Regulations 1995, as it has effect on the retirement day.
S0910 Retirement under the age of 60: entitlement to preserved pension 17.—(1)
S0911 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day);
S0912 (b) if P retires by virtue of having been removed from all judicial offices held by P— (i) the appropriate Minister makes a recommendation under regulation 15(1)(c), and (ii) P does not make an election under regulation 15(3); and (c) on the day on which P retires, P— (i) has not reached the age of 60, and (ii) has at least 2 years’ qualifying judicial service.
S0913 (2) On the pension start day, P becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S0914 (3) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day on which P reaches normal pension age; or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S0915 Annual rate of preserved pension 18.—(1)
S0916 The annual rate of a pension to which a member (“P”) is entitled under regulation 17 is determined under paragraph (3) or (5) (whichever applies).
S0917 (2) Paragraph (3) applies where, on retirement— (a) P has reckonable service one or more fee-paid judicial office, and (b) P did not, at any time before retirement, hold more than one fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S0918 (3) The annual rate is determined in accordance with the following formula— § R · X ¨ u S ¸ u © 40 Y where— (a) R is the maximum amount of reckonable service which P would be able to accrue if P continued in fee-paid judicial office until the date on which P reaches normal pension age, assuming that P would have accrued a number of qualifying fee-paid days each year until that date equal to— N 3 where N is the number of qualifying fee-paid days which ** (Reference: 1)
S0919 P accrued in the three years before the date on which P retired;
S0920 (b) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before retirement;
S0921 (c) X is the period, expressed in years and any fraction of a year, beginning with the day on which P was first appointed to a fee-paid judicial office (or if later, 7th April 2000) and ending on the day on which P retires from the relevant office;
S0922 (d) Y is the period, expressed in years and any fraction of a year, beginning with the day on which P was first appointed to a fee-paid judicial office (or if later, 7th April 2000) and ending on the day on which P reaches normal pension age (or if later, the day on which P left the last of the relevant offices).
S0923 (4) Paragraph (5) applies where— (a) on retirement P has reckonable service in more than one fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant offices”), and (b) at any time before retirement, P held more than one fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S0924 (5) The annual rate is determined by taking the following steps— Step 1 Determine the annual rate under paragraph (3) of this regulation, in relation to each relevant office, as if— (i) that office were the only office in which P had reckonable service on retirement (except in determining the maximum amount in relation to that office under regulation 5(3)), and (ii) P held that office (and held no other judicial office) immediately before retirement.
S0925 Step 2 Add together the rates found under Step 1.
S0926 Retirement aged 65 or over due to ill health: entitlement to ill health ordinary pension 19.—(1)
S0927 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day);
S0928 (b) on the day on which P retires, P— (i) has reached the age of 65; and (ii) has less than 5 years’ qualifying judicial service; and (c) the ill-health certification condition is met in relation to P.
S0929 (2) On the pension start day, P becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S0930 (3) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day after that on which P retires, or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S0931 Annual rate of ill health ordinary pension 20.
S0932 Regulation 13 (annual rate of ordinary pension) applies in relation to the annual rate of a pension to which a member becomes entitled under regulation 19 as it applies in relation to the annual rate of a pension to which a member becomes entitled under regulation 12.
S0933 Retirement under 65 due to ill health: entitlement to ill health enhanced pension 21.—(1)
S0934 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day);
S0935 (b) on the day on which P retires, P has not reached normal pension age; and (c) the ill-health certification condition is met in relation to P.
S0936 (2) On the pension start day, P becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S0937 (3) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day after that on which P retires, or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S0938 (4) Where, apart from this paragraph, P would be entitled to— (a) a pension under this regulation, and (b) a pension under— (i) regulation 14 (retirement between the ages of 60 and 65: entitlement to reduced pension), (ii) regulation 15 (retirement under the age of 60 due to removal from judicial office:
S0939 entitlement to reduced pension), or (iii) regulation 17 (retirement under the age of 60: entitlement to preserved pension), P is not entitled to the pension mentioned in sub-paragraph (b).
S0940 Annual rate of ill health enhanced pension 22.—(1)
S0941 The annual rate of a pension to which a member (“P”) becomes entitled under regulation 21 is determined under paragraph (3), (5) or (7) (whichever applies).
S0942 (2) Paragraph (3) applies where, on retirement, P has reckonable service in only one fee-paid judicial office.
S0943 (3) The annual rate is determined in accordance with the following formula— R U u S u 40 T where— (a) R is P’s reckonable service in the relevant office;
S0944 (b) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before retirement (“the relevant office”);
S0945 (c) U is the smaller of— (i) the amount equal to T E, and (ii) the maximum amount for the relevant office, determined in accordance with regulation 5(3);
S0946 (d) T is the smaller of— (i) the length of P’s relevant judicial appointment (see paragraph (8)), and (ii) the maximum amount for the relevant office, determined in accordance with regulation 5(3);
S0947 (e) E is the service enhancement (see paragraph (9)).
S0948 (4) Paragraph (5) applies where— (a) on retirement, P has reckonable service in more than one fee-paid judicial office, and (b) P did not, at any time before retirement, hold more than one fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S0949 (5) The annual rate is determined in accordance with the following formula— AR U u S u 40 T where— (a) AR is the aggregate length of P’s reckonable service in fee-paid judicial offices;
S0950 (b) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before retirement (“the relevant office”);
S0951 (c) U is the smaller of— (i) the amount equal to T E, and (ii) the maximum amount for the relevant office, determined in accordance with regulation 5(3);
S0952 (d) T is the smaller of— (i) the length of P’s relevant judicial appointment (see paragraph (8)); and (ii) the maximum amount for the relevant office, determined in accordance with regulation 5(3);
S0953 (e) E is the smaller of— (i) the service enhancement (see paragraph (9)); and (ii) the amount determined in accordance with the formula 20 T.
S0954 (6) Paragraph (7) applies where— (a) on retirement P has reckonable service in more than one fee-paid judicial office, and (b) at any time before retirement, P held more than one fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S0955 (7) The annual rate is determined by taking the following steps.
S0956 Step 1 Determine the annual rate under this regulation, in relation to each relevant office, as if— (i) that office were the only office in which P had reckonable service on retirement (except in determining the maximum amount in relation to that office under regulation 5(3)), and (ii) P held that office (and held no other judicial office) immediately before retirement.
S0957 Step 2 Add together the rates found under Step 1.
S0958 (8) The length of P’s relevant judicial appointment is the period, or the aggregate of the periods, expressed in years and any fraction of a year, during which P held one or more fee-paid judicial offices, disregarding any day in respect of which the conditions in regulation 4(2) (qualifying fee- paid days) are not met.
S0959 (9) The service enhancement is the period, expressed in years and any fraction of a year, which is one half of the period— (a) beginning with the day after that on which P retires, and (b) ending with the day on which P reaches normal pension age.
S0960 Ill-health certification condition 23.—(1)
S0961 The ill-health certification condition is met in relation to a member (“P”) if the appropriate Minister— (a) has received a medical certificate stating that P has suffered a permanent breakdown in health that makes P incapable of discharging the duties of the judicial office or offices which P held immediately before retirement;
S0962 (b) is satisfied as to the matters contained in that certificate; and (c) has, where he or she is the Lord Chancellor, consulted— (i) the Lord Chief Justice of England and Wales
S0963 (if P held a judicial office in England and Wales);
S0964 (ii) the Lord Chief Justice of Northern Ireland (if P held a judicial office in Northern Ireland).
S0965 (2) For the purposes of this regulation P’s breakdown in health is “permanent” if it is likely to continue until— (a) the day on which P reaches the age of 65; or (b) if later, the day on which, if P had not retired, P would have completed 5 years’
S0966 qualifying judicial service.
S0967 (3) The Lord Chief Justice of England and Wales may nominate a judicial office holder (as defined in section 109(4) of the Constitutional Reform Act 2005(a)) to exercise any of his or her functions under this regulation.
S0968 (4) The Lord Chief Justice of Northern Ireland may nominate— (a) the holder of one of the offices listed in Schedule 1 to the Justice (Northern Ireland) Act 2002(b);
S0969 (b) a Lord Justice of Appeal (as defined in section 88 of that Act), to exercise any of his or her functions under this regulation.
S0970 Times at which pension under this Part is payable 24.
S0971 A pension under this Part is payable at such intervals, not exceeding 3 months, as the Treasury may determine.
S0972 Lump sum: entitlement and amount 25.—(1)
S0973 This regulation applies where a member (“P”) retires on or after the commencement day, and becomes entitled to the payment of a pension under this Part.
S0974 (2) At the time P becomes entitled to the payment of the pension, P becomes entitled to a lump sum.
S0975 (3) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula— A u.2 25 where A is the annual rate of the pension payable to P under this Part.
S0976 Multiple retirements 26.—(1)
S0977 This regulation applies where a member (“P”) retires and subsequently resumes judicial office.
S0978 (2) The resumption of judicial office does not affect the entitlement of P to any payment under the principal scheme in respect of any period before the resumption.
S0979 (3) No amounts are payable to or in respect of P under the principal scheme as a result of the retirement mentioned in paragraph (1) in respect of any period after the resumption.
S0980 (4) On P’s retirement after resuming judicial office— (a) P’s entitlement (and that of any other person) to any payment under the principal scheme;
S0981 and (b) the rate or amount of any such payment, are (subject to paragraph (5)) to be determined as if no amounts had previously been paid under the principal scheme as a result of the retirement mentioned in paragraph (1).
S0982 (5) A person to whom a lump sum is paid under the principal scheme as a result of the retirement mentioned in paragraph (1) is not required to refund the lump sum; but if the whole or any part of it is not refunded, an amount equal to so much of it as has not been refunded is to be deducted from any lump sum which subsequently becomes payable to or in respect of that person under the principal scheme.
S0983 PARTIAL RETIREMENT Option to take partial retirement in relation to a fee-paid judicial office 27.—(1)
S0984 Paragraph (2) applies where a member (“P”) at any time on or after the commencement day ceases to hold a fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant office”) and— (a) P has reckonable service in relation to the relevant office;
S0985 (b) at the time P ceases to hold the relevant office, P does not hold any other fee-paid judicial office;
S0986 (c) at the time P ceases to hold the relevant office, P has at least 2 years’ qualifying judicial service; and (d) immediately after ceasing to hold the relevant office, P is appointed to another judicial office.
S0987 (2) P may take partial retirement in relation to the relevant office by giving notice in writing to the appropriate Minister.
S0988 (3) The notice— (a) must be in the form determined by the appropriate Minister, and (b) must be received by the appropriate Minister within the period of 6 months beginning with the day on which P ceases to hold the relevant office.
S0989 (4) A notice under this regulation— (a) has effect on the day on which it is received by the appropriate Minister, and (b) is irrevocable.
S0990 Effect of partial retirement 28.—(1) Where a member (“P”) takes partial retirement in relation to a fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant office”), P is treated for the purposes of the relevant provisions as if P had retired on the day on which P ceased to hold the relevant office.
S0991 (2) In this regulation “the relevant provisions” means— (a) Part 3 (retirement benefits) apart from regulation 26 (multiple retirements), and (b) Part 6 (death benefits).
S0992 (3) In the application of the relevant provisions in relation to P’s deemed retirement under paragraph (1), P is treated as having reckonable service only in the relevant office.
S0993 (4) The application of the relevant provisions in relation to P’s deemed retirement under paragraph (1) (and P’s pension under Part 3 in respect of that deemed retirement) does not prevent their application in relation to P’s actual retirement (and P’s pension under Part 3 in respect of that retirement).
S0994 (5) In the application of the relevant provisions in relation to P’s actual retirement, P is treated as having no reckonable service in the relevant office.
S0995 BENEFITS FOR PERSONS ENTITLED TO PENSION CREDITS Application of Part and interpretation 29.—(1)
S0996 This Part applies where— (a) a person (“the transferee”) becomes entitled to a pension credit under section 29(1)(b) of the Welfare Reform and Pensions Act 1999(a)
S0997 (“the 1999 Act”) or under Article 26(1)(b) of the Welfare Reform and Pensions (Northern Ireland)
S0998 Order 1999(b) (“the 1999 Order”); and (b) that pension credit is derived from the rights of another person (“P”) under the principal scheme.
S0999 (2) In this Part “the transferee”, “the 1999 Act”, “the 1999 Order” and “P” have the meanings given by paragraph (1).
S1000 Pension credit derived from the principal scheme: entitlement to pension 30.—(1)
S1001 On the pension start day the transferee becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S1002 (2) In paragraph (1) “the pension start day” means— (a) the day on which the transferee reaches normal pension age, or (b) if later, the day after the last day of the implementation period as determined in accordance with section 34 of the 1999 Act or Article 31 of the 1999 Order.
S1003 Annual rate of pension under regulation 30 31.—(1)
S1004 The annual rate of the pension to which the transferee is entitled under regulation 30 is the rate in relation to which the following condition is met.
S1005 (2) The condition is that the sum of— (a) the value of the pension, and (b) the value of any lump sum to which the transferee is entitled under this Part, equals the amount of the pension credit.
S1006 (3) For this purpose, the value of the pension and of any lump sum must be calculated— (a) in accordance with regulations made by the Secretary of State under paragraph 5(b) of Schedule 5 to the 1999 Act, or (b) (in any case where the 1999 Order applies) in accordance with regulations made by the Department for Communities under paragraph 5(b) of Schedule 5 to the 1999 Order, and any regulations made by the Secretary of State or by the Department for Communities for the purposes of those provisions apply accordingly.
S1007 (4) The annual rate of the pension must be calculated in accordance with guidance issued by the appropriate Minister.
S1008 (5) Before issuing guidance under paragraph (4), the appropriate Minister must consult the Government Actuary.
S1009 Lump sum for transferee at age 65 32.—(1)
S1010 This regulation applies if the transferee becomes entitled to the pension credit before P becomes entitled to a lump sum under Part 3.
S1011 (2) At the time the transferee becomes entitled to a pension under regulation 30, the transferee becomes entitled to a lump sum.
S1012 (3) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula— A u.2 25 where A is the annual rate of the pension to which the transferee is entitled under this Part.
S1013 Lump sum where transferee dies before age 65 33.—(1)
S1014 This regulation applies if the transferee dies before reaching the normal pension age.
S1015 (2) On the day after that on which the transferee dies the relevant person becomes entitled to a lump sum.
S1016 (3) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula— A u.2 25 where A is the annual rate of the pension to which the transferee would have been entitled under regulation 30 if the transferee had become entitled to a pension under that regulation on the day on which the transferee died.
S1017 (4) In paragraph (2) “the relevant person” means— (a) the person nominated by the transferee for the purpose of this regulation by notice in writing to the administrators; or (b) in default of such nomination, the transferee’s personal representatives.
S1018 Exclusions 34.
S1019 The appropriate Minister has no power to accept for the benefit of a member of the principal scheme— (a) a payment under paragraph 1(3) of Schedule 5 to the 1999 Act or paragraph 1(3) of Schedule 5 to the 1999 Order, (b) a payment under section 95 of the Pension Schemes Act 1993(a) or section 91 of the Pensions Schemes (Northern Ireland) Act 1993(b), or (c) any other payment, to the extent that that payment directly or indirectly represents a pension credit.
S1020 DEATH BENEFITS Interpretation 35.—(1)
S1021 In these Regulations “surviving adult”, in relation to a deceased member (“P”), means P’s surviving spouse or surviving civil partner.
S1022 (2) In these Regulations “surviving spouse”, in relation to P, means a person who— (a) was married to P on the date of P’s death, and (b) if P retired before death, entered into the marriage before P retired.
S1023 (3) In these Regulations “surviving civil partner”, in relation to P, means a person who— (a) was in a civil partnership with P on the date of P’s death, and (b) if P retired before death, entered into the civil partnership before P retired.
S1024 (4) In these Regulations “surviving adult’s pension” means a pension payable to a surviving adult under this Part.
S1025 Entitlement to a surviving adult’s pension 36.—(1)
S1026 This regulation applies where a member (“P”) dies (whether before, on or after the commencement day) leaving a surviving adult.
S1027 (2) On the pension start day, the surviving adult becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S1028 (3) For the purposes of paragraph (2) the “pension start day” means— (a) the day after the day on which P dies; or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S1029 (4) Where the surviving adult marries or enters into a civil partnership, the Treasury may, at any time, direct that payment of the pension be withheld.
S1030 (5) The Treasury may, at any time, direct that payment of a pension withheld under paragraph (4) be resumed.
S1031 Annual rate of surviving adult’s pension 37.—(1)
S1032 The annual rate of a pension to which a person becomes entitled under regulation 36 is equal to one half of the rate of the pension of the deceased member (“P”).
S1033 (2) For the purposes of this regulation the rate of P’s pension is determined as follows.
S1034 (3) Where P dies on or after the commencement day after having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P was entitled under Part 3 on the day on which P died.
S1035 (4) Where P dies on or after the commencement day without having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the day after that on which P died if— (a) on the day on which P died, P had not died but had instead retired, and (b) the ill-health certification condition were met in relation to P.
S1036 (5) Where P dies before the commencement day after having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the commencement day if P was still alive on that day.
S1037 (6) Where P dies before the commencement day without having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the commencement day if— (a) on the day on which P died, P had not died but had instead retired, (b) the ill-health certification condition were met in relation to P, and (c) P were still alive on the commencement day.
S1038 Annual rate of surviving adult’s pension: special provision for surviving civil partners 38.—(1)
S1039 Paragraph (2) applies where— (a) the surviving civil partner in relation to a member (“P”) becomes entitled to a pension under regulation 36;
S1040 (b) P held a judicial office before, and on, 5th December 2005; and (c) P elects in writing to the administrators, within the period of 6 months beginning with the relevant day, that the annual rate of the surviving civil partner’s pension under regulation 37 should be calculated as if P first held a judicial office on 5th December 2005.
S1041 (2) The rate of P’s pension, for the purposes of regulation 37, is to be determined on the basis that P first held a judicial office on 5th December 2005.
S1042 (3) In this regulation “the relevant day” means— (a) the commencement day, or (b) if later, the day on which the civil partnership is entered into.
S1043 (4) An election under this regulation is irrevocable.
S1044 Payment of a surviving adult’s pension 39.
S1045 A surviving adult’s pension is payable at such intervals, not exceeding 3 months, as the Treasury may determine.
S1046 Meaning of “eligible child”
S1047 40.—(1)
S1048 In these Regulations, “eligible child”, in relation to a deceased member, means a person who meets the age condition or the occupation condition (see regulation 41) and who is— (a) a natural child of the deceased member, (b) a person who was adopted by the deceased member before the deceased member retired or, where paragraph (2) applies, after the deceased member retired, (c) a person who is a child of the deceased member by virtue of a parental order made before the deceased member retired or, where paragraph (2) applies, after the deceased member retired, or (d) a step-child of the deceased member.
S1049 (2) This paragraph applies where the Treasury have issued a direction to the effect that they are satisfied that— (a) the deceased member had, before the deceased member retired, formed the intention of adopting, or applying for a parental order in respect of, the person; and (b) immediately before the deceased member retired, the person was wholly or mainly dependent on the deceased member.
S1050 (3) In a case where the deceased member died while holding fee-paid judicial office, the references in subsections (1)(b) and (2)(b) to things done before the deceased member’s retirement are to be read as references to things done before the deceased member’s death.
S1051 (4) For the purposes of this regulation a person (“C”) is a “natural child” of person (“P”) if P is C’s genetic father or mother (including anyone who is to be treated as C’s father, mother or parent under Part 2 of the Human Fertilisation and Embryology Act 2008(a)
S1052 (“the 2008 Act”), but not including anyone who is not to be so treated under that Part of that Act).
S1053 (5) For the purposes of this regulation a person (“C”) is a “step-child” of a person (“P”) if C— (a) is not a natural child of P, adopted by P or a child of P by virtue of a parental order, and (b) meets any of the following conditions.
S1054 (6) The first condition is that C— (a) is a natural child of a person who was at any time the spouse or civil partner of P, and (b) at the time the marriage or civil partnership was entered into, had been born or conceived.
S1055 (7) The second condition is that C— (a) was adopted by a person who was at any time the spouse or civil partner of P; and (b) was so adopted— (i) before the time the marriage or civil partnership was entered into, or (ii) after the time the marriage or civil partnership was entered into in a case where the adoption proceedings were pending at the time of the marriage or civil partnership.
S1056 (8) The third condition is that C— (a) is, by virtue of a parental order, a child of a person who was at any time the spouse or civil partner of P; and (b) the parental order was made— (i) before the time the marriage or civil partnership was entered into, or (ii) after the time the marriage or civil partnership was entered into in a case where the parental order proceedings were pending at the time of the marriage or civil partnership.
S1057 (9) In this regulation a “parental order” means an order made under section 54 of the Human Fertilisation and Embryology Act 2008.
S1058 Meaning of “eligible child”: the age condition and the occupation condition 41.—(1)
S1059 The age condition is that the person has not reached the age of 16.
S1060 (2) The occupation condition is that the person (“C”) has, at all times since reaching the age of 16, been— (a) in full-time education, or (b) undergoing eligible training for a trade, profession or vocation.
S1061 (3) For the purposes of this regulation training is “eligible” if— (a) it is full-time;
S1062 (b) its duration is at least 2 years; and (c) the rate of pay (if any) receivable by C, or payable by the employer in respect of C, in relation to the training does not exceed the specified maximum.
S1063 (4) In this regulation— “pay” means any salary, fees, wages, perquisites, profits or gains and includes the value of any free board, lodging or clothing;
S1064 “the specified maximum” at any time means an annual rate equal to that at which a pension of £250 a year— (a) first awarded under the Principal Civil Service Pension Scheme on 1st June 1972; and (b) increased from time to time by the amount of increase that would be applied under the 1971 Act to such a pension, would (as so increased) be payable at that time.
S1065 (5) Where a premium has been paid in respect of C’s training, all emoluments receivable by C, or payable by the employer in respect of C, are to be taken, for the purposes of paragraph (3)(c), as receivable or payable by way of return of the premium, unless and to the extent that the amount of those emoluments exceeds the amount of the premium.
S1066 (6) Where the Treasury are satisfied that C’s full-time education ought not be regarded as completed, the Treasury may direct that any period during which a person is not in full-time education and is not undergoing eligible training for a trade, profession or vocation, is to be disregarded for the purposes of this regulation.
S1067 Entitlement to an eligible child’s pension 42.—(1)
S1068 This regulation applies if a member (“P”) dies (whether before, on or after the commencement day) leaving one or more eligible children.
S1069 (2) On the pension start day, and until such time as there are no more eligible children, the relevant person becomes entitled to the payment of a pension.
S1070 (3) In this regulation, the “pension start day” means— (a) the day after the day on which P dies; or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S1071 (4) In this regulation, “the relevant person” means such person or persons as the Treasury may from time to time direct.
S1072 Annual rate of eligible child’s pension 43.—(1)
S1073 The annual rate of a pension to which a person becomes entitled under regulation 42, where P dies without leaving a surviving adult, is— (a) in relation to any period during which there is only one eligible child, an annual rate equal to one third of the rate of P’s pension;
S1074 (b) in relation to any period during which there are two or more eligible children, an annual rate equal to two thirds of the rate of P’s pension.
S1075 (2) The annual rate of a pension to which a person becomes entitled under regulation 42, where P dies leaving a surviving adult, is— (a) in relation to any period during which there is only one eligible child and the surviving adult is still alive, an annual rate equal to one quarter of the rate of P’s pension;
S1076 (b) in relation to any period during which there is only one eligible child and the surviving adult is dead, an annual rate equal to one third of the rate of P’s pension;
S1077 (c) in relation to any period during which there are two or more eligible children and the surviving adult is still alive, an annual rate equal to one half of the rate of P’s pension;
S1078 (d) in relation to any period during which there are two or more eligible children and the surviving adult is dead, an annual rate equal to two thirds of the rate of P’s pension.
S1079 (3) The Treasury may direct, where the deceased member leaves a surviving adult who marries or enters into a civil partnership, that paragraph (1) is to apply instead of paragraph (2) in respect of any period during which the surviving adult has a spouse or a civil partner.
S1080 (4) The Treasury may, at any time, cancel a direction given under paragraph (3).
S1081 (5) For the purposes of this regulation the “rate of P’s pension” is determined as follows.
S1082 (6) Where P dies on or after the commencement day after having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P was entitled under Part 3 on the day on which P died.
S1083 (7) Where P dies on or after the commencement day without having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the day after that on which P died if— (a) on the day on which P died, P had not died but had instead retired, and (b) the ill-health certification condition were met in relation to P.
S1084 (8) Where P dies before the commencement day after having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the commencement day if P was still alive on that day.
S1085 (9) Where P dies before the commencement day without having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the commencement day if— (a) on the day on which P died, P had not died but had instead retired, (b) the ill-health certification condition were met in relation to P, and (c) P had survived until the commencement day.
S1086 Payment and application of an eligible child’s pension 44.—(1)
S1087 A pension to which a person is entitled under regulation 42 is payable at such intervals, not exceeding 3 months, as the Treasury may determine (2) A person to whom a pension or part of a pension under regulation 42 is paid must apply the pension for the benefit of— (a) all eligible children of the deceased member; or (b) such eligible children of the deceased member as the Treasury may direct.
S1088 Lump sum on death: death in service 45.—(1)
S1089 Paragraph (2) applies where— (a) a member (“P”) dies (on or after the commencement day) without having retired; and (b) P is (immediately before death) an active member of the principal scheme.
S1090 (2) On the day after that on which P dies, the relevant person becomes entitled to a lump sum.
S1091 (3) The amount of the lump sum to which P becomes entitled under paragraph (2) is determined under paragraph (5), (7) or (9), whichever applies).
S1092 (4) Paragraph (5) applies where, on P’s death, P has reckonable service in only one fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant office”).
S1093 (5) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula—
S1094 where— (a) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before P’s death, determined as if P had retired on the date of P’s death;
S1095 (b) M is P’s qualifying fee-paid service in the relevant office; and (c) N is the annual divisor for that office specified in column 2 of the Schedule;
S1096 (d) JS is P’s qualifying judicial service, expressed in years and any fraction of a year.
S1097 (6) Paragraph (7) applies where— (a) on P’s death, P has reckonable service in more than one fee-paid judicial office; and (b) P did not, at any time before death, hold more than one fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S1098 (7) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula—
S1099 where— (a) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before P’s death, determined as if P had retired on the date of P’s death;
S1100 (b) AM is the aggregate length of P’s qualifying fee-paid service in fee-paid judicial offices;
S1101 (c) JS is the lower of P’s qualifying judicial service, expressed in years and any fraction of a year.
S1102 (8) Paragraph (9) applies where— (a) immediately before death P held more than one fee-paid judicial office, and (b) at any time before retirement, P held more than one fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S1103 (9) The amount of the lump sum is determined by taking the following steps.
S1104 Step 1 Determine the lump sum under this regulation in relation to each fee-paid judicial office, as if that office were the only relevant office held by P.
S1105 Step 2 Add together the lump sums found under Step 1.
S1106 (10)
S1107 In this regulation “the relevant person” means— (a) the person nominated by P for the purpose of this regulation by notice in writing to the administrators; or (b) in default of such nomination, P’s personal representatives.
S1108 Lump sum on death: death after retirement of member with preserved pension 46.—(1)
S1109 Paragraph (2) applies if— (a) a member (“P”) dies (on or after the commencement day) having retired (whether before, on or after the commencement day);
S1110 (b) where P retires on or after the commencement day, P is not, on retirement, an opted-out member; and (c) if P had not died P would, on reaching normal pension age have become entitled to a pension under regulation 17 (retirement under the age of 60: entitlement to preserved pension).
S1111 (2) On the day after that on which P dies, P’s personal representatives become entitled to a lump sum.
S1112 (3) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula— A u.2 25 where A is a notional annual rate of pension determined in accordance with regulation 18 as though P had reached normal pension age on the day P died.
S1113 Lump sum on death: death soon after retirement of member entitled to pension 47.—(1)
S1114 Paragraph (2) applies if— (a) a member (“P”) dies (on or after the commencement day) having retired (whether before, on or after the commencement day);
S1115 (b) where P retires on or after the commencement day, P is not, on retirement, an opted-out member;
S1116 (c) immediately before death, P was entitled to the payment of a pension under Part 3 (retirement benefits); and (d) the total benefits received are less than the minimum benefits amount.
S1117 (2) On the day after that on which P dies, P’s personal representatives become entitled to a lump sum equal to the difference between the total benefits received and the minimum benefits amount.
S1118 (3) In this regulation “the total benefits received” means the amount determined in accordance with the following formula—
S1119 where— (a) A is the interim payments amount (if any) in relation to P;
S1120 (b) B is the amount (if any) paid or payable to P under regulation 50 (payment of benefits in relation to the pre-commencement period);
S1121 (c) C is the total of the sums paid or payable to P under Part 3 on account of the pension (including any increases under the 1971 Act), and by way of lump sum, in relation to the period after commencement day;
S1122 (d) D is the amount (if any) paid or payable by P under regulation 51 (repayment of any excess interim payments).
S1123 (4) In this regulation “the minimum benefits amount” means the amount determined in accordance with the following formula— E u 5 where E is the annual rate of the pension payable to P under Part 3 immediately before P’s death.
S1124 BENEFITS IN RESPECT OF PERIODS BEFORE COMMENCEMENT Application of Part 48.—(1)
S1125 This Part applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retired, or died without having retired, before the commencement day, and (b) condition A or B is met.
S1126 (2) Condition A is that amounts were paid to any person during the pre-commencement period in respect of P’s retirement or death under arrangements made for the purpose of providing benefits during the pre-commencement period corresponding to those under Part 3 or 6.
S1127 (3) Condition B is that, if these Regulations had come into force on the day before the day on which P retired or died, amounts would have been payable during the pre-commencement period— (a) to P under Part 3, or (b) to another person under Part 6 in respect of P’s retirement or death.
S1128 (4) In this Part— (a) “the pre-commencement period” means the period— (i) beginning with the day on which P retired or on which P died without having retired, and (ii) ending with the day before the commencement day.
S1129 (b) references to “P” are to a member mentioned in paragraph (1)(a).
S1130 Meaning of “the interim payments amount” and “the pre-commencement benefits amount”
S1131 49.—(1)
S1132 In these Regulations “the interim payments amount”, in relation to any person, means the total amount (if any) that was paid to that person under arrangements made for the purpose of providing benefits during the pre-commencement period in respect of P’s retirement or death corresponding to those under Part 3 or 6.
S1133 (2) In this Part “the pre-commencement benefits amount”, in relation to any person, means, subject to paragraph (3), the total amount (if any) that would have been payable to that person under Part 3 or 6 during the pre-commencement period in respect of P’s retirement or death if these Regulations had come into force on the day before the day on which P retired or died without having retired.
S1134 (3) Where— (a) the amount payable to a person in respect of P under paragraph (2) includes a lump sum payable under regulation 45 or 46, and (b) P would have been liable to pay the pre-commencement contributions amount referred to in regulation 53(1) if P had survived until the commencement day, the pre-commencement benefits amount payable to that person is to be calculated after deducting the pre-commencement contributions amount (within the meaning of regulation 53) which would have been payable by P if these Regulations had come into force on the day before the day on which P died.
S1135 Additional payment where no or insufficient interim payments made 50.—(1)
S1136 Paragraph (2) applies where the interim payments amount in relation to a person is less than the pre-commencement benefits amount in relation to that person.
S1137 (2) The administrators must pay to the person the amount of the difference between the two amounts mentioned in paragraph (1).
S1138 Repayment of any excess interim payments 51.—(1) Paragraph (2) applies where the interim payments amount in relation to a person is greater than the pre-commencement benefits amount in relation to that person.
S1139 (2) The person must pay to the administrators the amount of the difference between the two amounts mentioned in paragraph (1).
S1140 (3) Where the person is entitled to the payment of a pension under Part 3 or 6, the liability imposed by this regulation is to be discharged by such deductions from payments of the pension as the administrators may decide.
S1141 (4) Where the person is not entitled to the payment of a pension under Part 3 or 6, the liability imposed by this regulation may be recovered as a debt due to the appropriate Minister.
S1142 CONTRIBUTIONS FOR PRE-COMMENCEMENT SERVICE Interpretation 52.
S1143 In this Part— “fee period” means a period specified in column 1 of the Contributions Table;
S1144 “the pre-commencement contributions amount” has the meaning given by regulation 53(1);
S1145 “P” means a member;
S1146 “the Contributions Table” means the table set out in regulation 57(1).
S1147 Liability to pay pre-commencement contributions amount 53.—(1)
S1148 P must pay to the appropriate Minister an amount (“the pre-commencement contributions amount”), being the sum of— (a) the pre-commencement personal contributions amount (see regulation 54), and (b) the pre-commencement dependants’ contributions amount (see regulation 55).
S1149 (2) Paragraph (1) is subject to paragraphs (3) and (4).
S1150 (3) P is not required to pay the pre-commencement contributions amount if P— (a) retired before the commencement day, and (b) had on retirement less than two years qualifying judicial service.
S1151 (4) P is not required to pay so much of the pre-commencement contributions amount as is represented by the amount mentioned in paragraph (1)(b) if P— (a) retired before the commencement day, (b) was not married or in a civil partnership at any time during the period— (i) beginning with the day on which P first held a fee-paid judicial office, and (ii) ending with the day on which P retired; and (c) did not have a qualifying child at any time during that period.
S1152 Calculation of pre-commencement personal contributions amount 54.
S1153 The pre-commencement personal contributions amount in relation to P is determined as follows— Step 1 For each fee period, find the total of the fees paid to P in respect of qualifying fee-paid days falling within the period.
S1154 Step 2 Multiply the total found under Step 1 for each fee period by the percentage specified in relation to that fee period in column 2 of the Contributions Table.
S1155 Step 3 Add together the amounts found under Step 2.
S1156 Calculation of pre-commencement dependants’ contributions amount 55.
S1157 The pre-commencement dependants’ contributions amount in relation to P is determined as follows— Step 1 For each fee period, find the total of the fees paid to P in respect of qualifying fee-paid days falling within the period.
S1158 Step 2 Multiply the total found under Step 1 for each fee period by the percentage specified in relation to that fee period in column 3 of the Contributions Table.
S1159 Step 3 Add together the amounts found under Step 2.
S1160 Qualifying fee-paid days disregarded if they do not add to reckonable service 56.—(1)
S1161 A qualifying fee-paid day worked in a fee-paid judicial office is disregarded for the purposes of regulations 54 and 55 if P’s reckonable service in relation to that office would have been the maximum amount in relation to that office, had P retired on the day before that day.
S1162 (2) The reference in paragraph (1) to the maximum amount in relation to an office is to that amount as determined in accordance with regulation 5(3)
S1163 (reckonable service).

S1204 CONTRIBUTIONS FOR SERVICE ON OR AFTER COMMENCEMENT DAY Interpretation 59.
S1205 In this Part— “fee year” means the period beginning with 1st April in any year and ending with the 31st March in the following year, and “P” means a member.
S1206 Liability to pay contributions on fees in respect of qualifying fee-paid days 60.
S1207 P must pay a contribution to the appropriate Minister on any fee paid to P in respect of a qualifying fee-paid day where the day is, or is after, the commencement day.
S1208 Qualifying fee-paid days disregarded if they do not add to reckonable service 61.—(1)
S1209 Regulation 60 does not require P to pay a contribution in respect of a qualifying fee- paid day worked in a fee-paid judicial office if P’s reckonable service in relation to that office would have been the maximum amount in relation to that office, had P retired on the day before that day.
S1210 (2) The reference in paragraph (1) to the maximum amount in relation to an office is to that amount as determined in accordance with regulation 5(3)
S1211 (reckonable service).
S1212 Amount of contribution 62.—(1)
S1213 The amount of the contribution payable on a fee under regulation 60 is the sum of— (a) the personal benefits contribution, and (b) the dependants’ benefits contribution.
S1214 (2) The amount of the personal benefits contribution is the amount of the fee multiplied by the percentage specified in column 2 of the table in regulation 63 for the relevant fee year, in the row relating to P’s annualised fees for that year.
S1215 (3) The amount of the dependants’ benefits contribution is the amount of the fee multiplied by the percentage specified in column 3 of the same row of the same table.
S1216 (4) P’s annualised fees for the relevant fee year are determined in accordance with the following formula— 365 F u N where— (a) F is the amount of the fee;
S1217 (b) N is the number of days in the applicable payment period.
S1218 (5) For the purposes of paragraph (4) the “applicable payment period” means— (a) where the qualifying fee-paid day in respect of which the fee is paid is the first qualifying fee-paid day to occur in the relevant fee year, the period— (i) beginning with the first day of the relevant fee year, and (ii) ending with the day before the qualifying fee-paid day;
S1219 (b) where the qualifying fee-paid day in respect of which the fee is paid is the second, or a later, qualifying fee-paid day to occur in the relevant fee year, the period— (i) beginning with the most recent previous qualifying fee-paid day, and (ii) ending with the day before the qualifying fee-paid day.
S1220 (6) If the relevant fee year is a leap year, the formula in paragraph (4) has effect as if for “365” there were substituted “366”.
S1221 (7) In this regulation “the relevant fee year” means the fee year in which the qualifying fee-paid day in respect of which the fee is paid falls.
