S1644 EXPLANATORY NOTE (This note is not part of the Regulations)
S1645 These Regulations make provision for a pension scheme for the benefit of those people who have held a fee-paid judicial office in the period between 7th April 2000 and 31st March 2015 (“the principal scheme”), and establishes the Fee-Paid Judicial Added Voluntary Contributions Scheme, the Fee-Paid Judicial Added Years Scheme and the Fee-paid Judicial Added Surviving Adult’s Pension Scheme to enable members of the principal scheme to pay voluntary contributions towards the costs of additional benefits under one or more of these additional schemes.
S1646 Part 1 (Preliminary matters: regulations 1 to 7) defines the main concepts used in these Regulations.
S1647 In particular, “reckonable service”, which will determine the level of pension to which a fee-paid judge is entitled, is defined as the total number of qualifying fee-paid days the judge has worked in a fee-paid office, divided by the annual divisor specified in column 2 of the Schedule.
S1648 This is subject to a maximum amount (provided for in regulation 5(3) to (9)) of 20 years, less any other pensionable service to which the judge is entitled.
S1649 Part 2 (scheme membership: regulations 8 to 11) sets out who may be a member of the principal scheme and how a member of the scheme may opt out of it.
S1650 Part 3 (retirement benefits: regulations 12 to 26) provides for the benefits available under the principal scheme: a pension for life and a lump sum.
S1651 It sets out how the annual rate of the pension is to be calculated.
S1652 This will depend the age at which the judge retires, either voluntarily or on ill- health grounds (a judge who retires between the ages of 60 and 65 will be entitled to a reduced pension, and a judge who retires under the age of 60 will be entitled to a preserved pension).
S1653 Where a judge retires due to ill-health the annual rate of their pension may be enhanced (see regulations 21 and 22).
S1654 Provision is made for an ill-health certification condition (see regulation 23), and for the case where a judge retires and subsequently resumes judicial office (see regulation 26).
S1655 Part 4 (partial retirement: regulations 27 to 28) sets out how the provisions on retirement benefits will apply where a judge ceases to hold a fee-paid judicial office and is immediately appointed to another judicial office.
S1656 This Part does not apply where a fee-paid judge holds more than one judicial office and only ceases to hold one of them.
S1657 Part 5 (benefits for persons entitled to pension credits: regulations 29 to 34) gives effect to pension credits created when a pension sharing order is made under Part 4 of the Welfare Reform and Pensions Act 1999 following the end of a marriage or civil partnership in relation to rights of a member of the principal scheme.
S1658 It sets out what benefits will be received by the beneficiary of such a pension sharing order.
S1659 Part 6 (death benefits: regulations 35 to 47) makes provision for the benefits which will be received by the surviving spouse or civil partner (referred to as the “surviving adult”) of a fee-paid judge who is a member of the principal scheme following the death of the judge.
S1660 They also provide for the benefits which will be received in respect of a child if the judge dies.
S1661 Part 7 (benefits in respect of periods before commencement: regulations 48 to 51) concerns the amounts due to fee-paid judges who retired or died before these Regulations came into force.
S1662 It applies where a judge has received interim payments to provide benefits during that period, or where a judge would have received benefits if these Regulations were in force before they retired or died.
S1663 Part 8 (contributions for pre-commencement service: regulations 52 to 58) makes provision for the contributions which would have been due in relation to pre-commencement benefits, identifying those members who are liable to pay such contributions, and setting out how the amount of the contributions is to be calculated.
S1664 Part 9 (contributions for service on or after commencement day: regulations 59 to 64) provides for the contributions which must be paid from fees earned by fee-paid judges in relation to service after the commencement of these Regulations.
S1665 Part 10 (repayment of dependants’ benefits contributions: regulations 65 to 69) provides for contributions paid by a fee-paid judge in respect of dependants’ benefits to be repaid where the judge has not married, entered a civil partnership or had an eligible child.
S1666 It also provides for repayment for any such contributions paid after a judge ceased to be married, in a partnership or to have an eligible child.
S1667 Part 11 (transfers: regulations 70 to 88) set out the circumstances in which a member of the principal scheme is entitled to take the cash equivalent of their benefits under the scheme and transfer them to another scheme, or transfer rights accrued under a registered pension scheme into the principal scheme.
S1668 Part 12 (judicial added voluntary contribution scheme: regulations 89 to 101) provide for a separate pension scheme into which a member may choose to make additional contributions in return for additional benefits.
S1669 Membership of the scheme is only open to active members of the principal scheme.
S1670 Part 13 (fee-paid judicial added years scheme: regulations 102 to 114) provides for another pension scheme which enables members to purchase additional years which will increase the length of their reckonable service (and therefore the amount of their pension).
S1671 Membership of the scheme is open to retired and active members of the principal scheme (but not members who have opted out of that scheme) who held a qualifying judicial office before 6th April 2006 (and were not contributing to any other registered pension scheme before the commencement day).
S1672 Part 14 (the fee-paid judicial added surviving adult’s pension scheme: regulations 115 to 126) provides for another separate pension scheme, which just like the added years scheme, is open to members of the principal scheme who held qualifying judicial office before 6th April 2006.
S1673 The scheme enables members to buy added units of surviving adult pension.
S1674 Each unit adds £1,000 to the value of the pension the member’s surviving spouse or civil partner would receive on the member’s death.
S1675 Part 15 (appeals: regulation 127) provides for a right of appeal to the Minister against any decision concerning one of the schemes provided for in these Regulations.
S1676 An impact assessment has not been produced for this instrument as no significant impact on the costs of business or the voluntary sector is foreseen.
S1677 Annex II – Examples from Government Actuary’s Department
S1678 The Fee-Paid Judicial Added Years Scheme Sample member 1 – a retired member Marital status M Date of birth 10 April 1945 Assumed contribution commencement date 10 April 2005 Date of actual retirement 10 April 2015 Appropriate annual salary at assumed contribution commencement date £77,000 Tax year of assumed contribution commencement date 2005/06 £175,000 Total fees earned between assumed contribution commencement date and retirement 4.0 (years)
S1679 Retained benefits at assumed contribution commencement date (equivalent service credit)
S1680 Reckonable service at retirement 2.5 (years)
S1681 £3,150 Contributions made to other judicial pension schemes between assumed contribution commencement date and retirement Regulation 105(2) limits the amount of added years the member is allowed to purchase and regulation 107 specifies the maximum contributions he is allowed to make.
S1682 Reckonable service at retirement of 2.5 years is calculated as shown in example set out earlier in the consultation document.
S1683 This is defined as A in regulation 105(2).
S1684 Retained benefits are the value of the benefits available in any other scheme to which the member was entitled at the assumed contribution commencement date.
S1685 These will be converted to an equivalent service credit by the scheme actuary.
S1686 As a rough indication, a service credit of 4.0 years for this member would be roughly equivalent to retained benefits with a value of around £100,000 at the assumed contribution commencement date.
S1687 This service credit is defined as B in regulation 105(2).
S1688 Regulation 105(2) prescribes that the maximum number of added years that this retired member may purchase is:
S1689 20 – (A + B), that is in this case:
S1690 20 – (2.5 + 4) = 13.5 years.
S1691 Regulation 107 provides that the maximum contributions the member can make towards buying added years is 15% of the fee income in each tax year less contributions made to any other judicial pension scheme in that tax year.
S1692 Contributions made to any other judicial pension scheme include the contributions towards the widow’s pension of 1.8% of earnings, and contributions to any other additional voluntary judicial pension schemes.
S1693 For this member the maximum amount of contributions the member is allowed to make is:
S1694 15% x £175,000 – £3,150 = £23,100 We then work out the cost of one added year in accordance with Regulation 106.
S1695 Step 1 We look up the personal benefits and family benefits percentage figures appropriate to the member’s salary group set out in Schedule 1 to the Judicial Pensions (Additional Voluntary Contributions)
S1696 Regulations 1995 (SI 1995/639, or ‘the 1995 Regulations’).
S1697 The assumed contribution commencement date of 10 April 2005 falls in the 2005/06 tax year.
S1698 The HMRC earnings cap for the 2005/06 tax year was £105,600.
S1699 The member’s appropriate annual salary at 10 April 2005, expressed as a percentage of the earnings cap, is £77,000 / £105,600 = 73% of the earnings cap, and so we need to use the personal benefits and family benefits percentage figures for salary group 3.
S1700 The member is 10 years from retirement at the assumed contribution commencement date, so the correct rates are 32.8% for the personal benefits and 6.4% for the family benefits, which gives a total of 39.2%.
S1701 This is defined as A/100 in Regulation 106(3).
S1702 Step 2
S1703 The member would have paid the required contributions over the term of his employment, which was 10 years.
S1704 This is defined as B in Regulation 106(3).
S1705 So the member would have paid 39.2% / 10 = 3.92% of his salary each year to purchase one added year.
S1706 This gives us (A/100)/B.
S1707 Step 3
S1708 The member actually earned £175,000 in fees over his service.
S1709 This is defined as C in Regulation 106(3).
S1710 He therefore needs to pay:
S1711 (A/100)/B x C that is in this case:
S1712 3.92% x £175,000 = £6,860 to buy one added year.
S1713 Step 4 Check whether either of the restrictions above on the amount of added years or maximum contributions bite, those restrictions being respectively 13.5 years and £23,100.
S1714 Therefore if the member wants to buy one added year, at a cost of £6,860, he would be able to do so.
S1715 If the member wanted to buy four added years, the cost would be 4 x £6,860 = £27,440.
S1716 Although four added years falls within the available headroom of 13.5 years, the cost exceeds the maximum contribution of £23,100 and so it would not be possible to buy four added years.
S1717 In this case, the member could buy up to a maximum of 3.367 added years, the cost of which would be 3.367 x £6,860 = £23,100, the maximum contribution payable.
S1718 Sample member 2 – an active member Marital status M Date of birth 10 April 1955 Assumed contribution commencement date 10 April 2005 Agreed commencement date 10 April 2017 Date of assumed retirement 10 April 2020 Salary at assumed contribution commencement date £77,000 Tax year of assumed contribution commencement date 2005/06 £200,000 Total fees earned between assumed contribution commencement date and agreed commencement date 4.0 (years)
S1719 Retained benefits at assumed contribution commencement date (equivalent service credit)
S1720 Reckonable service at date of assumed retirement 7.5 (years)
S1721 £3,600 Contributions made to other judicial pension schemes to the agreed commencement date First, we calculate the limits on the purchase of added years by active members under regulation 109(3) and the limits on the contributions payable by active members under regulation 110(4).
S1722 The reckonable service at the member’s assumed retirement date based on the member’s assumed sitting pattern is 7.5 years.
S1723 The retained benefits at the assumed contribution commencement date convert to an equivalent service credit of 4 years (see comment on example 1 for details on how this would be calculated).
S1724 Under regulation 109(3), the maximum number of added years which may be purchased is 20 – (7.5 + 4) = 8.5 years.
S1725 Under regulation 110(4), the maximum contributions the member can make towards his added years up to the fee-paid scheme commencement date is:
S1726 15% x £200,000 – £3,600= £26,400 We then calculate the cost of one added year.
S1727 Step 1 We look up the personal benefits and family benefits percentage figures appropriate to the member’s salary group set out in Schedule 1 to the 1995 Regulations.
S1728 The assumed contribution commencement date of 10 April 2005 falls in the 2005/06 tax year.
S1729 The HMRC earnings cap for the 2005/06 tax year was £105,600.
S1730 The member’s appropriate annual salary at 10 April 2005, expressed as a percentage of the earnings cap, is £77,000 / £105,600 = 73% of the earnings cap, and so we need to use the personal benefits and family benefits percentage figures for salary group 3.
S1731 The member is 15 years from retirement at the assumed contribution commencement date, so the correct rates are 31.0% for the personal benefits and 6.6% for the family benefits, which gives a total of 37.6%.
S1732 This is defined as A/100 in Regulation 110(1).
S1733 Step 2
S1734 The member’s appropriate annual salary at the assumed contribution commencement date is £77,000.
S1735 The cost of one added year is 37.6% x £77,000 = £28,952.
S1736 This is defined as A/100 x B in Regulation 110(1).
S1737 Step 3
S1738 We next need to split this cost between the periods before and after the agreed commencement date of 10 April 2017, after which periodic contributions towards these benefits will be made whilst the member is in service.
S1739 In this case, the period between the assumed contribution commencement date and the assumed retirement date is 15 years, while the period between the assumed contribution commencement date and the agreed commencement date is 12 years.
S1740 So the lump sum payment which the member needs to pay to purchase one added year is:
S1741 £28,952 x 1 x 12/15 = £23,162.
S1742 This is defined as (C x D / E) x F in Regulation 110(4b).
S1743 The member is restricted by the limit on contributions of £26,400 calculated above, and so the maximum number of added years he can buy is £26,400 / £23,162 = 1.1398 added years.
S1744 The member can make one lump sum payment of £23,162; or if he prefers, a lump sum payment below £23,162.
S1745 He can then spread the difference between the lump sum payment he made and £23,162 over the period between the agreed commencement date and the assumed retirement date.
S1746 Step 4 We now need to calculate the periodic contributions the member needs to make from the agreed commencement date.
S1747 The period between the assumed contribution commencement date and the assumed retirement date is 15 years, while the period between the agreed commencement date and the assumed retirement date is 3 years.
S1748 So the total amount of periodic payments the member needs to pay to purchase one added year is:
S1749 £28,952 x 1x 3/15 = £5,790.
S1750 We add to this any amount due which has not been paid by the member as a lump sum at step 3.
S1751 So, say the member has paid £20,000 as a lump sum, he will still need to pay £3,162 out of his future pay, which means that £5,790 + £3,162 = £8,952 will need to be met by periodic payments from his future pay.
S1752 This is defined as ((C x D)/E x F) + X in Regulation 110(7).
S1753 Step 5 On retirement the scheme actuary will calculate how many added years the contributions actually made by the member have purchased (Regulation 111 (1)).
S1754 If at retirement the member has underpaid towards the added years he wished to purchase, his added years will be pro-rated down accordingly.
S1755 This could happen if he retires earlier than assumed or sat fewer days than expected.
S1756 Conversely, if he has overpaid he will receive a refund of the excess contributions under Regulation 112(4).
S1757 The Fee-Paid Judicial Added Surviving Adults Pension Scheme Sample member 1 – a retired member Marital status M Date of birth 10 April 1945 Assumed contribution commencement date 10 April 2005 Date of actual retirement 10 April 2015 £77,000 Appropriate annual salary at assumed contribution commencement date Tax year of assumed contribution commencement date 2005/06 £175,000 Total fees earned between assumed contribution commencement date and retirement 4.0 (years)
S1758 Retained benefits at assumed contribution commencement date (equivalent service credit)
S1759 Reckonable service at retirement 2.5 (years)
S1760 £3,150 Contributions made to other judicial pension schemes between the assumed contribution commencement date and retirement First, we calculate the limits on the purchase of added benefits by retired members under regulation 119(2) and the limits on contributions under regulation 121(1).
S1761 Reckonable service at the member’s retirement date based on the member’s sitting pattern is 2.5 years and the annual salary at the assumed contributions commencement date is £77,000.
S1762 The maximum added benefits the member can purchase under regulation 121(1) is therefore 2.5 x £77,000 / 45 = £4,278 p.a.
S1763 Under regulation 121(1), the maximum level of contributions the member can make towards his added years is:
S1764 15% x £175,000 – £3,150 = £23,100 We then calculate the cost of £1,000 p.a.
S1765 additional surviving partner’s pension.
S1766 Step 1
S1767 The member’s age last birthday on the assumed contribution commencement date is 60.
S1768 The member retired at age 70.
S1769 We therefore look up the appropriate value of A in Schedule 2 to the 1995 regulations (as amended by SI 1996/52, or ‘the 1996 regulations’) which is 441.
S1770 Under Regulation 120(3) the cost of £1,000 p.a. surviving partner’s pension is then A x 12= £5,292.
S1771 The maximum surviving partner’s pension the member would be able to purchase is the lesser of £4,278 p.a. and £4,365 p.a.
S1772 (i.e. £23,100 / £5,292 x £1,000, being the surviving partner’s pension which can be purchased with the maximum contribution of £23,100).
S1773 The maximum surviving partner’s pension which may be purchased is £4,278 p.a.
S1774 Sample member 2 – an active member Marital status M Date of birth 10 April 1955 Assumed contribution commencement date 10 April 2005 Agreed commencement date 10 April 2017 Date of assumed retirement 10 April 2020 Salary at assumed contribution commencement date £77,000 Tax year of assumed contribution commencement date 2005/06 £200,000 Total fees earned between assumed contribution commencement date and agreed commencement date 4.0 (years)
S1775 Retained benefits at assumed contribution commencement date (equivalent service credit)
S1776 Reckonable service at retirement 7.5 (years)
S1777 £3,600 Contributions made to other judicial pension schemes to the agreed commencement date First, we calculate the limits on the purchase of added survivor pension by active members under regulation 123(2) and the limits on the contributions by active members under regulation 125(2).
S1778 Reckonable service at the member’s assumed retirement date based on the member’s assumed sitting pattern is 7.5 years.
S1779 Under regulation 123(2), the maximum added survivor pension is 7.5 x £77,000 / 45 = £12,833 p.a.
S1780 (or 12.833 units)
S1781 Under regulation 125(2), the maximum level of lump sum contributions the member can make towards his added surviving partner pension up to the fee-paid scheme commencement date is:
S1782 15% x £200,000 – £3,600 = £26,400 We then calculate the cost of £1,000 p.a. added survivor pension.
S1783 Step 1
S1784 The member’s age last birthday on the assumed contribution commencement date is 50.
S1785 The member expects to retire at age 65.
S1786 We can therefore look up the appropriate value of A in Schedule 2 to the 1995 regulations (as amended by the 1996 regulations) which is 317.
S1787 Under Regulation 125(1) the cost of £1,000 p.a.
S1788 surviving adult’s pension is then A x 12 = £3,804.
S1789 Step 2
S1790 We next need to split this cost between the periods before and after the agreed commencement date of 10 April 2017, after which periodic contributions towards these benefits will be made whilst the member is in service.
S1791 In this case, the period between the assumed contribution commencement date and the assumed retirement date is 15 years, while the period between the assumed contribution commencement date and the agreed commencement date is 12 years.
S1792 So the amount which the member needs to pay for £12,833 p.a.
S1793 surviving partner’s pension (if he wished to buy the maximum number of units) would be:
S1794 £3,804 x 12.8331 x 12/15 = £39,053.
S1795 This is defined as ((C x D) / E) x F in Regulation 125(2) (b).
S1796 The member is however restricted by the limit on lump sum contributions of £26,400, which allows the purchase of £6,940 p.a. added survivor pension (i.e. £26,400/£3,804)
S1797 The member can make one lump sum payment of £26,400; or if he prefers, a lump sum payment below that amount (the member may make up to three lump sum payments within three years).
S1798 Step 3 We now need to calculate the periodic contributions the member needs to make from the agreed commencement date.
S1799 The period between the assumed contribution commencement date and the assumed retirement date is 15 years, while the period between the agreed commencement date and the assumed retirement date is 3 years.
S1800 So the total amount of periodic payments the member needs to pay to purchase £1,000 p.a. added survivor pension is 3,804 x 1 x 3/15 = £761.
S1801 The amount required to purchase 5.89 units of added surviving adult pension (assuming that the member wishes to purchase the maximum number of units possible, and that he has purchased 6.94 units by lump sum payment) would be:
S1802 £3,804 x 5.89 x 3/15 = £4,481 Annex III – Undertaking by Ministry of Justice (1) Salaried judges had the option of exercising rights relating to the Judicial Added Voluntary Contribution Scheme, the Judicial Added Years Scheme and/or pension fund transfers (provided that they met the eligibility requirements for each scheme during the period that it was available under JUPRA).
S1803 (2) The fee-paid judiciary did not have these rights at the material time because they did not have any pension entitlement.
S1804 (3) The proposals for a fee-paid judicial pension scheme (FPJPS) include provisions that are intended to mirror those rights.
S1805 Individuals who would have exercised these rights (if they had been members of JUPRA) will have a period in which to do so after FPJPS commences.
S1806 It is proposed that the pension scheme manager will have a discretion to permit individuals who elect to exercise these rights to do so over a longer period than the normal period for doing so under FPJPS.
S1807 Only those individuals with transitional or tapering protection will have rights under the schemes going forward (for as long as each scheme continued or continues under JUPRA).
S1808 (4) The Respondent acknowledges that some individuals who would have exercised these rights at the material time may be treated less favourably than their salaried comparator on ground of part-time status.
S1809 In particular, HMRC will treat the rights as having been exercised for tax purposes on the date when the individual’s payments or transfers take place, whereas the salaried judges would have been taxed at the material time.
S1810 Depending on the individual’s financial situation at each point in time, this may or may not disadvantage them.
S1811 (5) The Respondent is not currently aware of any individual who will be treated less favourably on ground of part-time status as a result of the tax treatment of the Judicial Added Voluntary Contribution Scheme, the Judicial Added Years Scheme and/or pension fund transfers under FPJPS.
S1812 However, the Respondent confirms that it will compensate any individual who is in that position by undertaking to make good any difference in tax treatment on the basis of part-time status and to publish said undertaking in the literature which goes to all scheme members.
S1813 (6) For the avoidance of doubt, the Respondent considers that any such individual would have to establish the following elements (which would establish that they were treated less favourably than their salaried comparator on ground of part-time status and that this caused financial loss) in relation to any claim that they would have exercised their rights relating to the Judicial Added Voluntary Contribution Scheme, the Judicial Added Years Scheme and/or pension fund transfers on a particular date or dates from 7 April 2000:
S1814 (i) that the individual is entitled to be a member of FPJPS in respect of their service on the particular date or dates (from 7 April 2000) on which they claim that they would have exercised their rights relating to the Judicial Added Voluntary Contribution Scheme, the Judicial Added Years Scheme and/or pension fund transfers:
S1815 (ii) that the individual would have been eligible to exercise the right in question at the material time (taking into account the period when each scheme was in existence and the applicable criteria at the material time);
S1816 (iii) if so eligible, that the individual was likely to have chosen to exercise their rights (as claimed) at the material time, taking into account all of the circumstances;
S1817 (iv) if so, that the tax implications of exercising those rights under the FPJPS are less favourable than the tax implications that would have applied to them if they had exercised those rights at the material time; and (v) if so, that they have mitigated their loss (for example, by timing any payments under the FPJPS in the most tax efficient manner).
S1818 Annex IV – Consequential Amendment Regulations Draft Regulations laid before Parliament under section 38 of the Public Service Pensions Act, for approval by resolution of each House of Parliament.

S1900 Name Lord Chancellor Date Ministry of Justice We consent Name Name Two of the Lords Commissioners of Her Majesty’s Treasury Date EXPLANATORY NOTE (This note is not part of the Regulations)
S1901 These Regulations amend the Judicial Pensions Regulations 2015 (S.I. 2015/182) (“the 2015 Regulations”) consequential upon the making of the Judicial Pensions (Fee-Paid Judges)
S1902 Regulations 2017 (S.I.xxx) (“the 2017 Regulations”)
S1903 in order to ensure that, where relevant, pensions calculated under the 2015 regulations take account of a person’s service in the scheme established by the 2017 Regulations as they would take account of a person’s entitlements under the scheme established by the Judicial Pensions and Retirement Act 1993 (c.8).
S1904 Regulation 14 ensures that the pension entitlements of, or in respect of, individuals who have already retired or died and who would have been a member of the scheme established by the 2017 Regulations if they had been in force are calculated in the same way as those who die or retire after the coming into force of these regulations.
S1905 An impact assessment has not been prepared for this instrument as no impact on the costs of business or the voluntary sector is foreseen.
S1906 Question 1: Do you agree that the FPJPS regulations as drafted provide a pension scheme for eligible fee-paid judges that is no less favourable than the pension provision for salaried judges under the JUPRA scheme?
S1907 Question 2: If you do not agree please explain why and specifically in relation to which part of the regulations?
S1908 Question 3: In relation to trivial pensions, should the regulations allow the facility to take a single one off payment rather than receive a monthly pension?
S1909 Question 4: In relation to trivial pensions, if you agree with having the facility for a one off payment, what should be the amount?
S1910 Question 5: In relation trivial pensions, should this be an automatic process or always be the choice of the individual member?
S1911 Question 6: Do you agree that the draft amendment regulations provide parity of treatment for pensions calculated under the Judicial Pension Regulations 2015, to take account of a person’s service in the FPJPS, as they would take account of a person’s entitlement under JUPRA?

S1938 Addendum to Consultation on Fee Paid Judicial Pension Scheme Draft Regulations This Addendum draws attention to a revised version of the draft regulations for the Fee Paid Judicial Pension Scheme (FPJPS), which is issued for consultation until 31 October 2016.
S1939 The Consultation on the original draft regulations was issued on 15 September and was intended to run until 20 October 2016.
S1940 This period has been extended for the reasons set out below.
S1941 During the consultation period we have found that the draft FPJPS regulations do not give effect to the MoJ’s policy – as set out in the consultation document itself - regarding eligibility in relation to time limits (see particularly page 9, paragraph 27 and the table at the top of page 10 regarding eligibility).
S1942 The same position stands in relation to the policy set out in the previous MoJ consultation document issued on 19 September 2014 (see page 6 regarding membership) and the MoJ response to that consultation issued on 23 March 2015 (page 7 and paragraph 24 regarding eligibility under the scheme).
S1943 As originally drafted, Regulation 8 does not define membership of the FPJPS in relation to a particular office in accordance with the Miller judgment.
S1944 In addition, the calculation of benefits under the original draft regulations suggested that someone who is eligible to be a member of the scheme is invariably entitled to receive benefits in relation to all the fee-paid judicial offices they have held after 6 April 2000.
S1945 As amended to reflect the policy set out above, the revised draft regulations now clarify who is entitled to be a member of the FPJPS by reference to a particular office (see page 9 paragraph 27 and the table on page 10 of the consultation issued 15 September 2016).
S1946 They also ensure that an office holder who ceased to hold judicial office before the date the moratorium took effect is only entitled to benefits under the FPJPS for service in that office, if he or she made an Employment Tribunal or Northern Ireland Industrial Tribunal claim for a pension in relation to that office within the relevant time limits.
S1947 To achieve this effect, the revised draft regulations show the revisions made in track changes.
S1948 
S1949 Due to this change in the draft regulations for the FPJPS, the consultation period has been extended to 31 October 2016.
S1950  We continue to welcome responses and it would be helpful to receive them as early as possible.
S1951  You should also note that the revised draft regulations issued with this addendum also include an updated Schedule 1 of eligible fee-paid judicial offices.
S1952 Judicial Policy, Ministry of Justice 10 October 2016.
S1953 Draft Regulations laid before Parliament under section 29(2A) of the Judicial Pensions and Retirement Act 1993, for approval by resolution of each House of Parliament.

S2112 “permitted maximum” has the meaning given in section 3(3A) of the Judicial Pensions and Retirement Act 1993;
S2113 “the principal scheme” means the scheme constituted by Parts 2 to 10 of these Regulations;
S2114 “qualifying child” in relation to a member means a child who would be an eligible child if the member was dead;
S2115 “qualifying fee-paid day” has the meaning given by regulation 4;
S2116 “qualifying fee-paid service” has the meaning given by regulation 4;
S2117 “qualifying judicial service” has the meaning given by regulation 6;
S2118 “reckonable service” has the meaning given by regulation 5;
S2119 “registered pension scheme” means a pension scheme that is a registered pension scheme under Chapter 2 of Part 4 of the Finance Act 2004(a);
S2120 “retires” has the meaning given by regulation 3(23) and cognate expressions are to be construed accordingly;
S2121 “scheme actuary” means the actuary appointed by the appropriate Minister for the purposes of these Regulations;
S2122 “surviving adult” has the meaning given by regulation 35(1);
S2123 “surviving adult’s pension” has the meaning given by regulation 35(4);
S2124 “surviving civil partner” has the meaning given by regulation 35(3);
S2125 “surviving spouse” has the meaning given by regulation 35(2);
S2126 “tax year” has the meaning given in section 4(2) of the Income Tax Act 2007(b).
S2127 (2) Where a calculation performed under these Regulations— (a) results in an amount containing a fraction of £1, the amount is to be rounded up to the next whole £1, (b) results in a period containing a fraction of a year, the period is to be given to four decimal places.
S2128 Judicial office, retirement and partial retirement 3.—(1)
S2129 In these Regulations— (a) “judicial office” means an office specified in column 1 of the Schedule (“a specified office”), or an office which has been replaced by a specified office;
S2130 (b) “fee-paid judicial office” means a judicial office held by a person whose service in that office is remunerated by the payment of fees (as opposed to the payment of salary).
S2131 (2) For the purposes of these Regulations, a fee-paid judicial office held by a person is an “eligible fee-paid judicial office” if it falls within regulation 8(1) or (5)(c) (offices which qualify a person for membership of the principal scheme).
S2132 (2)(3)
S2133 For the purposes of these Regulations, a person (“P”) “retires” at the time when P, having held one or more judicial offices— (a) ceases to hold all such offices, other than by reason of P’s death; and (b) is not immediately afterwards appointed to another judicial office.
S2134 (3)(4)
S2135 In these Regulations, a reference to a member taking “partial retirement” in respect of a fee-paid judicial office is to the member giving notice in relation to that office under regulation 27(2).
S2136 (a) 2004 c. 12 (b) 2007 c. 3.
S2137 Qualifying fee-paid days and qualifying fee-paid service 4.—(1)
S2138 For the purposes of these Regulations a day (“the relevant day”) is a “qualifying fee-paid day” worked by a member (“P”) in aan eligible fee-paid judicial office, if— (a) P received a fee, in P’s capacity as the holder of that fee-paid judicial office, in respect of the relevant day; and (b) the following conditions are met.
S2139 (2) The conditions are— (a) that P is not an opted-out member of the principal scheme on the relevant day;
S2140 (b) that the relevant day is after 6th April 2000;
S2141 (c) that where, on 1st April 2012, P had not reached the age of 51 years and 6 months, the relevant day is before 1st April 2015; and (d) that where, on 1st April 2012, P had reached the age of 51 years and 6 months but had not reached the age of 55 years, the relevant day is before the specified date.
S2142 (3) The specified date is such date, within the period beginning with 31st May 2015 and ending with 31st January 2022, as is determined by the appropriate Minister by reference to a table published for the purpose of paragraph 3 of Schedule 2 to the Judicial Pensions Regulations 2015(a).
S2143 (4) In these Regulations “qualifying fee-paid service”, in relation to aan eligible fee-paid judicial office held by P means (subject to paragraph (5))
S2144 the total number of qualifying fee-paid days worked by P in that office.
S2145 (5) Where the fee paid to P in respect of a qualifying fee-paid day was not paid at the full daily rate for the fee-paid judicial office in question but at a proportion of that rate, only that proportion of the qualifying fee-paid day is to be taken into account under paragraph (4) in determining P’s qualifying fee-paid service.
S2146 Reckonable service 5.—(1)
S2147 In these Regulations, “reckonable service”, in relation to aan eligible fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant office”) held by a member (“P”), means the period, expressed in years and any fraction of a year, determined in accordance with the following formula— M N (a) M is P’s qualifying fee-paid service in the relevant office;
S2148 (b) N is the annual divisor for that office specified in column 2 of the Schedule.
S2149 (2)
S2150 But where P’s reckonable service in relation to the relevant office, as determined under paragraph (1), is greater than the maximum amount in relation to that office, P’s reckonable service is instead that maximum amount.
S2151 (3) The maximum amount in relation to the relevant office is the amount determined in accordance with the following formula— 20   A  B  C  where A, B and C have the meanings given in paragraphs (4), (5) and (6) respectively.
S2152 (4) A is— (a) S.I.2015/182.
S2153 (a) where P is not entitled to a pension under Part 1 of the Judicial Pensions and Retirement Act 1993 on the reckonable service date and will not become entitled to such a pension at any time after that date, nil;
S2154 (b) otherwise, the smaller of— (i) the aggregate length of P’s service in qualifying judicial office (within the meaning of that Part of that Act), and (ii) 20.
S2155 (5) B is— (a) where— (i) A is 20, or (ii) P has not, before the reckonable service date, ceased to hold aan eligible fee-paid judicial office in respect of which P has taken partial retirement, nil;
S2156 (b) otherwise, the smaller of— (i) the aggregate length of P’s reckonable service in eligible fee-paid judicial offices which P ceased to hold before the reckonable service date and in respect of which P has taken partial retirement, and (ii) 20  A.
S2157 (6) C is— (a) where A B  20, nil;
S2158 (b) otherwise, the smaller of— (i) where P retired from one or more eligible fee-paid judicial offices on the reckonable service date, the aggregate length of P’s reckonable service in those judicial offices, and (ii) 20   A  B .
S2159 (7) For the purposes of paragraphs (5)(b)(i) and (6)(b)(i), the aggregate length of P’s reckonable service in the eligible fee-paid judicial offices referred to in those paragraphs is to be determined in accordance with the formula in paragraph (1) as though paragraph (2) does not apply.
S2160 (8) Where paragraph (5) or (6) do not permit all P’s reckonable service to be taken into account, P’s reckonable service in judicial offices with a higher appropriate annual salary shall be taken into account before P’s reckonable service in judicial offices with a lower appropriate annual salary.
S2161 (9) In this regulation “the reckonable service date” means the day on which P ceases to hold the relevant office.
S2162 Qualifying judicial service 6.—(1)
S2163 In these Regulations, “qualifying judicial service”, in relation to a member (“P”), means the period during which P holds a judicial office.
S2164 (2) For the purposes of determining P’s qualifying judicial service— (a) it does not matter whether P holds a judicial office before or after the commencement day;
S2165 (b) where there is more than one period during which P holds a judicial office, all such periods are to be aggregated;
S2166 (c) where P holds more than one judicial office during any period, the period is to be counted only once; and (d) any period during which P is an opted-out member of the principal scheme is to be disregarded.
S2167 Appropriate annual salary 7.—(1) References in these Regulations to “the appropriate annual salary” of a judicial office (“the relevant judicial office”) held by a member (“P”), are to be construed in accordance with paragraphs (2) and (3).
S2168 (2) Where the relevant judicial office is a fee-paid judicial office, the references are to the amount determined in accordance with the following formula— F  N (a) F is the higher of— (i) the highest daily fee payable to a holder of that office within the period of 3 years ending with— (aa) where P takes partial retirement in relation to the relevant judicial office, the day on which P takes partial retirement;
S2169 (bb) otherwise, the day on which P retires, or (ii) the daily fee which has within the period referred to in paragraph (i) been determined by an Employment Tribunal to be payable to a holder of that office, and (b) N is the annual divisor for that office specified in column 2 of the Schedule.
S2170 (3) Where the relevant judicial office is a salaried judicial office, the references are to the highest salary payable to a holder of that office in respect of any continuous period of 12 months falling within the period of 3 years ending with the day on which P retires.
S2171 (4) In this regulation, “salaried judicial office” means a judicial office held by a person which is remunerated by payment of a salary.
S2172 PRINCIPAL SCHEME MEMBERSHIP Members 8.—(1)
S2173 A person (“P”) is a member of the principal scheme if the personP held a fee-paid judicial office at any time in the period beginning with 7th April 2000 and ending with 31st March 2015 and either— (a) beginning with 7th April 2000; and (b) ending with 31st March 2015.
S2174 (2) But— (a) if P retireddid not cease to hold that office before the relevant date, (a)(b) P is a member of the principal scheme only ifceased to hold that office before the relevant date and condition A is met; and, or (b)(c) if P died before the relevant date without having retired, P is a member of the principal scheme only ifceased to hold that office and condition B is met.
S2175 (3)(2) Condition A is that— (a) P notified the appropriate Minister on or before the day 3 months after the day on which P retired, ceased to hold that office, and in a form the appropriate Minister considers to be satisfactory, that P is entitled to a pension by virtue of holding fee-paid judicial office,that office; or (b) an employment tribunal or an industrial tribunal has determined, or the appropriate Minister has accepted, that P is entitled to bring a claim that P has a right to a pension by virtue of holding suchthat office.
S2176 (4)(3) Condition B is that— (a) P’s personal representatives notified the appropriate Minister on or before the day 3 months after the day on which P died, and in a form the appropriate Minister considers to be satisfactory, that benefits are payable in respect of P’s death by virtue of P having held fee-paid judicialthat office, or (b) an employment tribunal or an industrial tribunal has determined, or the appropriate Minister has accepted, that P’s personal representatives are entitled to bring a claim that P has a right to a pension by virtue of holding suchP having held that office.
S2177 (5)(4) Where P retiredceased to hold a fee-paid judicial office before the relevant date but died before the relevant day, notice under paragraph (32)(a) may be given by P’s personal representatives.
S2178 (6)(5)
S2179 A person (“P”) is also a member of the principal scheme if— (a) P did not hold a fee-paid judicial office before 1st April 2015;
S2180 (b) P is a person in whose case section 18(1) of the 2013 Act or section 18(1) of the 2014 Act (restriction of existing pension schemes) does not apply in relation to an existing scheme by virtue of provision made under section 18(5) or (5A) of the 2013 Act or section 18(5) or (6) of the 2014 Act (transitional protection under existing schemes); and (c) within the period of 5 years beginning with the final day of service in respect of which P is entitled to benefits under the existing scheme mentioned in sub-paragraph (b), P is appointed to a fee-paid judicial office.
S2181 (7)(6) In paragraph (6)— “the 2013 Act” is the Public Service Pensions Act 2013;
S2182 “the 2014 Act” is the Public Service Pensions Act (Northern Ireland) 2014;
S2183 “existing scheme” has the meaning given by section 18(2) of the Public Service Pensions Act 2013 and section 18(2) of the Public Service Pensions Act (Northern Ireland) 2014.
S2184 (8)(7)
S2185 For the purposes of this regulation, “the relevant date” is— (a) 2nd February 2013 where P only held a fee-paid judicial office whose jurisdiction is exercised exclusively in relation to Northern Ireland;
S2186 (b) 2nd December 2012 in all other cases.
S2187 Opted-out members and active members 9.—(1)
S2188 A member (“P”) is an “opted-out member” of the principal scheme on any day if P— (a) has, on or before that day, opted out of the principal scheme under regulation 10; and (b) has not opted back in under regulation 11.
S2189 (2) P is an “active member” of the principal scheme on any day if— (a) P is not an opted-out member;
S2190 (b) P holds aan eligible fee-paid judicial office on that day; and (c) if P had received a fee in P’s capacity as a holder of that office in respect of that day, the day would have been a qualifying fee-paid day.
S2191 Opting out 10.—(1)
S2192 A member who has not retired may opt out by sending a notice to the appropriate Minister.
S2193 (2) The notice— (a) must be in the form determined by the appropriate Minister, and (b) must specify the date on which the member wishes to opt out.
S2194 (3) The member opts out on the date specified under paragraph (2) or, if earlier, 3 months after the date on which the appropriate Minister receives the notice.
S2195 Opting back in 11.—(1)
S2196 A member (“P”) who has opted out and who has not retired may apply to the appropriate Minister, in the form determined by the appropriate Minister, to opt back in.
S2197 (2) The application must be accompanied by such evidence relating to P’s health as the appropriate Minister may require.
S2198 (3) P must submit to any medical examination as the appropriate Minister may require in connection with the application.
S2199 (4) If the appropriate Minister is satisfied that P is in good health, the appropriate Minister must allow the application.
S2200 (5) The appropriate Minister must, within the period of 3 months beginning with the date on which the application is received, send a notice to P specifying— (a) whether the appropriate Minister has allowed the application; and (b) the date on which the decision was made.
S2201 (6) The member opts back in on the date specified under paragraph (5)(b).
S2202 RETIREMENT BENEFITS Retirement aged 65 or over: entitlement to ordinary pension 12.—(1)
S2203 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day); and (b) on the day on which P retires, P— (i) has reached the age of 65, and (ii) has at least 5 years’ qualifying judicial service.
S2204 (2) On the pension start day, P becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S2205 (3) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day after that on which P retires; or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S2206 Annual rate of ordinary pension 13.—(1)
S2207 The annual rate of a pension to which a member (“P”) becomes entitled under regulation 12 is determined under paragraph (3), (5) or (7) (whichever applies).
S2208 (2) Paragraph (3) applies where, on retirement, P has reckonable service in only one eligible fee- paid judicial office (“the relevant office”).
S2209 (3) The annual rate is determined in accordance with the following formula— R  S 40 (a) R is P’s reckonable service in the relevant office;
S2210 (b) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before retirement.
S2211 (4) Paragraph (5) applies where— (a) on retirement, P has reckonable service in more than one eligible fee-paid judicial office;
S2212 and (b) P did not, at any time before retirement, hold more than one eligible fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S2213 (5) The annual rate is determined in accordance with the following formula— AR  S 40 (a) AR is the aggregate length of P’s reckonable service in eligible fee-paid judicial offices;
S2214 (b) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before retirement.
S2215 (6) Paragraph (7) applies where— (a) on retirement, P has reckonable service in more than one eligible fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant offices”); and (b) at any time before retirement, P held more than one eligible fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S2216 (7) The annual rate is determined by taking the following steps— Step 1 Determine the annual rate under paragraph (3), in relation to each relevant office, as if— (i) that office were the only office in which P had reckonable service on retirement (except in determining the maximum amount in relation to that office under regulation 5(3)); and (ii) P held that office (and held no other judicial office) immediately before retirement.
S2217 Step 2 Add together the rates found under Step 1.
S2218 Retirement between the ages of 60 and 65: entitlement to reduced pension 14.—(1)
S2219 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day); and (b) on the day on which P retires, P— (i) has reached the age of 60 but not the age of 65, and (ii) has at least 5 years’ qualifying judicial service.
S2220 (2) On the pension start day, P becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S2221 (3) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day after that on which P retires; or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S2222 Retirement under age of 60 on removal from judicial office: entitlement to reduced pension 15.—(1)
S2223 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day) by virtue of having been removed from all judicial offices held by P;
S2224 (b) on the day on which P retires, P has not reached the age of 60; and (c) the appropriate Minister recommends that P’s accrued rights under the principal scheme be given effect.
S2225 (2) The administrators must notify P of the recommendation under paragraph (1)(c).
S2226 (3) If P elects in writing to the administrators within 3 months of receiving the notice that P wishes to receive benefits under this regulation— (a) P becomes entitled, on the day on which the administrators receive the election, to the payment for life of a pension; and (b) P also becomes entitled, on that day, to the amount P would have been entitled to if P had been entitled under this regulation to the payment of a pension in respect of the period beginning with the pension start day and ending with the day before the day mentioned in sub-paragraph (a).
S2227 (4) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day after that on which P retires; or (b) if later, the commencement day.

S2267 Retirement under the age of 60: entitlement to preserved pension 17.—(1)
S2268 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day);
S2269 (b) if P retires by virtue of having been removed from all judicial offices held by P— (i) the appropriate Minister makes a recommendation under regulation 15(1)(c), and (ii) P does not make an election under regulation 15(3); and (c) on the day on which P retires, P— (i) has not reached the age of 60, and (ii) has at least 2 years’ qualifying judicial service.
S2270 (2) On the pension start day, P becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S2271 (3) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day on which P reaches normal pension age; or (b) if later, the commencement day.

S2284 Retirement aged 65 or over due to ill health: entitlement to ill health ordinary pension 19.—(1)
S2285 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day);
S2286 (b) on the day on which P retires, P— (i) has reached the age of 65; and (ii) has less than 5 years’ qualifying judicial service; and (c) the ill-health certification condition is met in relation to P.
S2287 (2) On the pension start day, P becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S2288 (3) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day after that on which P retires, or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S2289 Annual rate of ill health ordinary pension 20.

S2291 Retirement under 65 due to ill health: entitlement to ill health enhanced pension 21.—(1)
S2292 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day);
S2293 (b) on the day on which P retires, P has not reached normal pension age; and (c) the ill-health certification condition is met in relation to P.
S2294 (2) On the pension start day, P becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S2295 (3) In this regulation “the pension start day” means— (a) the day after that on which P retires, or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S2296 (4) Where, apart from this paragraph, P would be entitled to— (a) a pension under this regulation, and (b) a pension under— (i) regulation 14 (retirement between the ages of 60 and 65: entitlement to reduced pension), (ii) regulation 15 (retirement under the age of 60 due to removal from judicial office:
S2297 entitlement to reduced pension), or (iii) regulation 17 (retirement under the age of 60: entitlement to preserved pension), P is not entitled to the pension mentioned in sub-paragraph (b).
