S2307 (5) The annual rate is determined in accordance with the following formula— AR U  S  40 T (a) AR is the aggregate length of P’s reckonable service in eligible fee-paid judicial offices;
S2308 (b) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before retirement (“the relevant office”);;
S2309 (c) U is the smaller of— (i) the amount equal to T  E, and (ii) the maximum amount for the last eligible fee-paid judicial office in which P accrued reckonable service (“the relevant office,”), determined in accordance with regulation 5(3);
S2310 (d) T is the smaller of— (i) the length of P’s relevant judicial appointment (see paragraph (8)); and (ii) the maximum amount for the relevant office, determined in accordance with regulation 5(3);
S2311 (e) E is the smaller of— (i) the service enhancement (see paragraph (9)); and (ii) the amount determined in accordance with the formula 20  T.
S2312 (6) Paragraph (7) applies where— (a) on retirement P has reckonable service in more than one eligible fee-paid judicial office, and (b) at any time before retirement, P held more than one eligible fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S2313 (7) The annual rate is determined by taking the following steps.
S2314 Step 1 Determine the annual rate under this regulation, in relation to each relevant office, as if— (i) that office were the only office in which P had reckonable service on retirement (except in determining the maximum amount in relation to that office under regulation 5(3)), and (ii) P held that office (and held no other judicial office) immediately before retirement.
S2315 Step 2 Add together the rates found under Step 1.
S2316 (8) The length of P’s relevant judicial appointment is the period, or the aggregate of the periods, expressed in years and any fraction of a year, during which P held one or more eligible fee-paid judicial offices, disregarding any day in respect of which the conditions in regulation 4(2) (qualifying fee-paid days) are not met.
S2317 (9) The service enhancement is the period, expressed in years and any fraction of a year, which is one half of the period— (a) beginning with the day after that on which P retires, and (b) ending with the day on which P reaches normal pension age.
S2318 Ill-health certification condition 23.—(1)
S2319 The ill-health certification condition is met in relation to a member (“P”) if the appropriate Minister— (a) has received a medical certificate stating that P has suffered a permanent breakdown in health that makes P incapable of discharging the duties of the judicial office or offices which P held immediately before retirement;
S2320 (b) is satisfied as to the matters contained in that certificate; and (c) has, where he or she is the Lord Chancellor, consulted— (i) the Lord Chief Justice of England and Wales
S2321 (if P held a judicial office in England and Wales);
S2322 (ii) the Lord Chief Justice of Northern Ireland (if P held a judicial office in Northern Ireland).
S2323 (2) For the purposes of this regulation P’s breakdown in health is “permanent” if it is likely to continue until— (a) the day on which P reaches the age of 65; or (b) if later, the day on which, if P had not retired, P would have completed 5 years’
S2324 qualifying judicial service.
S2325 (3) The Lord Chief Justice of England and Wales may nominate a judicial office holder (as defined in section 109(4) of the Constitutional Reform Act 2005(a)) to exercise any of his or her functions under this regulation.
S2326 (4) The Lord Chief Justice of Northern Ireland may nominate— (a) the holder of one of the offices listed in Schedule 1 to the Justice (Northern Ireland) Act 2002(b);
S2327 (b) a Lord Justice of Appeal (as defined in section 88 of that Act), to exercise any of his or her functions under this regulation.
S2328 Times at which pension under this Part is payable 24.
S2329 A pension under this Part is payable at such intervals, not exceeding 3 months, as the Treasury may determine.
S2330 Lump sum: entitlement and amount 25.—(1)
S2331 This regulation applies where a member (“P”) retires on or after the commencement day, and becomes entitled to the payment of a pension under this Part.
S2332 (2) At the time P becomes entitled to the payment of the pension, P becomes entitled to a lump sum.
S2333 (3) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula— A .2 25 where A is the annual rate of the pension payable to P under this Part.
S2334 (a) 2005 c.4.
S2335 (b) 2002 c.26.
S2336 Multiple retirements 26.—(1)
S2337 This regulation applies where a member (“P”) retires and subsequently resumes judicial office.
S2338 (2) The resumption of judicial office does not affect the entitlement of P to any payment under the principal scheme in respect of any period before the resumption.
S2339 (3) No amounts are payable to or in respect of P under the principal scheme as a result of the retirement mentioned in paragraph (1) in respect of any period after the resumption.
S2340 (4) On P’s retirement after resuming judicial office— (a) P’s entitlement (and that of any other person) to any payment under the principal scheme;
S2341 and (b) the rate or amount of any such payment, are (subject to paragraph (5)) to be determined as if no amounts had previously been paid under the principal scheme as a result of the retirement mentioned in paragraph (1).
S2342 (5) A person to whom a lump sum is paid under the principal scheme as a result of the retirement mentioned in paragraph (1) is not required to refund the lump sum; but if the whole or any part of it is not refunded, an amount equal to so much of it as has not been refunded is to be deducted from any lump sum which subsequently becomes payable to or in respect of that person under the principal scheme.
S2343 PARTIAL RETIREMENT Option to take partial retirement in relation to a fee-paid judicial office 27.—(1)
S2344 Paragraph (2) applies where a member (“P”) at any time on or after the commencement day ceases to hold aan eligible fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant office”) and— (a) P has reckonable service in relation to the relevant office;
S2345 (b) at the time P ceases to hold the relevant office, P does not hold any other eligible fee-paid judicial office;
S2346 (c) at the time P ceases to hold the relevant office, P has at least 2 years’ qualifying judicial service; and (d) immediately after ceasing to hold the relevant office, P is appointed to another judicial office.
S2347 (2) P may take partial retirement in relation to the relevant office by giving notice in writing to the appropriate Minister.
S2348 (3) The notice— (a) must be in the form determined by the appropriate Minister, and (b) must be received by the appropriate Minister within the period of 6 months beginning with the day on which P ceases to hold the relevant office.
S2349 (4) A notice under this regulation— (a) has effect on the day on which it is received by the appropriate Minister, and (b) is irrevocable.
S2350 Effect of partial retirement 28.—(1) Where a member (“P”) takes partial retirement in relation to aan eligible fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant office”), P is treated for the purposes of the relevant provisions as if P had retired on the day on which P ceased to hold the relevant office.
S2351 (2) In this regulation “the relevant provisions” means— (a) Part 3 (retirement benefits) apart from regulation 26 (multiple retirements), and (b) Part 6 (death benefits).
S2352 (3) In the application of the relevant provisions in relation to P’s deemed retirement under paragraph (1), P is treated as having reckonable service only in the relevant office.
S2353 (4) The application of the relevant provisions in relation to P’s deemed retirement under paragraph (1) (and P’s pension under Part 3 in respect of that deemed retirement) does not prevent their application in relation to P’s actual retirement (and P’s pension under Part 3 in respect of that retirement).
S2354 (5) In the application of the relevant provisions in relation to P’s actual retirement, P is treated as having no reckonable service in the relevant office.
S2355 BENEFITS FOR PERSONS ENTITLED TO PENSION CREDITS Application of Part and interpretation 29.—(1)
S2356 This Part applies where— (a) a person (“the transferee”) becomes entitled to a pension credit under section 29(1)(b) of the Welfare Reform and Pensions Act 1999(a)
S2357 (“the 1999 Act”) or under Article 26(1)(b) of the Welfare Reform and Pensions (Northern Ireland)
S2358 Order 1999(b) (“the 1999 Order”); and (b) that pension credit is derived from the rights of another person (“P”) under the principal scheme.
S2359 (2) In this Part “the transferee”, “the 1999 Act”, “the 1999 Order” and “P” have the meanings given by paragraph (1).
S2360 Pension credit derived from the principal scheme: entitlement to pension 30.—(1)
S2361 On the pension start day the transferee becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S2362 (2) In paragraph (1) “the pension start day” means— (a) the day on which the transferee reaches normal pension age, or (b) if later, the day after the last day of the implementation period as determined in accordance with section 34 of the 1999 Act or Article 31 of the 1999 Order.
S2363 Annual rate of pension under regulation 30 31.—(1)
S2364 The annual rate of the pension to which the transferee is entitled under regulation 30 is the rate in relation to which the following condition is met.
S2365 (2) The condition is that the sum of— (a) the value of the pension, and (b) the value of any lump sum to which the transferee is entitled under this Part, equals the amount of the pension credit.
S2366 (3) For this purpose, the value of the pension and of any lump sum must be calculated— (a) 1999 c.30.
S2367 (b) S.I.1999/3147 (N.I.11)
S2368 (a) in accordance with regulations made by the Secretary of State under paragraph 5(b) of Schedule 5 to the 1999 Act, or (b) (in any case where the 1999 Order applies) in accordance with regulations made by the Department for Communities under paragraph 5(b) of Schedule 5 to the 1999 Order, and any regulations made by the Secretary of State or by the Department for Communities for the purposes of those provisions apply accordingly.
S2369 (4) The annual rate of the pension must be calculated in accordance with guidance issued by the appropriate Minister.
S2370 (5) Before issuing guidance under paragraph (4), the appropriate Minister must consult the Government Actuary.
S2371 Lump sum for transferee at age 65 32.—(1)
S2372 This regulation applies if the transferee becomes entitled to the pension credit before P becomes entitled to a lump sum under Part 3.
S2373 (2) At the time the transferee becomes entitled to a pension under regulation 30, the transferee becomes entitled to a lump sum.
S2374 (3) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula— A .2 25 where A is the annual rate of the pension to which the transferee is entitled under this Part.
S2375 Lump sum where transferee dies before age 65 33.—(1)
S2376 This regulation applies if the transferee dies before reaching the normal pension age.
S2377 (2) On the day after that on which the transferee dies the relevant person becomes entitled to a lump sum.
S2378 (3) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula— A .2 25 where A is the annual rate of the pension to which the transferee would have been entitled under regulation 30 if the transferee had become entitled to a pension under that regulation on the day on which the transferee died.
S2379 (4) In paragraph (2) “the relevant person” means— (a) the person nominated by the transferee for the purpose of this regulation by notice in writing to the administrators; or (b) in default of such nomination, the transferee’s personal representatives.
S2380 Exclusions 34.
S2381 The appropriate Minister has no power to accept for the benefit of a member of the principal scheme— (a) a payment under paragraph 1(3) of Schedule 5 to the 1999 Act or paragraph 1(3) of Schedule 5 to the 1999 Order, (b) a payment under section 95 of the Pension Schemes Act 1993(a) or section 91 of the Pensions Schemes (Northern Ireland) Act 1993(b), or (c) any other payment,
S2382 DEATH BENEFITS Interpretation 35.—(1)
S2383 In these Regulations “surviving adult”, in relation to a deceased member (“P”), means P’s surviving spouse or surviving civil partner.
S2384 (2) In these Regulations “surviving spouse”, in relation to P, means a person who— (a) was married to P on the date of P’s death, and (b) if P retired before death, entered into the marriage before P retired.
S2385 (3) In these Regulations “surviving civil partner”, in relation to P, means a person who— (a) was in a civil partnership with P on the date of P’s death, and (b) if P retired before death, entered into the civil partnership before P retired.
S2386 (4) In these Regulations “surviving adult’s pension” means a pension payable to a surviving adult under this Part.
S2387 Entitlement to a surviving adult’s pension 36.—(1)
S2388 This regulation applies where a member (“P”) dies (whether before, on or after the commencement day) leaving a surviving adult.
S2389 (2) On the pension start day, the surviving adult becomes entitled to the payment for life of a pension.
S2390 (3) For the purposes of paragraph (2) the “pension start day” means— (a) the day after the day on which P dies; or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S2391 (4) Where the surviving adult marries or enters into a civil partnership, the Treasury may, at any time, direct that payment of the pension be withheld.
S2392 (5) The Treasury may, at any time, direct that payment of a pension withheld under paragraph (4) be resumed.
S2393 Annual rate of surviving adult’s pension 37.—(1)
S2394 The annual rate of a pension to which a person becomes entitled under regulation 36 is equal to one half of the rate of the pension of the deceased member (“P”).
S2395 (2) For the purposes of this regulation the rate of P’s pension is determined as follows.
S2396 (3) Where P dies on or after the commencement day after having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P was entitled under Part 3 on the day on which P died.
S2397 (4) Where P dies on or after the commencement day without having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the day after that on which P died if— (a) on the day on which P died, P had not died but had instead retired, and (b) the ill-health certification condition were met in relation to P.
S2398 (5) Where P dies before the commencement day after having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the commencement day if P was still alive on that day.
S2399 (6) Where P dies before the commencement day without having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the commencement day if— (a) on the day on which P died, P had not died but had instead retired, (b) the ill-health certification condition were met in relation to P, and (c) P were still alive on the commencement day.
S2400 Annual rate of surviving adult’s pension: special provision for surviving civil partners 38.—(1)
S2401 Paragraph (2) applies where— (a) the surviving civil partner in relation to a member (“P”) becomes entitled to a pension under regulation 36;
S2402 (b) P held a judicial office before, and on, 5th December 2005; and (c) P elects in writing to the administrators, within the period of 6 months beginning with the relevant day, that the annual rate of the surviving civil partner’s pension under regulation 37 should be calculated as if P first held a judicial office on 5th December 2005.
S2403 (2) The rate of P’s pension, for the purposes of regulation 37, is to be determined on the basis that P first held a judicial office on 5th December 2005.
S2404 (3) In this regulation “the relevant day” means— (a) the commencement day, or (b) if later, the day on which the civil partnership is entered into.
S2405 (4) An election under this regulation is irrevocable.
S2406 Payment of a surviving adult’s pension 39.
S2407 A surviving adult’s pension is payable at such intervals, not exceeding 3 months, as the Treasury may determine.
S2408 Meaning of “eligible child”
S2409 40.—(1)
S2410 In these Regulations, “eligible child”, in relation to a deceased member, means a person who meets the age condition or the occupation condition (see regulation 41) and who is— (a) a natural child of the deceased member, (b) a person who was adopted by the deceased member before the deceased member retired or, where paragraph (2) applies, after the deceased member retired, (c) a person who is a child of the deceased member by virtue of a parental order made before the deceased member retired or, where paragraph (2) applies, after the deceased member retired, or (d) a step-child of the deceased member.
S2411 (2) This paragraph applies where the Treasury have issued a direction to the effect that they are satisfied that— (a) the deceased member had, before the deceased member retired, formed the intention of adopting, or applying for a parental order in respect of, the person; and (b) immediately before the deceased member retired, the person was wholly or mainly dependent on the deceased member.
S2412 (3) In a case where the deceased member died while holding an eligible fee-paid judicial office, the references in subsections (1)(b) and (2)(b) to things done before the deceased member’s retirement are to be read as references to things done before the deceased member’s death.
S2413 (4) For the purposes of this regulation a person (“C”) is a “natural child” of person (“P”) if P is C’s genetic father or mother (including anyone who is to be treated as C’s father, mother or parent under Part 2 of the Human Fertilisation and Embryology Act 2008(a)
S2414 (“the 2008 Act”), but not including anyone who is not to be so treated under that Part of that Act).
S2415 (a) 2008 c.22.
S2416 (5) For the purposes of this regulation a person (“C”) is a “step-child” of a person (“P”) if C— (a) is not a natural child of P, adopted by P or a child of P by virtue of a parental order, and (b) meets any of the following conditions.
S2417 (6) The first condition is that C— (a) is a natural child of a person who was at any time the spouse or civil partner of P, and (b) at the time the marriage or civil partnership was entered into, had been born or conceived.
S2418 (7) The second condition is that C— (a) was adopted by a person who was at any time the spouse or civil partner of P; and (b) was so adopted— (i) before the time the marriage or civil partnership was entered into, or (ii) after the time the marriage or civil partnership was entered into in a case where the adoption proceedings were pending at the time of the marriage or civil partnership.
S2419 (8) The third condition is that C— (a) is, by virtue of a parental order, a child of a person who was at any time the spouse or civil partner of P; and (b) the parental order was made— (i) before the time the marriage or civil partnership was entered into, or (ii) after the time the marriage or civil partnership was entered into in a case where the parental order proceedings were pending at the time of the marriage or civil partnership.
S2420 (9) In this regulation a “parental order” means an order made under section 54 of the Human Fertilisation and Embryology Act 2008.
S2421 Meaning of “eligible child”: the age condition and the occupation condition 41.—(1)
S2422 The age condition is that the person has not reached the age of 16.
S2423 (2) The occupation condition is that the person (“C”) has, at all times since reaching the age of 16, been— (a) in full-time education, or (b) undergoing eligible training for a trade, profession or vocation.
S2424 (3) For the purposes of this regulation training is “eligible” if— (a) it is full-time;
S2425 (b) its duration is at least 2 years; and (c) the rate of pay (if any) receivable by C, or payable by the employer in respect of C, in relation to the training does not exceed the specified maximum.
S2426 (4) In this regulation— “pay” means any salary, fees, wages, perquisites, profits or gains and includes the value of any free board, lodging or clothing;
S2427 “the specified maximum” at any time means an annual rate equal to that at which a pension of £250 a year— (a) first awarded under the Principal Civil Service Pension Scheme on 1st June 1972; and (b) increased from time to time by the amount of increase that would be applied under the 1971 Act to such a pension, would (as so increased) be payable at that time.
S2428 (5) Where a premium has been paid in respect of C’s training, all emoluments receivable by C, or payable by the employer in respect of C, are to be taken, for the purposes of paragraph (3)(c), as receivable or payable by way of return of the premium, unless and to the extent that the amount of those emoluments exceeds the amount of the premium.
S2429 (6) Where the Treasury are satisfied that C’s full-time education ought not be regarded as completed, the Treasury may direct that any period during which a person is not in full-time education and is not undergoing eligible training for a trade, profession or vocation, is to be disregarded for the purposes of this regulation.
S2430 Entitlement to an eligible child’s pension 42.—(1)
S2431 This regulation applies if a member (“P”) dies (whether before, on or after the commencement day) leaving one or more eligible children.
S2432 (2) On the pension start day, and until such time as there are no more eligible children, the relevant person becomes entitled to the payment of a pension.
S2433 (3) In this regulation, the “pension start day” means— (a) the day after the day on which P dies; or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S2434 (4) In this regulation, “the relevant person” means such person or persons as the Treasury may from time to time direct.
S2435 Annual rate of eligible child’s pension 43.—(1)
S2436 The annual rate of a pension to which a person becomes entitled under regulation 42, where P dies without leaving a surviving adult, is— (a) in relation to any period during which there is only one eligible child, an annual rate equal to one third of the rate of P’s pension;
S2437 (b) in relation to any period during which there are two or more eligible children, an annual rate equal to two thirds of the rate of P’s pension.
S2438 (2) The annual rate of a pension to which a person becomes entitled under regulation 42, where P dies leaving a surviving adult, is— (a) in relation to any period during which there is only one eligible child and the surviving adult is still alive, an annual rate equal to one quarter of the rate of P’s pension;
S2439 (b) in relation to any period during which there is only one eligible child and the surviving adult is dead, an annual rate equal to one third of the rate of P’s pension;
S2440 (c) in relation to any period during which there are two or more eligible children and the surviving adult is still alive, an annual rate equal to one half of the rate of P’s pension;
S2441 (d) in relation to any period during which there are two or more eligible children and the surviving adult is dead, an annual rate equal to two thirds of the rate of P’s pension.
S2442 (3) The Treasury may direct, where the deceased member leaves a surviving adult who marries or enters into a civil partnership, that paragraph (1) is to apply instead of paragraph (2) in respect of any period during which the surviving adult has a spouse or a civil partner.
S2443 (4) The Treasury may, at any time, cancel a direction given under paragraph (3).
S2444 (5) For the purposes of this regulation the “rate of P’s pension” is determined as follows.
S2445 (6) Where P dies on or after the commencement day after having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P was entitled under Part 3 on the day on which P died.
S2446 (7) Where P dies on or after the commencement day without having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the day after that on which P died if— (a) on the day on which P died, P had not died but had instead retired, and (b) the ill-health certification condition were met in relation to P.
S2447 (8) Where P dies before the commencement day after having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the commencement day if P was still alive on that day.
S2448 (9) Where P dies before the commencement day without having retired, the rate of P’s pension is the annual rate of the pension to which P would have become entitled under Part 3 on the commencement day if— (a) on the day on which P died, P had not died but had instead retired, (b) the ill-health certification condition were met in relation to P, and (c) P had survived until the commencement day.
S2449 Payment and application of an eligible child’s pension 44.—(1)
S2450 A pension to which a person is entitled under regulation 42 is payable at such intervals, not exceeding 3 months, as the Treasury may determine.
S2451 (2) A person to whom a pension or part of a pension under regulation 42 is paid must apply the pension for the benefit of— (a) all eligible children of the deceased member; or (b) such eligible children of the deceased member as the Treasury may direct.
S2452 Lump sum on death: death in service 45.—(1)
S2453 Paragraph (2) applies where— (a) a member (“P”) dies (on or after the commencement day) without having retired; and (b) P is (immediately before death) an active member of the principal scheme.
S2454 (2) On the day after that on which P dies, the relevant person becomes entitled to a lump sum.
S2455 (3) The amount of the lump sum to which P becomes entitled under paragraph (2) is determined under paragraph (5), (7) or (9), whichever applies).
S2456 (4) Paragraph (5) applies where, on P’s death, P has reckonable service in only one eligible fee- paid judicial office (“the relevant office”).
S2457 (5) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula— M  N S 2  JS (a) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before P’s death, determined as if P had retired on the date of P’s death;
S2458 (b) M is P’s qualifying fee-paid service in the relevant office; and (c) N is the annual divisor for that office specified in column 2 of the Schedule;
S2459 (d) JS is P’s qualifying judicial service, expressed in years and any fraction of a year.
S2460 (6) Paragraph (7) applies where— (a) on P’s death, P has reckonable service in more than one eligible fee-paid judicial office;
S2461 and (b) P did not, at any time before death, hold more than one eligible fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S2462 (7) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula— AM
S2463  N S
S2464 2  JS (a) S is the appropriate annual salary of the judicial office held by P immediately before P’s death, determined as if P had retired on the date of P’s death;
S2465 (b) AM is the aggregate length of P’s qualifying fee-paid service in eligible fee-paid judicial offices;
S2466 (c) JS is the lower of P’s qualifying judicial service, expressed in years and any fraction of a year.
S2467 (8) Paragraph (9) applies where— (a) immediately before death P held more than one eligible fee-paid judicial office, and (b) at any time before retirement, P held more than one eligible fee-paid judicial office simultaneously.
S2468 (9) The amount of the lump sum is determined by taking the following steps.
S2469 Step 1 Determine the lump sum under this regulation in relation to each eligible fee-paid judicial office, as if that office were the only eligible fee-paid relevant office held by P.
S2470 Step 2 Add together the lump sums found under Step 1.
S2471 (10)
S2472 In this regulation “the relevant person” means— (a) the person nominated by P for the purpose of this regulation by notice in writing to the administrators; or (b) in default of such nomination, P’s personal representatives.
S2473 Lump sum on death: death after retirement of member with preserved pension 46.—(1)
S2474 Paragraph (2) applies if— (a) a member (“P”) dies (on or after the commencement day) having retired (whether before, on or after the commencement day);
S2475 (b) where P retires on or after the commencement day, P is not, on retirement, an opted-out member; and (c) if P had not died P would, on reaching normal pension age have become entitled to a pension under regulation 17 (retirement under the age of 60: entitlement to preserved pension).
S2476 (2) On the day after that on which P dies, P’s personal representatives become entitled to a lump sum.
S2477 (3) The amount of the lump sum is to be determined in accordance with the following formula— A .2 25 where A is a notional annual rate of pension determined in accordance with regulation 18 as though P had reached normal pension age on the day P died.
S2478 Lump sum on death: death soon after retirement of member entitled to pension 47.—(1)
S2479 Paragraph (2) applies if— (a) a member (“P”) dies (on or after the commencement day) having retired (whether before, on or after the commencement day);
S2480 (b) where P retires on or after the commencement day, P is not, on retirement, an opted-out member;
S2481 (c) immediately before death, P was entitled to the payment of a pension under Part 3 (retirement benefits); and (d) the total benefits received are less than the minimum benefits amount.
S2482 (2) On the day after that on which P dies, P’s personal representatives become entitled to a lump sum equal to the difference between the total benefits received and the minimum benefits amount.
S2483 (3) In this regulation “the total benefits received” means the amount determined in accordance with the following formula—
S2484 (a) A is the interim payments amount (if any) in relation to P;
S2485 (b) B is the amount (if any) paid or payable to P under regulation 50 (payment of benefits in relation to the pre-commencement period);
S2486 (c) C is the total of the sums paid or payable to P under Part 3 on account of the pension (including any increases under the 1971 Act), and by way of lump sum, in relation to the period after commencement day;
S2487 (d) D is the amount (if any) paid or payable by P under regulation 51 (repayment of any excess interim payments).
S2488 (4) In this regulation “the minimum benefits amount” means the amount determined in accordance with the following formula— E  5 where E is the annual rate of the pension payable to P under Part 3 immediately before P’s death.
S2489 BENEFITS IN RESPECT OF PERIODS BEFORE COMMENCEMENT Application of Part 48.—(1)
S2490 This Part applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retired, or died without having retired, before the commencement day, and (b) condition A or B is met.
S2491 (2) Condition A is that amounts were paid to any person during the pre-commencement period in respect of P’s retirement or death under arrangements made for the purpose of providing benefits during the pre-commencement period corresponding to those under Part 3 or 6.
S2492 (3) Condition B is that, if these Regulations had come into force on the day before the day on which P retired or died, amounts would have been payable during the pre-commencement period— (a) to P under Part 3, or (b) to another person under Part 6 in respect of P’s retirement or death.
S2493 (4) In this Part— (a) “the pre-commencement period” means the period— (i) beginning with the day on which P retired or on which P died without having retired, and (ii) ending with the day before the commencement day.
S2494 (b) references to “P” are to a member mentioned in paragraph (1)(a).
S2495 Meaning of “the interim payments amount” and “the pre-commencement benefits amount”
S2496 49.—(1)
S2497 In these Regulations “the interim payments amount”, in relation to any person, means the total amount (if any) that was paid to that person under arrangements made for the purpose of providing benefits during the pre-commencement period in respect of P’s retirement or death corresponding to those under Part 3 or 6.
S2498 (2) In this Part “the pre-commencement benefits amount”, in relation to any person, means, subject to paragraph (3), the total amount (if any) that would have been payable to that person under Part 3 or 6 during the pre-commencement period in respect of P’s retirement or death if these Regulations had come into force on the day before the day on which P retired or died without having retired.
S2499 (3) Where— (a) the amount payable to a person in respect of P under paragraph (2) includes a lump sum payable under regulation 45 or 46, and (b) P would have been liable to pay the pre-commencement contributions amount referred to in regulation 53(1) if P had survived until the commencement day, the pre-commencement benefits amount payable to that person is to be calculated after deducting the pre-commencement contributions amount (within the meaning of regulation 53) which would have been payable by P if these Regulations had come into force on the day before the day on which P died.
S2500 Additional payment where no or insufficient interim payments made 50.—(1)
S2501 Paragraph (2) applies where the interim payments amount in relation to a person is less than the pre-commencement benefits amount in relation to that person.
S2502 (2) The administrators must pay to the person the amount of the difference between the two amounts mentioned in paragraph (1).
S2503 Repayment of any excess interim payments 51.—(1) Paragraph (2) applies where the interim payments amount in relation to a person is greater than the pre-commencement benefits amount in relation to that person.
S2504 (2) The person must pay to the administrators the amount of the difference between the two amounts mentioned in paragraph (1).
S2505 (3) Where the person is entitled to the payment of a pension under Part 3 or 6, the liability imposed by this regulation is to be discharged by such deductions from payments of the pension as the administrators may decide.
S2506 (4) Where the person is not entitled to the payment of a pension under Part 3 or 6, the liability imposed by this regulation may be recovered as a debt due to the appropriate Minister.
S2507 CONTRIBUTIONS FOR PRE-COMMENCEMENT SERVICE Interpretation 52.
S2508 In this Part— “fee period” means a period specified in column 1 of the Contributions Table;
S2509 “the pre-commencement contributions amount” has the meaning given by regulation 53(1);
S2510 “P” means a member;
S2511 “the Contributions Table” means the table set out in regulation 57(1).
S2512 Liability to pay pre-commencement contributions amount 53.—(1)
S2513 P must pay to the appropriate Minister an amount (“the pre-commencement contributions amount”), being the sum of— (a) the pre-commencement personal contributions amount (see regulation 54), and (b) the pre-commencement dependants’ contributions amount (see regulation 55).
S2514 (2) Paragraph (1) is subject to paragraphs (3) and (4).
S2515 (3) P is not required to pay the pre-commencement contributions amount if P— (a) retired before the commencement day, and (b) had on retirement less than two years qualifying judicial service.
S2516 (4) P is not required to pay so much of the pre-commencement contributions amount as is represented by the amount mentioned in paragraph (1)(b) if P— (a) retired before the commencement day, (b) was not married or in a civil partnership at any time during the period— (i) beginning with the day on which P first held aan eligible fee-paid judicial office, and (ii) ending with the day on which P retired; and (c) did not have a qualifying child at any time during that period.
S2517 Calculation of pre-commencement personal contributions amount 54.
S2518 The pre-commencement personal contributions amount in relation to P is determined as follows— Step 1 For each fee period, find the total of the fees paid to P in respect of qualifying fee-paid days falling within the period.
S2519 Step 2 Multiply the total found under Step 1 for each fee period by the percentage specified in relation to that fee period in column 2 of the Contributions Table.
S2520 Step 3 Add together the amounts found under Step 2.
S2521 Calculation of pre-commencement dependants’ contributions amount 55.
S2522 The pre-commencement dependants’ contributions amount in relation to P is determined as follows— Step 1 For each fee period, find the total of the fees paid to P in respect of qualifying fee-paid days falling within the period.
S2523 Step 2 Multiply the total found under Step 1 for each fee period by the percentage specified in relation to that fee period in column 3 of the Contributions Table.
S2524 Step 3 Add together the amounts found under Step 2.
S2525 Qualifying fee-paid days disregarded if they do not add to reckonable service 56.—(1)
S2526 A qualifying fee-paid day worked in aan eligible fee-paid judicial office is disregarded for the purposes of regulations 54 and 55 if P’s reckonable service in relation to that office would have been the maximum amount in relation to that office, had P retired on the day before that day.
S2527 (2) The reference in paragraph (1) to the maximum amount in relation to an office is to that amount as determined in accordance with regulation 5(3)
S2528 (reckonable service).
S2529 The Contributions Table (2) In the entry in that table for the fee period beginning on 1st April 2015, “X%” means the percentage specified in column 2 of the table in paragraph (5), in the row relating to P’s annualised fees for that period.
S2530 (3)
S2531 In the entry in that table for the fee period beginning on 1st April 2016 (“the final fee period”), Y% means the percentage specified in column 2 of the table in paragraph (6), in the row relating to P’s annualised fees for that period.
S2532 (4) P’s annualised fees for the final fee period are determined in accordance with the following formula— 365 F  N (a) F is the total of the fees paid to P in respect of qualifying fee-paid days in the final fee period;
S2533 (b) N is the number of days in that period.
S2534 Payment of pre-commencement contributions amount 58.—(1)
S2535 The pre-commencement contributions amount, or any part of it, may be paid— (a) by way of a lump sum at any time during the period of 6 months beginning with the commencement day;
S2536 7th April 2000 to 31st March 2012:

S2545 Contribution (personal): X%; Contribution (dependants): 1.80%.
S2546 1st April 2016 to the day before the commencement day:
S2547 Contribution (personal): Y%; Contribution (dependants): 1.80%.

S2562 (b) by way of such deductions from fees paid to P in respect of fee-paid days as may be specified in an agreement between P and the appropriate Minister, and for the purposes of paragraph (b), a “fee-paid” day means a day in respect of which P receives a fee in P’s capacity as a holder of aan eligible fee-paid judicial office.
S2563 (2) The deductions specified under paragraph (1)(b)— (a) must be of fixed monetary amounts, and (b) must be the same for each fee.
S2564 (3) A new agreement under paragraph (1)(b) may be entered into at any time after the end of the period of 12 months beginning with the day on which the previous agreement was entered into.
S2565 (4) Except to the extent that the pre-commencement contributions amount has already been paid under paragraph (1), it is to be paid by way of a deduction from— (a) the lump sum payable to P under Part 3, or (b) where P dies before retiring, the lump sum payable in respect of P under Part 6.
S2566 CONTRIBUTIONS FOR SERVICE ON OR AFTER COMMENCEMENT DAY Interpretation 59.
S2567 In this Part— “fee year” means the period beginning with 1st April in any year and ending with the 31st March in the following year, and “P” means a member.
S2568 Liability to pay contributions on fees in respect of qualifying fee-paid days 60.
S2569 P must pay a contribution to the appropriate Minister on any fee paid to P in respect of a qualifying fee-paid day where the day is, or is after, the commencement day.
S2570 Qualifying fee-paid days disregarded if they do not add to reckonable service 61.—(1)
S2571 Regulation 60 does not require P to pay a contribution in respect of a qualifying fee- paid day worked in aan eligible fee-paid judicial office if P’s reckonable service in relation to that office would have been the maximum amount in relation to that office, had P retired on the day before that day.
S2572 (2) The reference in paragraph (1) to the maximum amount in relation to an office is to that amount as determined in accordance with regulation 5(3)
S2573 (reckonable service).
S2574 Amount of contribution 62.—(1)
S2575 The amount of the contribution payable on a fee under regulation 60 is the sum of— (a) the personal benefits contribution, and (b) the dependants’ benefits contribution.
S2576 (2) The amount of the personal benefits contribution is the amount of the fee multiplied by the percentage specified in column 2 of the table in regulation 63 for the relevant fee year, in the row relating to P’s annualised fees for that year.
S2577 (3) The amount of the dependants’ benefits contribution is the amount of the fee multiplied by the percentage specified in column 3 of the same row of the same table.
S2578 (4) P’s annualised fees for the relevant fee year are determined in accordance with the following formula— 365 F  N (a) F is the amount of the fee;
S2579 (b) N is the number of days in the applicable payment period.
S2580 (5) For the purposes of paragraph (4) the “applicable payment period” means— (a) where the qualifying fee-paid day in respect of which the fee is paid is the first qualifying fee-paid day to occur in the relevant fee year, the period— (i) beginning with the first day of the relevant fee year, and (ii) ending with the day before the qualifying fee-paid day;
S2581 (b) where the qualifying fee-paid day in respect of which the fee is paid is the second, or a later, qualifying fee-paid day to occur in the relevant fee year, the period— (i) beginning with the most recent previous qualifying fee-paid day, and (ii) ending with the day before the qualifying fee-paid day.
S2582 (6) If the relevant fee year is a leap year, the formula in paragraph (4) has effect as if for “365” there were substituted “366”.
S2583 (7) In this regulation “the relevant fee year” means the fee year in which the qualifying fee-paid day in respect of which the fee is paid falls.
S2584 Contribution rates for fee years Payment of contribution 64.
S2585 A contribution payable under this Part is to be paid by way of deduction from the fee to which it relates.
S2586 Up to but not including £15,001: Contribution (personal): 0.96%; Contribution (dependants’): 1.80%.
S2587 £15,001 to but not including £21,423: Contribution (personal): 0.96%; Contribution (dependants’): 1.80%.
S2588 £21,423 to but not including £51,006: Contribution (personal): 1.47%; Contribution (dependants’): 1.80%.
S2589 £51,006 to but not including £150,001:
S2590 Contribution (personal): 2.61%; Contribution (dependants’): 1.80%.
S2591 £150,001 and above: Contribution (personal): 4.43%; Contribution (dependants’): 1.80%.

S2599 REPAYMENT OF DEPENDANTS’ BENEFITS CONTRIBUTIONS Full repayment of dependants’ benefits contributions on retirement 65.—(1)
S2600 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires on or after the commencement day, and (b) conditions A and B are met.
S2601 (2) On the day after that on which P retires, P becomes entitled to a payment from the appropriate Minister.
S2602 (3) The amount of the payment under paragraph (2) is the sum of— (a) the amount previously paid by P under Part 8 to the extent that it consists of the pre- commencement dependants’ contributions amount; and (b) the total of the contributions previously paid by P under Part 9 to the extent that they represent dependants’ benefits contributions.
S2603 (4) Condition A is that, on the day on which P retires— (a) P is not married and is not in a civil partnership; and (b) P does not have a qualifying child.
S2604 (5) Condition B is that, at all times since P first held aan eligible fee-paid judicial office (or if later, since 7th April 2000)— (a) P was not married and was not in a civil partnership, and (b) P did not have a qualifying child.
S2605 (6) In this Part— “the pre-commencement dependants’ contributions amount” means the amount determined under regulation 55;
S2606 “dependants’ benefits contribution” means an amount determined under regulation 62(3).
S2607 Partial repayment of dependants’ benefits contributions on retirement 66.—(1)
S2608 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) retires (whether before, on or after the commencement day), and (b) conditions A and B are met.
S2609 (2) On the relevant day, P becomes entitled to a payment from the appropriate Minister.
S2610 (3) In paragraph (2) “the relevant day” means— (a) the day after that on which P retires, or (b) if later, the commencement day.
S2611 (4) The amount of the payment under paragraph (2) is the sum of— (a) the amount previously paid by P under Part 8 which consists of so much of the pre- commencement dependants’ contributions amount as is attributable to qualifying fee-paid days falling after the specified date; and (b) the total of the contributions previously paid by P under Part 9 which represent dependants’ benefits contributions paid on fees in respect of qualifying fee-paid days falling after the specified date.
S2612 (5) Condition A is that, on the day on which P retires— (a) P is not married and is not in a civil partnership; and (b) P does not have a qualifying child.
S2613 (6) Condition B is that, on any day during the period beginning with the day on which P first held aan eligible fee-paid judicial office and ending with the day on which P retires— (a)
S2614 P was married or was in a civil partnership, or (b) P had a qualifying child.
S2615 (7) In this regulation “the specified date” means the last day in respect of which condition B is met.
S2616 Full repayment of dependants’ benefits contributions on partial retirement 67.—(1)
S2617 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) takes partial retirement in relation to aan eligible fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant office”), and (b) conditions A and B are met.
S2618 (2) On the day after that on which P takes partial retirement, P becomes entitled to a payment from the appropriate Minister.
S2619 (3) The amount of the payment under paragraph (2) is the sum of— (a) the amount previously paid by P under Part 8 which consists of so much of the pre- commencement dependants’ contributions amount as is attributable to qualifying fee-paid days worked in the relevant office; and (b) the total of the contributions previously paid by P under Part 9 which represent dependants’ benefits contributions paid on fees in respect of qualifying fee-paid days worked in the relevant office.
S2620 (4) Condition A is that, on the day on which P takes partial retirement— (a) P is not married and is not in a civil partnership; and (b) P does not have a qualifying child.
S2621 (5) Condition B is that, at all times since P first held the relevant office (or if later, since 7th April 2000)— (a) P was not married and was not in a civil partnership, and (b) P did not have a qualifying child.
S2622 Partial repayment of dependants’ benefits contributions on partial retirement 68.—(1)
S2623 This regulation applies where— (a) a member (“P”) takes partial retirement in relation to aan eligible fee-paid judicial office (“the relevant office”), and (b) conditions A and B are met.
S2624 (2) On the day after that on which P takes partial retirement, P becomes entitled to a payment from the appropriate Minister.
S2625 (3) The amount of the payment under paragraph (2) is the sum of— (a) the amount previously paid by P under Part 8 which consists of so much of the pre- commencement dependants’ contributions amount as is attributable to qualifying fee-paid days that— (i) were worked in the relevant office; and (ii) fell after the specified date; and (b) the total of the contributions previously paid by P under Part 9 to the extent that they consist of dependants’ benefits contributions and were paid on fees in respect of qualifying fee-paid days that— (i) were worked in the relevant office; and (ii) fell after the specified date.
S2626 (4) Condition A is that, in the day on which P takes partial retirement in relation to the relevant office— (a) P is not married and is not in a civil partnership; and (b) P does not have a qualifying child.
S2627 (5) Condition B is that, on any day during the period beginning with the day on which P first held the relevant office and ending with the day on which P takes partial retirement— (a) P was married or was in a civil partnership, or (b) P had a qualifying child.
S2628 (6) In this regulation “the specified date” means the last day in respect of which condition B is met.
S2629 Repayments under this Part to be paid with compound interest 69.
S2630 Where the appropriate Minister makes a payment to a member under this Part, it must be paid together with compound interest at the rate announced annually in relation to the Principal Civil Service Pension Scheme.
S2631 TRANSFERS CHAPTER 1 GENERAL Interpretation 70.—(1)
S2632 In this Part— “1995 Transfer Regulations” means the Judicial Pensions (Transfer of Accrued Benefits) Regulations 1995(a);
S2633 “authorised insurer” means— (a) a person who has permission under Part 4A of the Financial Services and Markets Act 2000 to effect or carry out contracts of long-term insurance, or (b) an EEA firm of the kind mentioned in paragraph 5(d) of Schedule 3 to that Act, which has permission under paragraph 15 of that Schedule (as a result of qualifying for authorisation under paragraph 12 of that Schedule) to effect or carry out contracts of long-term insurance;
S2634 “derivative benefit” has the meaning given in regulation 85;
S2635 “MLA” has the meaning given in regulation 77;
S2636 “normal pension age” means the earliest age at which, if a person’s qualifying judicial service had continued until retirement at that age, a member might have been entitled to receive a pension under the principal scheme, otherwise than by reason of meeting the ill-health certification condition;
S2637 “occupational pension scheme” has the meaning given by section 1 of the Pension Schemes Act 1993 or, in relation to Northern Ireland, section 1 of the Pension Schemes (Northern Ireland) Act 1993;
S2638 “the option” has the meaning given by regulation 74(1);
S2639 (a) S.I. 1995/637, amended by S.I. 2005/3325.
S2640 “personal pension scheme” has the meaning given by section 1 of the Pension Schemes Act 1993 or, in relation to Northern Ireland, section 1 of Pension Schemes (Northern Ireland) Act 1993;
S2641 “relevant date”— (a) when used in Chapter 2, has the meaning given by regulation 73;
S2642 (b) when used in Chapter 3, has the meaning given by regulation 81;
S2643 “relevant schemes” means the principal scheme, the FPJAYS and the JASAPS;
S2644 “transfer credit” has the meaning given by section 181(1) of the Pension Schemes Act 1993, or in relation to Northern Ireland, section 176(1) of the Pension Schemes (Northern Ireland) Act 1993.
S2645 (2) In this Part, references to numbered Tables are to the Tables in Schedule 2 to the 1995 Transfer Regulations.
S2646 (3) The definition of “authorised insurer” in paragraph (1) must be read with— (a) section 22 of the Financial Services and Markets Act 2000;
S2647 (b) any relevant order under that section;
S2648 (c) Schedule 2 to that Act.
S2649 Disapplication of other provisions about transfers 71.
S2650 Chapter 1 of Part 4ZA of the Pension Schemes Act 1993 (transfer rights: general) and Chapter 1 of Part 4ZA of the Pension Schemes (Northern Ireland) Act 1993 (transfer rights: general) do not apply in relation to the relevant schemes.
S2651 CHAPTER 2 TRANSFERS OUT Transfers out 72.—(1)
S2652 Where the conditions set out in paragraph (2) are met, regulations 73 to 81 apply to any member (“P”)— (a) to or in respect of whom benefits are payable under one or more of the relevant schemes;
S2653 and (b) the last qualifying fee-paid day which counts towards P’s reckonable service (“last day of reckonable service”) is on or after the commencement day.
S2654 (2) The conditions are that— (a) P’s age on 6th February 2013 was more than one year under normal pension age;
S2655 (b) P’s age on the date P exercises the option in regulation 74 is at least one year under normal pension age; and (c) on P’s last day of reckonable service— (i) P has accrued rights to benefits under the principal scheme; or (ii) P would have such rights if P’s qualifying judicial service had also ended on that date.
S2656 Qualifying member’s right to a transfer payment 73.—(1)
S2657 On P’s last day of reckonable service, P acquires a right to the cash equivalent at the relevant date of any benefits which have accrued to, or in respect of P, under the relevant schemes.
S2658 (2) In this regulation “the relevant date” means the date of any application which P has made under regulation 79 and which has not been withdrawn.
S2659 Method of taking cash benefit 74.—(1) P may only exercise the right in regulation 73(1) by exercising the option conferred by this paragraph (“the option”).
S2660 (2) The option is that of requiring the Treasury to use the cash equivalent in whichever of the following ways P chooses— (a) for acquiring transfer credits allowed under the rules of another occupational pension scheme— (i) whose trustees or managers are able and willing to accept the transfer; and (ii) which satisfies the requirements in regulation 75;
S2661 (b) for acquiring rights allowed under the rules of a personal pension scheme— (i) whose trustees or managers are able and willing to accept the transfer; and (ii) which satisfies the requirements in regulation 75;
S2662 (c) for purchasing one or more annuities satisfying the requirements in regulation 75 from one or more authorised insurers— (i) chosen by P, and (ii) willing to accept payment on P’s account from the Treasury;
S2663 (d) for subscribing to other pension arrangements which satisfy the requirements in regulation 75.
S2664 (3) P may exercise the option in different ways in relation to different portions of P’s cash equivalent.
S2665 (4) If P exercises the option P must do so in relation to the whole of P’s cash equivalent.
S2666 Requirements to be satisfied by schemes 75.
S2667 The requirements to be satisfied by an occupational pension scheme, personal pension scheme, annuity or other pension arrangement referred to in regulation 74(2) are that the scheme, annuity or arrangement is— (a) a registered pension scheme, or (b) a pension arrangement that is a qualifying recognised overseas pensions scheme, for the purposes of Part 4 (pension schemes etc) of the Finance Act 2004.
S2668 Calculation of cash equivalents 76.—(1)
S2669 The cash equivalent of accrued benefits is to be calculated in accordance with the formula— (a) MLA   p 
S2670 PF  I  L  w  SF   2  g 78  g 88   GMP  where P is married or a civil partner at the date of leaving the relevant schemes; or (b) MLA   p 
S2671 PF  I  L   g 78  2  g 88
S2672   SF   2  g 78  g 88   GMP  where P is male and neither married nor a civil partner at the date of leaving the relevant schemes;
S2673 (c) MLA   p 
S2674 PF  I  L  g 88  SF   2  g 78  g 88   GMP  where P is female and neither married nor a civil partner at the date of leaving the relevant schemes.
S2675 (2) For the purpose of the calculations in paragraph (1)— (a) p is the annual rate of the pension to which P would be entitled under these Regulations;
S2676 (b) PF is the pension factor set out in Table 3 applicable to P’s age last birthday as at the relevant date;
S2677 (c) I is the accrued lump sum under the relevant schemes of P (less any deductions in respect of unpaid contributions payable under Part 9 of these Regulations, or any other sum payable under these Regulations);
S2678 (d) L is the lump sum factor set out in Table 3 applicable to P’s age last birthday as at the relevant date;
S2679 (e) w is the annual rate of the surviving adult’s pension under the relevant schemes;
S2680 (f) SF is the spouse’s or civil partner’s factor applicable to P’s age last birthday as at the relevant date set out under the heading— (i) WM in Table 3 where P is married or a civil partner at the date of leaving the relevant schemes;
S2681 (ii) GSM in Table 3 where P is male and neither married nor a civil partner at the date of leaving the relevant schemes;
S2682 (iii) GSF in Table 3 where P is female and neither married nor a civil partner at the date of leaving the relevant schemes;
S2683 (g) g78 is the guaranteed minimum pension per annum accrued prior to 6th April 1988, together with any increases under section 148 of the Social Security Administration Act 1992 or section 130 of the Social Security Administration (Northern Ireland) Act 1992 in the period between leaving the relevant scheme and the relevant date;
S2684 (h) g88 is the guaranteed minimum pension per annum accrued on or after 6th April 1988, together with any increases under section 148 of the Social Security Administration Act 1992 or section 130 of the Social Security Administration (Northern Ireland) Act 1992 in the period between leaving the relevant scheme and the relevant date;
S2685 (i) GMP is the guaranteed minimum pension factor applicable to P’s age last birthday as at the relevant date set out in the column under the heading— (i) GM in Table 5 where P is male; and (ii) GF in Table 5 where P is female.
S2686 Calculation of market level adjustment 77.—(1)
S2687 For the purposes of regulations 76 and 87 the market level adjustment figure (“MLA”) is to be calculated in accordance with the formula— A  B   1  A C  rounded up to four decimal places, where— (a) A is the decimal part of the yield on index-linked stocks;
S2688 (b) B is the adjustment factor set out in Table 1 in the row relating to P’s age last birthday as at the relevant date and in the column headed by the full percentage figure immediately above the percentage figure for the yield on index-linked stocks; and (c) C is the adjustment factor set out in Table 1 which is in the row relating to P’s age last birthday as at the relevant date and which appears in the column headed by the full percentage figure which is equal to or immediately below the percentage figure for the yield on index linked stocks.
S2689 (2) In this regulation “the yield on index-linked stocks” means the real yield to redemption on the Financial Times-Actuaries Index of index-linked Government securities with 5 or more years to redemption assuming 5% inflation, in respect of the first working day of the month in which the relevant date falls as published in the Financial Times.
S2690 Time within which option must be exercised 78.—(1) P may only exercise the option on or before the last option date.
S2691 (2) The last option date is the later of— (a) the date which falls one year before the date on which P reaches normal pension age; or (b) the end of the period of 6 months beginning with the day after P’s last day of reckonable service.
S2692 (3) P loses the right to any cash equivalent under this Chapter if— (a) P’s pension under these Regulations becomes payable before P reaches normal pension age; or (b) P fails to exercise the option on or before the last option date.
S2693 Option to be exercised in writing 79.—(1)
S2694 P may only exercise the option by making an application in writing to the Treasury.
S2695 (2) If the Treasury receive an application under this regulation, the Treasury must do what is needed to comply with the choice made by P in exercising the option— (a) within 12 months of the date on which it receives P’s application, or (b) by the date on which P attains normal pension age, whichever is earlier.
S2696 Options: supplementary 80.—(1)
S2697 In any case where— (a) P has exercised the option, and (b) the Treasury has done what is needed to comply with the choice made by P in exercising the option, the Treasury is discharged from any obligation to provide benefits to which the cash equivalent related.
S2698 Cancellation of exercise of option 81.—(1)
S2699 P may cancel the exercise of the option by giving the Treasury notice in writing that P no longer wishes it to be exercised.
S2700 (2) A notice given under paragraph (1) does not have effect if it is given to the Treasury at a time when, in order to comply with the choice made by P in exercising the option, the Treasury have entered into an agreement with a third party to use the whole or part of P’s cash equivalent in a way specified in regulation 74(2) above.
S2701 (3) The cancellation of the exercise of an option by P under paragraph (1) does not affect P’s right to make another application under regulation 79.
S2702 CHAPTER 3 TRANSFERS IN Application of Chapter and interpretation 82.—(1)
S2703 This Chapter applies in relation to a member (“P”) if— (a) P has accrued rights under a registered pension scheme, and (b) P’s age on 6th February 2013 was more than one year under normal pension age.
S2704 (2) In this Chapter— “the relevant date” means the date on which a transfer payment is received by the administrators;
S2705 “transfer payment” has the meaning given by regulation 83.
S2706 Application to accept payment into scheme 83.—(1)
S2707 P may ask the administrators to accept a payment into the principal scheme (referred to in this Chapter as a transfer payment) representing the cash equivalent of P’s accrued rights in any registered pension scheme.
S2708 (2) The administrators may refuse to accept the payment or any part of it.
S2709 (3) A request under paragraph (1) must be made— (a) in writing;
S2710 (b) before P has reached normal pension age; and (c) not less than one year before P becomes entitled to a pension under these Regulations on retirement from P’s reckonable service.
S2711 Cancellation of request 84.—(1) P may, by notice in writing given to the administrators, cancel a request made under regulation 82 at any time before it has been accepted.
S2712 (2) If P cancels a request, P may make another.
S2713 Manner of accepting payment 85.
S2714 Payments into the principal scheme must be made directly from the trustees or administrators of the registered pension scheme and in no other manner.
S2715 Benefits to be provided 86.—(1)
S2716 The benefits to be provided to P in respect of a transfer payment into the principal scheme are— (a) an annual pension payable to P from the same date and at the same intervals as P’s pension under the principal scheme; and (b) derivative benefits payable at the same intervals and to the same persons as the benefits under the principal scheme are payable.
S2717 (2) In this Part, “derivative benefit” means— (a) a lump sum under regulation 25, (b) a surviving adult’s pension under regulation 36, or (c) a children’s pension under regulation 42.
S2718 Calculation of benefits in respect of transfer payments 87.—(1)
S2719 This regulation applies for the purposes of calculating the annual pension and derivative benefits to be provided to P to reflect a transfer payment into the principal scheme.
S2720 (2) In this regulation— (a) the guaranteed minimum pension used in the calculations is that provided by the Department for Work and Pensions, or, in relation to Northern Ireland, the Department for Communities which corresponds to the application of revaluation orders under section 148 of the Social Security Administration Act 1992 or section 130 of the Social Security Administration (Northern Ireland) Act 1992;
S2721 (b) the transfer payment is to include the amount of any limited revaluation premium (as defined in section 55 of the Pension Schemes Act 1993 and section 51 of the Pension Schemes (Northern Ireland) Act 1993) paid by a previous scheme (as those provisions had effect before they were repealed).
S2722 (3) The annual pension in respect of a transfer payment (“the pension scheme”) shall be calculated in accordance with the formula—  TP  (a)    2  g 78  g 88   GMP 

S2736 (ii) WS in Table 5 where P is neither married nor a civil partner at the relevant date;
